Kato’s Ramification filtration via de Rham-Witt complex and applications
Amalendu Krishna, Subhadip Majumder

Abstract




2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 14G17, Secondary 19F15

Abstract.

Given an
F
-finite regular scheme
X
of positive characteristic and a
simple normal crossing divisor
E
on
X
,
we introduce a filtration on the de Rham-Witt
complex
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X\setminus E}
. When
X
is the spectrum of a
henselian discrete valuation ring
A
with quotient field
K
,
this extends the classical filtration on

W_{m}(K)
due to Brylinski. We show that Kato’s ramification filtration on

H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X\setminus E,{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}(q-1))
for
q\geq 1
admits an explicit
description in terms of the above filtration of the de Rham-Witt complex
of
X\setminus E
. When
q=1
, this specializes to the results of Kato and
Kerz-Saito.

As applications, we prove refinements of the
duality theorem of Jannsen-Saito-Zhao for smooth projective schemes over finite
fields and the duality theorem of Zhao for semi-stable schemes over
henselian discrete valuation rings of positive characteristic with finite
residue fields. We also prove a modulus version of the duality theorem of
Ekedahl. As another
application, we prove Lefschetz theorems for Kato’s ramification filtrations
for smooth projective varieties over
F
-finite fields. This extends a
result of Kerz-Saito for
H^{1}
to higher cohomology. Similar results are
proven for the Brauer group.

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Background

To study the wild ramification in the
p
-adic étale motivic cohomology classes
of a henselian
discrete valuation field
K
with imperfect residue field, Kato
[
Kato-89
]

introduced a ramification filtration

\{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))\}_{n\geq 0}

on the cohomology groups
H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))
for
q\geq 1
.
This generalizes the classical upper numbering filtration of the
character groups of the Galois groups of complete discrete valuation fields with
perfect residue fields (cf.
[
Serre-LF
, Chap. IV, § 3, Chap. XV, § 2]
).

Kato showed that when
K
has characteristic

p>0
, his ramification filtration on
H^{1}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})

has a simple description as the direct limit (over
m
) of the images of
a filtration on
W_{m}(K)
defined earlier by Brylinski
[
Brylinski
, § 1]

under the canonical maps
\partial_{K,m}\colon W_{m}(K)\to H^{1}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})
induced by the Artin-Schreier-Witt
exact sequence

(1.1)

0\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}\to W_{m}(K)\xrightarrow{1-\overline{F}}W_{m}(K)\xrightarrow{\partial_{K,m}}H^{1}_{et}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}).

The description of Kato’s ramification filtration on
H^{1}_{et}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})

in terms of the Brylinski’s filtration on
W_{m}(K)
has many consequences
(e.g., see
[
Daichi
]
,
[
Kato-89
]
,
[
Kerz-Saito-Duke
]
,
[
Leal
]
,

[
GK-Adv
]
,
[
GK-Nis
]
).

If
X
is a Noetherian normal and integral scheme of characteristic
p>0

and
D\subset X
is an effective Weil divisor with complement
U
, Kato
(cf.
[
Kato-89
, § 7,8]
) defined a ramification filtration on

H^{q}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))
(see §
7
for the
definition of this group) for all
q\geq 1
by

{\rm fil}_{D}^{\operatorname{log}}H^{q}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))=\{\chi\in H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))|\chi_{i}\in{\rm fil}_{n_{i}}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K_{i},{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))\ \forall i\},

where
\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{r}\}
is the set of generic points of
D
,

n_{i}
is the multiplicity of
D
at
x_{i}
,
K_{i}
is the henselization of
the function field of
X
at
x_{i}
and
\chi_{i}
is the image of
\chi

under the canonical pull-back map
H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))\to H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K_{i},{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))
. The group

{\rm fil}_{D}^{\operatorname{log}}H^{q}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q-1))
is defined analogously
(cf. §
8
).

Using Kato’s description of his ramification filtration on

H^{1}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})
in terms of Brylinski’s filtration on
W_{m}(K)
for
henselian field
K
,
Kerz-Saito
[
Kerz-Saito-ANT
]
showed that if
X
is a smooth projective variety
over a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
and
D_{\rm red}\subset X
is a normal
crossing divisor with complement
U
, then
{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})

can be described as an étale cohomology group (see §
8.1
for
details). As a major application of this cohomological description, they

[
Kerz-Saito-Duke
]
solved the class
field theory problem for the abelianized étale fundamental group with modulus
over finite fields (see also
[
BKS
]
).

The principal objective of this paper is to
extend Brylinski’s filtration on
W_{m}(K)
to a filtration on the full de Rham-Witt
complex
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}
and prove that Kato’s ramification filtration
on
H^{q}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))
can be described in terms of this new
filtration on
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}
. This
extends
[
Kato-89
, Thm. 3.2]
to all integers
q\geq 1
.
Using this extension and its generalization to schemes, we then show that
Kato’s global filtration
{\rm fil}_{D}^{\operatorname{log}}H^{q}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))
recalled above
can be described as an étale cohomology group,
extending the result of Kerz-Saito for
H^{1}
to
H^{q}
for all
q\geq 1
.
We derive several applications of these generalizations some of which are included in
this paper while the remaining ones are given in
[
KM-1
]
and
[
KM-2
]
.

Standing assumption:

We fix once and for all a prime
p>0
and assume all fields to be
of characteristic
p
and all schemes to be
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-schemes. If
a different assumption is made in a specific context, we shall spell that out.

1.2.
Extensions of the filtrations of Brylinski and Kato

Let
X
be a equidimensional
Noetherian regular
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme and let
E\subset X
be a simple
normal crossing divisor with irreducible components
E_{1},\ldots,E_{r}
.
We shall refer to the pair
(X,E)
as an snc-pair.
Let
j\colon U=X\setminus E\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the complement of
E
.
Let
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
denote the subgroup of the Weil divisor group
{\rm Div}(X)
consisting
of divisors supported on
E
. Recall that
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
is a partially
ordered group where
D\leq D^{\prime}
if
D^{\prime}-D
is effective.
We refer to Definitions
3.8
and

3.9
for the definition of good filtered
Witt-complexes.

Our first result is the following extension of Brylinski’s filtration on
the rings of Witt vectors of discrete valuation fields.

Theorem 1.1
.

There is a
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
-filtered Witt-complex

\{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}
together with an inclusion of
filtered Witt-complexes
\{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}\hookrightarrow\{j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}
over
X
such that the following hold.

(1)

\varinjlim_{D}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\xrightarrow{\cong}j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
.

(2)

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
coincides with Brylinski’s filtration on
W_{m}(K)

when
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
,
where
A
is a discrete valuation ring with quotient field
K
,
residue field
{\mathfrak{f}}
and
E={\rm Spec\,}({\mathfrak{f}})
.

(3)

\{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}
is good.

(4)

\{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}
is contravariantly functorial in the pair

(X,E)
.

Remark 1.2
.

We make some remarks about Theorem
1.1
.

(1)

In
[
JSZ
]
, Jannsen-Saito-Zhao defined a filtration

{\rm fil}^{\rm JSZ}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
of
j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.
Their filtration is related to the one in Theorem
1.1

via the isomorphism

{\rm fil}^{\rm JSZ}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\cong{\rm fil}_{p^{m-1}D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}

(cf. Lemma
3.6
(1)). The filtration of
j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
defined
by Jannsen-Saito-Zhao is not fine enough to prove their main duality theorem
for a fixed modulus. Another issue with
{\rm fil}^{\rm JSZ}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}

is that it does not yield the logarithmic Hodge-Witt sheaf

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X|{D},{\operatorname{log}}}
of op. cit. when
D
is effective and
one intersects the classical logarithmic Hodge-Witt sheaf
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}
with

{\rm fil}^{\rm JSZ}_{-D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
. The filtration
\{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}

rectifies these defects (cf. Theorem
1.8
, Lemma
).

(2)

In
[
Ren-Rulling
]
, Ren-Rülling introduced subsheaves

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(X,\pm D)}

of
j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
when
X
is a smooth variety over a perfect field and

D
is effective.
Their subsheaf
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(X,D)}
(resp.
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(X,-D)}
)
coincides with the subsheaf
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}

(resp.
{\rm fil}_{-D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
) given by Theorem
1.3

when
D=0
(resp.
D=E
), see Lemma
3.6
and Corollary
.
In other cases, (for
D
effective)
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(X,D)}
and
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}

(resp.
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(X,-D)}
and
{\rm fil}_{-D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
) are
different from each other, even when
m=1
and
q\geq 1

(or
q=0
and
m\geq 2
), as is evident
from Lemma
3.6
and
[
Ren-Rulling
, (6.2.4), Lem. 8.4]

(see also Remark
3.7
).

We now proceed to give applications of Theorem
1.1
.

1.3.
Ramification subgroups as sheaf cohomology

Let
X
be as in Theorem
1.1
.
For the first application, we assume that
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
is effective.
For
q\geq 0
, we let
W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}
denote the two term complex

[Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}]
on the
étale site of
X
, where

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}={\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\bigcap j_{*}Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
and

C
is the Cartier operator.
We refer to §
6.3
for the proof that

W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}
is well-defined. We now have
the following extension of the theorems of Kato (cf. (
1.1
)) and
Kerz-Saito
[
Kerz-Saito-ANT
]
.

Theorem 1.3
.

For
m,q\geq 1
, there is a canonical isomorphism

{\rm fil}_{D}^{\operatorname{log}}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q-1))\xrightarrow{\cong}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q-1,\bullet}_{X|D}).

One immediate corollary of Theorem
1.3
is the following functoriality
property of Kato’s ramification filtration (cf. Corollary
8.8
) which
can not be deduced from its definition.
We refer the reader to §
2.1

for the definition of morphisms between snc-pairs.

Corollary 1.4
.

For
m,q\geq 1
, the ramification filtration

{\rm fil}_{D}^{\operatorname{log}}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q-1))

is functorial with respect to morphisms of snc-pairs.

1.4.
Global refined Swan conductor

Let
X
be as in Theorem
1.1
and let
D\geq D^{\prime}\geq D/p\geq 0
be two divisors
in
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
(see §
3
for the definition of
D/p
).
If
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
, where
A
is an
F
-finite henselian discrete
valuation ring with maximal ideal
(\pi)
, quotient field
K
and residue field
{\mathfrak{f}}
,
then one of the main results of
Kato in
[
Kato-89
]
is the construction of an injective refined Swan
conductor map

(1.2)

{{\rm rsw}}^{q}_{K,n}\colon\frac{{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{n}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))}{{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{n-1}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))}\hookrightarrow\pi^{-n}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\otimes_{A}{\mathfrak{f}}

for
n,q\geq 1
.
For
q=1
, Kato-Leal-Saito
[
KLS
, § 4]
showed that Kato’s Swan conductor at the
generic points of
E
glue together to give rise to an injective
refined Swan conductor map

{{\rm Rsw}}\colon\frac{{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{D}H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})}{{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{D/p}H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})}\to H^{0}_{\rm zar}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)}{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D/p)})
.

As the first application of Theorem
1.3
, we prove the following
generalization of these results.

Theorem 1.5
.

Let
X
be as in Theorem
1.1
and let
D\geq D^{\prime}\geq D/p\geq 0
be two divisors
in
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
. Let
q\geq 1
be an integer and let
F=D-D^{\prime}
.
Then there exists a monomorphism of ind-abelian groups

(1.3)

{\rm Rsw}^{\bullet,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}\colon\left\{\frac{{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q-1))}{{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D^{\prime}}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q-1))}\right\}_{m\geq 1}\longrightarrow H^{0}_{\rm zar}\left(F,\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(D)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F}\right).

Moreover,
{\rm Rsw}^{\bullet,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}
is functorial with respect to morphisms of
snc-pairs.

Theorem
1.5
has several applications. For instance, Kato’s integrality theorem
(cf.
[
Kato-89
, Thm. 7.1]
) follows immediately from it.
Using Theorem
1.5
, we can define Matsuda’s non-log version of Kato’s
filtration (cf.
[
Matsuda
]
) for
H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}(q-1))
for all
q\geq 1
, and
show that Matsuda’s filtration is also functorial with respect to morphisms of
snc-pairs (cf.
[
KM-2
]
).
Theorem
1.5
also plays the central role in the proofs of the main
results of
[
KM-2
]
.

1.5.
Extension of Ekedahl’s duality

Let
k
be a perfect field and let
X
be a regular and proper
k
-scheme
which is connected of dimension
d
.
Let
i,q\geq 0,m\geq 1
be integers. Then Ekedahl’s
duality theorem
[
Ekedahl
, Thm. II.2.2]
for the Hodge-Witt cohomology states that
there is a canonical isomorphism of finitely generated
W_{m}(k)
-modules

H^{i}_{\rm zar}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm Hom}_{W_{m}(k)}(H^{d-i}_{\rm zar}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{d-q}_{X}),W_{m}(k)).

We now let
X
be a connected, regular and proper
k
-scheme of dimension
d

and let
E\subset X
be a simple normal crossing divisor with complement
U
.
In this paper, we extend Ekedahl’s duality theorem to the filtered
Hodge-Witt cohomology as follows.

Theorem 1.6
.

Let
i,q\geq 0,m\geq 1
be integers and
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
.
Then the multiplication operation of

W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
induces a canonical isomorphism of finitely generated

W_{m}(k)
-modules

H^{i}_{\rm zar}(X,{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm Hom}_{W_{m}(k)}(H^{d-i}_{\rm zar}(X,{\rm fil}_{-D-E}W_{m}\Omega^{d-q}_{U}),W_{m}(k)).

Remark 1.7
.

In
[
Ren-Rulling
, Cor. 9.4]
, Ren-Rülling proved a
duality theorem similar to Theorem
1.6
.
However, the cohomology groups used in their result are different from the ones used
in Theorem
1.6
except when
D=0
(cf. Corollary
).
In the latter case, Theorem
1.6
also recovers
the duality theorems of
Nakkajima
[
Nakkajima
, Thm. 5.3(1)]
and Hyodo
[
Hyodo
, (3.3.1)]
.
One notable difference between
[
Ren-Rulling
, Cor. 9.4]
and
Theorem
1.6
is that while the former result requires

D
to be effective, no such condition is imposed in the latter.

1.6.
Refinement of Jannsen-Saito-Zhao duality

Assume now that
k
is a finite field and
X
is a projective scheme of dimension

d
over
k
which is regular and connected.
The famous duality theorem of Milne (cf.
[
Milne-Zeta
]
) for the
p
-adic étale
motivic cohomology says that there is a perfect pairing of finite groups

(1.4)

H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\times H^{d+1-i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{d-q}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},

where
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}
are the logarithmic Hodge-Witt sheaves
à la Bloch, Deligne and Illusie.
Ever since the discovery of motives and motivic cohomology with modulus, it has been
an open question if there is an extension of Milne’s duality if one imposes a modulus
condition. There have been attempts by several authors to resolve this (cf.

[
JSZ
]
,
[
Zhao
]
,
[
GK-Duality
]
) but the complete
answer remained elusive until recently.
As an application of Theorems
1.1
and
1.3
, we
obtain a solution of this problem as follows.
In
[
Ren-Rulling
, Thm.11.15]
, Ren-Rülling have independently obtained another
duality theorem for a fixed modulus over finite fields. But the two duality
theorems are different except when
D=0
.

Let
E\subset X
be a simple normal crossing divisor as in Theorem
1.1
and
assume that
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
is effective. For
q,m\geq 1
, we let

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}}
be the image of the map of étale sheaves

{\rm dlog}\colon{\rm Ker}({\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{1,X}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{1,D})\otimes j_{*}({\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q-1,U})\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}
(cf.
[
JSZ
, Defn. 1.1.1, Prop. 1.1.3]
), where

{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{\star,(-)}
is the improved Milnor
K
-theory sheaf à la Gabber-Kerz
(cf.
[
Kerz-JAG
]
).
We let
W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}}={\rm Ker}({{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{0,X}}/{p^{m}}\to{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{0,D}}/{p^{m}})
.
We now have the following.

Theorem 1.8
.

For
q,i\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, there is a perfect pairing of finite groups

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D})\times H^{d+1-i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{d-q}_{X|D+E,{\operatorname{log}}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.

We show in this paper that this pairing is compatible with respect to
the partial order of
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
. In particular, we can take the limit of
the pairing in Theorem
1.8
over
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
which allows us to deduce the
following duality theorem of Jannsen-Saito-Zhao
[
JSZ
]

(which coincides with the duality theorem of Gupta-Krishna
[
GK-Duality
]
) as
a corollary.

Corollary 1.9
.

(
Jannsen-Saito-Zhao
)

For
q,i\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, there is a perfect pairing of topological
abelian groups

H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}})\times{\varprojlim}_{D}H^{d+1-i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{d-q}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},

where the first group on the left has discrete topology and the second
has the profinite topology.

Remark 1.10
.

In
[
GK-Duality
]
, an analogue of Corollary
1.9
was proven without
assuming that
E
is a simple normal crossing divisor. In light of
Theorem
1.8
, an interesting open question
is whether the methods of this paper allows one to prove a refinement of the
duality theorem of op. cit. for a fixed modulus.

1.7.
Duality for log schemes over finite fields

Let
(X,E)
be as in §
1.6
. Assume that
X

endowed with the log structure given by the normal crossing
divisor
E
and we let
X_{\operatorname{log}}
denote the resulting log scheme.
We let
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{{\operatorname{log}}},{\operatorname{log}}}
denote the logarithmic de Rham-Witt complex of
the log scheme
X_{\operatorname{log}}
(cf.
[
Lorenzon
, § 2]
).
This is the Frobenius fixed point of the de Rham-Witt complex

W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{{\operatorname{log}}}}
of
X_{\operatorname{log}}

(cf.
[
Hyodo-Kato
, § 4]
,
[
Geisser-Hesselholt-JAMS
, § 1.2]
).
As another consequence of Theorem
1.8
, we get the following extension of
Milne’s duality to log schemes.

Corollary 1.11
.

For
q,i\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, there is a
canonical isomorphism
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X_{{\operatorname{log}}},{\operatorname{log}}}\xrightarrow{\cong}j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}}
. In particular,
there is a perfect pairing of finite groups

H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X_{{\operatorname{log}}},{\operatorname{log}}})\times H^{d+1-i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{d-q}_{X|E,{{\operatorname{log}}}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.

1.8.
Refinement of Zhao’s duality

We let
R
be an equicharacteristic henselian discrete valuation ring with finite
residue field. Let
f\colon X\to{\rm Spec\,}(R)
be a strictly semi-stable connected
scheme of relative dimension
d
(cf.
[
Zhao
, Defn. 1.4.2]
).
Let
X_{s}
denote the closed fiber of
f
.
We let
E\subset X
be a simple normal crossing divisor with
irreducible components
E_{1},\ldots,E_{r}
. For an
r
-tuple of
non-negative integers
\underline{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})
, we let

D_{\underline{n}}=\sum_{i}n_{i}E_{i}
and
D_{\underline{n}+1}=\sum_{i}(n_{i}+1)E_{i}
.
For an étale sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
on
X
, let
H^{*}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}},X_{s}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})
denote the
étale cohomology of
{\mathcal{F}}
with support in
X_{s}
.
An yet another application of Theorems
1.1
and
1.3
is the
following refinement of Zhao’s duality theorem
[
Zhao
]
.

Theorem 1.12
.

For
q,i\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, there is a canonical isomorphism of
(not necessarily finite)
{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}
-modules

(1.5)

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm Hom}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}}(H^{d+2-i}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}},X_{s}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{d+1-q}_{X|D_{\underline{n}+1},{\operatorname{log}}}),{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}).

1.9.
Lefschetz theorem for ramification filtration and Brauer group

Let us now assume that
X\subset{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{k}
is a smooth projective connected scheme
of dimension
d
over an
F
-finite field
k
and

E\subset X
is a simple normal crossing divisor with complement
U
.
We let
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
be effective and let
m,q\geq 1
. As an application of
Theorem
1.3
, we prove the following
Lefschetz hyperplane theorem for the logarithmic Hodge-Witt cohomology with
modulus and the ramification subgroups
{\rm fil}_{D}^{\operatorname{log}}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q-1))
.
When
q=1
, part (1) of this theorem was shown earlier by
Kerz-Saito (cf.
[
Kerz-Saito-ANT
, Thm. 3.8]
).
We refer to Definition
for the definition of sufficiently ample
hypersurface sections.

Theorem 1.13
.

Let
X^{\prime}
be a smooth hypersurface section of
X
in
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{k}

which intersects
E
transversely. Let
D^{\prime}=D\times_{X}X^{\prime}
and

U^{\prime}=X^{\prime}\setminus D^{\prime}
. Assume also that
X^{\prime}
is sufficiently ample
(relative to
D
in
(1)
and
(2)
). Then the pull-back maps

(1)

{\rm fil}_{D}^{\operatorname{log}}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q-1))\to{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D^{\prime}}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U^{\prime},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q-1))

(2)

H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})\to H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X^{\prime},W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{X^{\prime}|D^{\prime},{\operatorname{log}}})

(3)

H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\to H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X^{\prime},W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{X^{\prime},{\operatorname{log}}})

are isomorphisms if
i+q\leq d-1
and injective if
i+q=d
.

The Lefschetz theorem for the étale fundamental group of
smooth projective schemes over a field is a fundamental result of
Grothendieck (cf.
[
SGA2
, Exp. XII, Cor. 3.5]
).
To our knowledge, similar results for the Brauer group are not known.
As a consequence of Theorem
1.13
, we prove the following.

Theorem 1.14
.

Assume in the set-up of Theorem
1.13
that
d\geq 4
.
Then, for sufficiently ample
X^{\prime}
, the pull-back map
{\operatorname{Br}}(X)\{p\}\to{\operatorname{Br}}(X^{\prime})\{p\}

is an isomorphism.
If
k
is either finite, local or separably closed, then the pull-back map

{\operatorname{Br}}(X)\to{\operatorname{Br}}(X^{\prime})
is an isomorphism.

We show that the second part of Theorem
1.14
is valid even when

k
is a local or an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.
As a corollary of Theorem
1.14
,
we get the following result whose prime-to-
{\rm char}(k)
part
(in particular, characteristic 0 analogue) was earlier shown in
[
CTS
, Cor. 5.5.4]
.
This can be considered as a version of Grothendieck-Lefschetz theorem for the
Brauer group.

Corollary 1.15
.

Let
k
be an arbitrary field and let
X
be a smooth complete
intersection in
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{k}
of dimension at least three.
Then the pull-back map
{\operatorname{Br}}(k)\to{\operatorname{Br}}(X)
is an isomorphism.

1.10.
Other applications

There are further applications of Theorems
1.1
and
1.3

besides those mentioned above. In
[
KM-1
]
, we prove a duality theorem for
the étale cohomology of the logarithmic Hodge-Witt sheaves with modulus
on smooth projective curves over local fields. This is first result of its kind and
was not known before. In op. cit., we apply this duality theorem to establish a
class field theory for open curves over local fields (see also
[
KM-0
]
).
Several other applications of Theorems
1.1
and
1.3
will be
proven in
[
KM-2
]
. The goal of the latter work is to extend the results
of Bright-Newton
[
Bright-Newton
]
, Saito-Sato
[
Saito-Sato-ENS
]
and
Kai
[
Kai
]
to positive characteristic.

1.11.
Layout of the paper

The proofs of Theorems
1.1
and
1.3
constitute the heart of
this paper. They span over the first eight sections. In §
2
, we
recall the de Rham-Witt complex with log poles and identify it with the
de Rham-Witt complex of a log scheme arising from a normal crossing divisor on
a regular
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme. We use this identification in the study of our main
object of interest: the filtered de Rham-Witt complex.

In §
3
, we introduce the filtered de Rham-Witt complex and
prove its basic properties except its goodness.
The proof of the latter is the most challenging part of the proofs of
Theorems
1.1
and
1.3
. We divide the proof of goodness
into several steps.
In §
4
, we do this for a power series algebra in one variable. Here,
our main tool is
a presentation of the usual de Rham-Witt complex of such algebras by
Geisser-Hesselholt
[
Geisser-Hesselholt-Top
]
. In §
5
, we
extend this to all regular local
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebras. We achieve this by
reducing to the case of complete algebras and then to
power series algebras in several variables which we prove
separately using the ideas of §
4
.

In §
6
, we introduce Cartier operator on our filtered de Rham-Witt
complex. This plays a key role in our investigation. We prove several properties
of the Cartier and Frobenius operators on filtered de Rham-Witt
complex which are classically known for the ordinary de Rham-Witt complex.
We then extend the proof of the goodness of filtered de Rham-Witt
complex to all
F
-finite regular
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-schemes. This completes the proof
of Theorem
1.1
.

In §
7
, we prove Theorem
1.3
for henselian
discrete valuation fields which are
F
-finite. We use this special case to
finish the proof of this theorem in §
8
.
In §
9
, we apply Theorem
1.3
to construct a very
general version of Kato’s refined Swan conductor for all regular and
F
-finite

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-schemes. This generalized refined Swan conductor map has many applications.
These applications will be given in
[
KM-2
]
. Kato’s integrality theorem

[
Kato-89
]
is an immediate consequence of the generalized refined Swan
conductor. In §
10
, we combine Theorem
6.19
with
Ekedahl’s duality to prove Theorem
1.6
.
In §
, we apply Theorem
1.3
to prove
Theorems
1.8
and
1.12
and its corollaries.
In §
, we apply Theorem
1.3
to prove Lefschetz
theorems for Kato’s ramification subgroups and Brauer groups.

1.12.
Common notations

For a commutative ring
A
, an
A
-scheme will mean a scheme over
{\rm Spec\,}(A)
.
We fix a prime
p
for all of this paper and shall assume all schemes to be Noetherian
and separated
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-schemes. If we need to talk about a scheme of other
type (e.g., in §
), we shall mention separately.
If
A
is a commutative ring,
we let
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{A}
denote the category of separated Noetherian
A
-schemes. The product

X\times_{{\rm Spec\,}(A)}Y
will be written as
X\times_{A}Y
.
For a scheme
X
, we let
X^{(q)}
(resp.
X_{(q)}
) denote the set of points on
X

having codimension (resp. dimension)
q
. We let
X_{\rm reg}
(resp.
X_{\rm sing}
)
denote the regular (resp. singular) locus of
X
.
We let
{\mathbf{Sm}}_{X}
denote the category of smooth schemes over
X
.

Given a commutative ring
A
, we let
Q(A)
denote the total ring of fractions of

A
. We let
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{A/{\rm zar}}
(resp.
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{A/{\rm nis}}
, resp.
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{A/{\acute{e}t}})

denote the Zariski (resp. Nisnevich, resp. étale) site of
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{A}
.
We let
\epsilon\colon{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{A/{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}\to{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{A/{\rm nis}}
denote the canonical morphism of sites.
If
{\mathcal{F}}
is a sheaf on
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{A/{\rm nis}}
,
we shall write
\epsilon^{*}{\mathcal{F}}
also as
{\mathcal{F}}
as long as the usage of
the étale topology is clear in a context.
For an
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme
X
,
\psi\colon X\to X
will denote the
absolute Frobenius morphism.

For an abelian group
A
, we shall write
{\operatorname{Tor}}^{1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(A,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/n)
as

A[n]
and
A/{nA}
as
A/n
.
The tensor product
A\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}B
will be written as
A\otimes B
.
We shall let
A\{p\}
(resp.
A\{p^{\prime}\}
) denote the
subgroup of elements of
A
which are annihilated by a power of (resp.
an integer prime to)
p
. If
{\mathcal{F}}
is a sheaf on some site of a scheme, we shall write
the kernel (resp. cokernel) of the map
{\mathcal{F}}\xrightarrow{n}{\mathcal{F}}
as
{}_{n}{\mathcal{F}}

(resp.
{{\mathcal{F}}}/n
).
Given a surjective ring homomorphism
A\twoheadrightarrow A/I
, we shall denote the image of
an element
a\in A
in
A/I
typically by
\overline{a}
if no confusion arises.
We shall let
{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
denote the set of non-negative integers.

2.
de Rham-Witt complex with log poles

In this section, we shall recall the de Rham-Witt complex with log poles on which
our construction of the filtered de Rham-Witt complex will be based.
We shall identify this complex with the de Rham-Witt
complex of a log scheme (à la Hyodo-Kato) obtained from a normal crossing divisor
on a regular scheme. We shall use this identification to derive some key
properties of the de Rham-Witt complex with log poles which will subsequently be used
in our study of the filtered de Rham-Witt complex.
We refer the reader to
[
Kato-log
]
and
[
Ogus
]
for the
basic results that we shall use about log schemes in this paper.

2.1.
Definition and Witt complex structure

Let
X
be a connected regular Noetherian
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme
and let
E\subset X
be a simple normal crossing divisor (sncd) with irreducible
components
E_{1},\ldots,E_{r}
. Let
j\colon U\to X
be the inclusion of the
complement of
E
and let
K
denote the function field of
X
.
We shall refer
(X,E)
as an
snc-pair
in the sequel.
We let
{\rm Div}(X)
denote the group of Weil divisors on
X
and let
{\rm Div}_{Z}(X)

denote the subgroup of
{\rm Div}(X)
consisting of Weil divisors which are supported on
a closed subset
Z\subset X
.
The divisor
E
endows
X
with a unique log structure
(X,{\mathcal{M}}_{X},\alpha_{X})
, where

\alpha_{X}\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}\cap j_{*}{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}_{U}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is the usual
inclusion (cf.
[
Kato-log
]
).
We shall denote by the resulting log scheme by
X_{\operatorname{log}}
.
A morphism
f\colon(X^{\prime},E^{\prime})\to(X,E)
of snc-pairs is, by definition, a morphism of
log schemes
f\colon X^{\prime}_{\operatorname{log}}\to X_{\operatorname{log}}
. Under such a morphism, one checks that

f^{*}(E_{i})\in{\rm Div}_{E^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
for every
i
and
f(U^{\prime})\subset U
.

We let
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}
denote the de Rham-Witt complex. We shall denote the
pro-de Rham-Witt complex
\{W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}\}_{m\geq 1}

by
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}
. A similar notation will be used for any variant of the
pro-de Rham-Witt complex. We let
[\cdot]_{m}\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
be the
Teichmüller map and recall that this is a morphism of monoids under the product
operations on its two sides.
We also recall that the pull-back map
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\to j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
is injective
for
q\geq 0

(cf.
[
KP-Comp
, Prop. 2.8]
), a fact which will be implicit throughout our
discussion.

To define the de Rham-Witt complex with log poles, we suppose first that

X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
, where
A
is a regular
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra and

E_{i}={\rm Spec\,}({A}/{(x_{i})})
, we let
\pi=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\prod}}}x_{i}

and let
A_{\pi}=A[\pi^{-1}]
.
We let
S_{0}=\{0\}
and for
1\leq j\leq r
, let
S_{j}
be the collection of ordered
subsets
J=\{i_{1}<\cdots<i_{j}\}
of
\{1,\ldots,r\}
.
For
J=\{i_{1}<\cdots<i_{j}\}\in S_{j}
, we let
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)_{J}=W_{m}\Omega^{q-j}_{A}{\rm dlog}[x_{i_{1}}]_{m}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}[x_{i_{j}}]_{m}

as a
W_{m}(A)
-submodule of
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}
. For
J=S_{0}
, we let

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)_{J}=W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}
. We let
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
be the
image
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
of the canonical map

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{min\{q,r\}}}}{{\underset{j=0}{\bigoplus}}}{\underset{J\in S_{j}}{\bigoplus}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)_{J}

\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}
.
It is easy to check that the map
d\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{A_{\pi}}

takes
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
to
W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
which yields a
subcomplex
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\subset W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\pi}}
.

Let now
(X,E)
be an arbitrary snc-pair.
Since the assignment
X^{\prime}\mapsto W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})
is functorial
on the category of affine snc-pairs
(X^{\prime},E^{\prime})
, it defines a unique subcomplex
whose term at the
q
-th level is a Zariski subsheaf
j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
, denoted by

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
. The stalk of this subsheaf at a point
x\in X
is

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X_{x}}({\operatorname{log}}E_{x})
, where
X_{x}={\rm Spec\,}({\mathcal{O}}_{X,x})
and

E_{x}=X_{x}\times_{X}E
. The complex
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
is the desired
de Rham-Witt complex of
X
with log poles along
E
.
There are functorial inclusions
W_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}\hookrightarrow W_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\hookrightarrow j_{*}W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
of pro-Witt complexes on
X_{\rm zar}
.
Furthermore,
W_{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
coincides with the classical de Rham
complex with log poles
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
.

We let
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\operatorname{log}}}
denote the pro-de Rham-Witt complex of the
log scheme
X_{\operatorname{log}}
, associated to the snc-pair
(X,E)
(cf.
[
Hyodo-Kato
, § 4]
,

[
Geisser-Hesselholt-JAMS
, § 1.2]
,
[
Matsue
, § 3.4]
).
Note that
W_{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\operatorname{log}}}
coincides with the de Rham complex

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\operatorname{log}}}
of the log scheme
X_{\operatorname{log}}
.
For
m\geq 1
, the map
\alpha_{m}=[\cdot]_{m}\circ\alpha_{X}\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{X}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}

makes the latter into a sheaf of log rings on
X
. This log structure
on
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
allows one to define the notion of Witt complexes and the
universal Witt complex on
X_{\operatorname{log}}
.
We refer the reader to
[
Geisser-Hesselholt-JAMS
, § 1.2]
(see also

[
HM-Annals
, § 3]
) for the definitions of Witt complexes and the
universal Witt complex on
X_{\operatorname{log}}
. We only note that the pro-de Rham-Witt
complex
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\operatorname{log}}}
is the universal Witt complex on
X_{\operatorname{log}}
.
In this paper, a log Witt-complex on a scheme with a log structure (e.g.,
X
)
will be another name of a Witt-complex on the resulting log scheme
(e.g.,
X_{\operatorname{log}}
).

Lemma 2.1
.

W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
is a Witt complex on
X_{\operatorname{log}}
. In particular,
there is a canonical (strict) map of Witt complexes

\theta_{X}\colon W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\operatorname{log}}}\to W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
.

Proof.

To endow
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
with the structure of a Witt complex
on
X_{\operatorname{log}}
, we let
{\rm dlog}\circ[\cdot]_{m}\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{X}\to W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
be
the map locally defined by
a\mapsto{\rm dlog}[a]_{m}
.
We take
\lambda\colon(W_{\star}{\mathcal{O}}_{X},{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\to(W_{\star}\Omega^{0}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E),{\mathcal{M}}_{X})
to
be the identity map. Since
j_{*}W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
is a Witt complex over

X
and
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
is its subcomplex, the lemma will follow
if we verify
that the latter is preserved by the Frobenius and Verchiebung operators of

j_{*}W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
(cf.
[
Geisser-Hesselholt-JAMS
, § 1]
).
Since
F
is multiplicative and
F({\rm dlog}[a]_{m})={\rm dlog}[a]_{m-1}
,
the claim is clear for
F
.
Since
V(w{\rm dlog}[a]_{m})=V(wF({\rm dlog}[a]_{m+1}))=V(w){\rm dlog}[a]_{m+1}
, we
see that
V
also preserves
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
.
∎

2.2.
Cartier isomorphism for
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{{\operatorname{log}}}}

Before we proceed further, we need to recall the notion of
F
-finiteness.
An
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme
Y
is called
F
-finite if the absolute Frobenius map

\psi\colon Y\to Y
is a finite morphism. The following result about
F
-finite
rings and schemes is well known. For the definitions of excellent and quasi-excellent
rings, we refer to
[
Matsumura
, Chap. 32. p. 260]
.

Proposition 2.2
.

The following are true for all
q\geq 0,m\geq 1
.

(1)

If
A
is an
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra, then every essentially of finite type

A
-scheme is
F
-finite. The henselization and completion of
A
along any ideal
are
F
-finite. The strict henselization of
A
at any maximal ideal is
F
-finite.

(2)

An
F
-finite Noetherian ring is excellent.

(3)

If
A
is a quasi-excellent Noetherian local
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra, then it is

F
-finite if and only if its residue field is
F
-finite.

(4)

If
A
is a complete Noetherian local
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra, then it is

F
-finite if and only if its residue field is
F
-finite.

(5)

If
A
is Noetherian and
F
-finite, then so is
W_{m}(A)
and

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}
is a finitely generated
W_{m}(A)
-module.

(6)

If
A
is Noetherian local
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra with completion

\widehat{A}
, then the canonical map
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}
is an isomorphism.

(7)

If
A
is a regular local
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra with maximal ideal

{\mathfrak{m}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
and residue field
{\mathfrak{f}}
, then
\Omega^{1}_{A}
is a free

A
-module of finite rank. If
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in A
are such that

\{d\overline{y}_{1},\ldots,d\overline{y}_{s}\}
forms a basis of
\Omega^{1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}

as an
{\mathfrak{f}}
-vector space, then
\{dy_{1},\ldots,dy_{s},dx_{1},\ldots,dx_{n}\}
is
a basis for
\Omega^{1}_{A}
as an
A
-module.

Proof.

Items (1) and (5) follow from
[
Dundas-Morrow
, Thm. 3.6, Lem. 3.8, 3.10]

using the surjection
\Omega^{q}_{W_{m}(A)}\twoheadrightarrow W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}
. Item (2) follows
from
[
Kunz-AJM
, Thm. 2.5]
and (4) follows from the Cohen structure theorem.
Item (3) follows from (4) and
[
Hashimoto
, Cor. 22]
. Item (6) follows from

[
Morrow-ENS
, Lem. 2.11]
and item (7) follows from
[
Kato-89
, Lem. 7.2]
.
∎

We now return to the set-up of
2.1
.
We assume that the snc-pair
(X,E)
is local in that

X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
, where
A
is a
regular local
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra of Krull dimension
n
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
denote the maximal ideal of
A
. We let

E_{i}={\rm Spec\,}({A}/{(x_{i})})
and let
\pi=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\prod}}}x_{i}
,
where
1\leq r\leq n
. We let
A_{\operatorname{log}}
denote the log ring structure on
A

corresponding to the log scheme
X_{\operatorname{log}}
.

Lemma 2.3
.

The canonical maps of log differential graded
A
-algebras

\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\xrightarrow{\theta_{A}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)

\xleftarrow{\phi_{A}}{\Lambda}^{\bullet}_{A}(\Omega^{1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi))

are isomorphisms. Moreover, each
\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
is a free
A
-module of
finite rank.

Proof.

This is standard using Proposition
2.2
(7) and the structure of

\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
(cf.
[
Ogus
, Thm. 1.2.4]
).
∎

To prove the next result, we let
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[m]}={\mathbb{F}}_{p}[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]
for

m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. There are then homomorphisms of log rings

(2.1)

{\mathbb{F}}_{p,{\operatorname{log}}}\to({\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]})_{\operatorname{log}}\xrightarrow{\gamma}A_{\operatorname{log}},\ \mbox{where}\ \gamma(X_{i})=x_{i}\ \forall\ 1\leq i\leq n.

The log structure of
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
is trivial while
that of
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]}
(resp.
A
) is given by the simple normal crossing
divisor
V((X))
(resp.
V((\pi))
) on
{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
(resp.
{\rm Spec\,}(A)
), where

X=X_{1}\cdots X_{r}
. The first arrow in (
2.1
) is log smooth
while the second arrow is a morphism of log rings such that the underlying morphism of
rings is regular. To see the latter claim, first note that
A
is formally

{\mathfrak{m}}
-smooth over
({\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]})_{(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})}
, as
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
is perfect and
A
is
regular (cf.
[
Tanimoto-2
, Lem. 1.3]
). Since
A
is excellent
by Proposition
2.2
(2), it follows now from André’s theorem
(cf.
[
Matsumura
, p. 260]
) that
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]}\to A
is regular.
We let
H_{{\rm dR}}^{i}(A_{\operatorname{log}})
denote the
i
-th cohomology of the log de Rham complex

\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.

Lemma 2.4
.

For
q\geq 0
, there is an isomorphism of
A
-modules

C^{-1}_{q}\colon\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\xrightarrow{\cong}H_{{\rm dR}}^{q}(A_{\operatorname{log}})
.

Proof.

Since the map
\gamma\colon{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]}\to A
is regular, it follows from

[
Popescu
, Thm. 2.5]
that there is a direct system
\{A(\lambda)\}
of
smooth (in particular, finitely generated)

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]}
-algebras such that
A=\varinjlim_{\lambda}A(\lambda)
.
We endow each
A(\lambda)
with the log structure given by the pull-back of the
log structure of
({\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]})_{\operatorname{log}}
. Since the log structure of
A
also has this
property, it follows that we have a direct system of
log smooth
({\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]})_{{\operatorname{log}}}
-algebras
\{A(\lambda)_{\operatorname{log}}\}
such that

A_{\operatorname{log}}=\varinjlim_{\lambda}A(\lambda)_{\operatorname{log}}
in the category of log rings.
As
{\mathbb{F}}_{p,{\operatorname{log}}}\to({\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{[n]})_{\operatorname{log}}
is log smooth, we get that each

A(\lambda)_{\operatorname{log}}
is log smooth over
{\mathbb{F}}_{p,{\operatorname{log}}}
.

It is easy to check directly that each map

F_{p,{\operatorname{log}}}\to A(\lambda)_{\operatorname{log}}
is integral and saturated, in particular
of Cartier type (cf.
[
Hyodo-Kato
, § 2.12]
).
We conclude from op. cit. that there is an isomorphism

C^{-1}_{q}\colon\Omega^{q}_{A(\lambda)_{\operatorname{log}}}\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{q}_{\rm dR}(A(\lambda)_{{\operatorname{log}}})
given by
C^{-1}_{q}(a{\rm dlog}(b_{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}(b_{q}))=a^{p}{\rm dlog}(b_{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}(b_{q})
.
Since this is clearly natural with respect to the homomorphisms of log rings, we
get after passing to the limit that there is a similar inverse Cartier isomorphism
for
A
as well. It is clear from the description that
C^{-1}_{q}
is
A
-linear
if we endow
{\rm Ker}(d)
with the
A
-module structure via latter’s Frobenius
endomorphism.
∎

We let
Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}={\rm Ker}(d\colon\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to\Omega^{q+1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}})

and
B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}={\rm Image}(d\colon\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}})
. Taking the inverse of
C^{-1}_{\bullet}
, we get the following.

Corollary 2.5
.

For
q\geq 0
, there is an
A
-linear Cartier homomorphism

C_{q}\colon Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
which induces an
isomorphism of
A
-modules
C_{q}\colon H^{q}_{\rm dR}({A_{\operatorname{log}}})\xrightarrow{\cong}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.

One can now define
A
-modules
Z_{i}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
and
B_{i}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
as
in the non-log case (cf.
[
Illusie
, Chap. 0, (2.2.3)]
) so that there is a
filtration

0=B_{0}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\subseteq B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\ \bigcup_{i}B_{i}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\subseteq\ \bigcap_{i}Z_{i}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\subseteq Z_{0}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}=\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}

of
\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
together with an isomorphism

C^{-i}_{q}\colon\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\xrightarrow{\cong}{Z_{i}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}}/{B_{i}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}}
for each
i\geq 0
. Using this
isomorphism and mimicking the proof of
[
Lorenzon
, Lem. 1.14]
, one gets the
following.

Corollary 2.6
.

For
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, there are exact sequences

(2.2)

0\to B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to B_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\xrightarrow{C^{m}}B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to 0;

(2.3)

0\to Z_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to Z_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\xrightarrow{dC^{m}}B_{1}\Omega^{q+1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to 0.

2.3.
Identification with
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{{\operatorname{log}}}}

The key fact we need to identify
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
with

W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{{\operatorname{log}}}}
is the following.

Lemma 2.7
.

For every
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, the canonical map

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}
is injective.

Proof.

We shall prove the lemma by induction on
m
. The base case
m=1
follows from
Lemma
2.3
.
For
q,r\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, we let

{\rm fil}^{r}_{V}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}=V^{r}(W_{m-r}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}})+dV^{r}(W_{m-r}\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}})\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.
This yields the (decreasing) V-filtration on
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
as a

W_{m}(A)
-module. Furthermore, we have an exact sequence
(cf.
[
HM-Annals
, Lem. 3.2.4]
)

(2.4)

0\to{\rm fil}^{r}_{V}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\xrightarrow{R^{m-r}}W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to 0,

which is natural in
A_{\operatorname{log}}
.

We let
R^{q}_{r}(A_{\operatorname{log}})={\rm Ker}((V^{r},dV^{r}):\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to W_{r+1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}})
.
By comparing the above exact sequence with the analogous exact sequence for
A_{\pi}

and using induction on
m
, it suffices to show that
the square

(2.5)

\textstyle{R^{q}_{m-1}(A_{\operatorname{log}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{q}_{m-1}(A_{\pi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\pi}}}

is Cartesian for
m\geq 2
.

Suppose now that there is an element
\alpha\in(\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}})\bigcap R^{q}_{m-1}(A_{\pi})\subset(\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\pi}})
. Then

\alpha\in B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\oplus Z_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\pi}}

by
[
Lorenzon
, (1.15.3)]
. We write
\alpha=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1})
with

\alpha_{0}\in B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\bigcap\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
and

\alpha_{1}\in Z_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\pi}}\bigcap\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.
We claim that
\alpha\in B_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\oplus Z_{m-1}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.

To prove the claim, we first note that
B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\subset\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}
by Lemma
2.3
, and this implies that
the map
B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\to B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}
is injective.
We next note that as
\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\pi}}
,
our assumption already implies that

\alpha\in Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\oplus Z_{1}\Omega^{q-1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.
Using
[
Lorenzon
, (1.14.2)]
, (
2.3
) and induction on
m
,
we conclude that

\alpha_{1}\in Z_{m-1}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
. Since
\alpha_{0}\in B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\bigcap\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\subset Z_{i}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}

for every
i\geq 1
, the same argument shows that

\alpha_{0}\in Z_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.

In the commutative diagram of short exact sequences

(2.6)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{q}_{\rm dR}(A_{\operatorname{log}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{q}_{\rm dR}(A_{\pi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

the right vertical arrow is injective by the
m=1
case of the lemma and
Corollary
2.5
. A diagram chase implies that

\alpha_{0}\in B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
. This proves the claim.

We now use
[
Lorenzon
, (1.15.3)]
for
A_{\operatorname{log}}
and
A_{\pi}

(together with a limit argument as in Lemma
2.4
), giving us a
commutative diagram of short exact sequences

(2.7)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{q}_{m-1}(A_{\operatorname{log}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\oplus Z_{m-1}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{q}_{m-1}(A_{\pi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}\oplus Z_{m-1}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since the right vertical arrow is injective, we conclude from the above claim
and a diagram chase that
\alpha\in R^{q}_{m-1}(A_{\operatorname{log}})
. This proves that
(
2.5
) is Cartesian and completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We can now prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 2.8
.

Let
X
be a regular
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme and let
X_{\operatorname{log}}
denote the
log scheme associated to a simple normal crossing divisor
E\subset X
.
Then the canonical map
of Witt complexes
\theta_{X}\colon W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\operatorname{log}}}\to W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}({\operatorname{log}}E)
on
X_{\operatorname{log}}
is a levelwise (wrt
m\geq 1
) isomorphism.

Proof.

We can assume
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
with
A
as in Lemma
2.3
.
We now look at the commutative diagram of Witt complexes on
A_{\operatorname{log}}
:

(2.8)

\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\pi}}.}

The diagonal arrow on the right is injective by Lemma
2.7
.
It is clear from the definition of
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
that it lies in

W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
as a Witt subcomplex of
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\pi}}
.
In particular, the inclusion

W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\hookrightarrow W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\pi}}
uniquely factors through
an inclusion of Witt complexes

\phi_{A}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\to W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.
Since
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
is the universal Witt-complex over
A_{\operatorname{log}}
,
this forces
\phi_{A}
and
\theta_{A}
to be the inverses of each other.
∎

Corollary 2.9
.

Let
X
be as in Theorem
2.8
. Then for every
q,r,s\geq 0
with

r+s=m\geq 1
, there is a short exact sequence
of
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

0\to V^{r}(W_{s}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E))+dV^{r}(W_{s}\Omega^{q-1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E))\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\xrightarrow{R^{s}}W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\to 0.

Proof.

Combine
[
HM-Annals
, § 3]
and Theorem
2.8
.
∎

Corollary 2.10
.

Let
S
be a regular
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra and let
A=S[[T]]
. Then we
have a canonical isomorphism of
W_{m}(S)
-modules

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\oplus W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[T]_{m}\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}T)

for every
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
.

Proof.

Note that
\{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\oplus W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[T]_{m}\}_{m,q}
is already a Witt complex on

A_{\operatorname{log}}
and there is a canonical map
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\oplus W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[T]_{m}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}T)
which is compatible with the
monoid maps

\alpha_{m}\colon M_{A}\to W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}T);\hskip 42.67912pt\alpha^{\prime}_{m}\colon M_{A}\to W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A}\oplus W_{m}(S){\rm dlog}[T]_{m},

where
M_{A}=\{uT^{n}:u\in A^{\times},n\geq 0\}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{m}(uT^{n})=({\rm dlog}[u]_{m},n{\rm dlog}[T]_{m})
. The corollary now follows from Theorem
2.8
and
the universal property of
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A_{\operatorname{log}}}
.
∎

Let
S
be as in Corollary
2.10
. Let
A=S[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}]]
and

T=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\prod}}}T_{i}
. For
q\geq 0
, let

(2.9)

F^{1,q}_{0}(S)={\underset{r+1\leq i_{s+1}<\cdots<i_{q-i_{0}}\leq d}{\underset{0\leq i_{0},1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{s}\leq r}{\bigoplus}}}\Omega^{i_{0}}_{S}{\rm dlog}(x_{i_{1}})\cdots{\rm dlog}(x_{i_{s}})d(x_{i_{s+1}})\cdots d(x_{i_{q-i_{0}}}).

A proof identical to that of Corollary
2.10
also shows the following.

Corollary 2.11
.

For
q\geq 0
, one has a canonical isomorphism of
S
-modules

\Omega^{q}_{A}\oplus F^{1,q}_{0}(S)\xrightarrow{\cong}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}T).

3.
The filtered de Rham-Witt complex

In this section, we shall define the filtered de Rham-Witt complex of an
F
-finite
regular
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme with respect to a simple normal crossing divisor. We shall
also derive the basic properties of this complex. We begin by considering
a special case.

Given an
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme
X
and

D=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}D_{i}\in{\rm Div}(X)
, we let
|D|
denote the support of
D
and
let
D/p=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}\lfloor{{n_{i}}/p}\rfloor D_{i}
,
where
\lfloor{\cdot}\rfloor
is the greatest integer function.
For
m\geq 1
, we let

W_{m}(X)=W_{m}(\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}))
. The elements of
W_{m}(X)
will be denoted by

m
-tuples
\underline{a}=(a_{m-1},\dots,a_{0})
with
a_{i}\in\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
.
If
I\subset A
is an ideal in a commutative ring, we let
W_{m}(I)=\{\underline{a}\in W_{m}(A)|a_{i}\in I\ \forall\ i\}
.

3.1.
Filtration of
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}

Let
X
be an integral and locally factorial
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme and

D=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}D_{i}\in{\rm Div}(X)
. Let

j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the complement of
E
.
The following definition is inspired by (though slightly different from)

[
Kerz-Saito-ANT
, § 3, p. 695]
. When
X
is the spectrum of a henselian discrete
valuation ring, this definition is originally due to Brylinski
[
Brylinski
]
.

Definition 3.1
.

We let

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}({U})=\left\{\underline{a}=(a_{m-1},\ldots,a_{0})\in W_{m}({U})|\ a_{i}^{p^{i}}\in\Gamma({X},{\mathcal{O}}_{X}({D}))\ \forall\ i\right\}.

We let
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
be the presheaf on
{\mathbf{Sm}}_{X}
such that

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}({X^{\prime}})={\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}(U^{\prime})
, where

D^{\prime}=X^{\prime}\times_{X}D\in{\rm Div}(X^{\prime})
and
U^{\prime}=X^{\prime}\times_{X}U
.

One easily checks that
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
actually restricts to a sheaf on
X_{\rm zar}
.
If
X
is integral, we moreover have the inclusions of Zariski sheaves

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\subset{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{W}\subset h_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{W}

whenever
D\leq D^{\prime}
and
h\colon W=X\setminus|D^{\prime}|\hookrightarrow X
is the
inclusion. Note also that the canonical map

j^{*}({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U})\to W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
is an isomorphism.

We let
\iota\colon\{\eta\}\hookrightarrow X
denote the
inclusion of the generic point and let
K=k(\eta)
. Throughout our discussion, we
shall write the constant sheaf
\iota_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\eta}
on
X

simply as
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}
.
If
P\in X
and
x_{i}
is a generator of the ideal of

D_{i}
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}
, then one checks that

a_{i}^{p^{i}}\in({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))_{P}={\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}.(\prod_{j}x^{-n_{j}}_{j})
iff
a_{i}^{p^{i}}(\prod_{j}x^{n_{j}}_{j})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}
. Letting
\pi=\prod_{j}x_{j}
, we therefore
obtain

(3.1)

({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U})_{P}=\left\{\underline{a}\in W_{m}({\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}[\pi^{-1}])|\ a_{i}^{p^{i}}({\prod}_{j}x^{n_{j}}_{j})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}\ \forall\ i\right\}.

If
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
, where
A
is a local ring and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
are
generators of the ideals of the irreducible components of
D
, we shall write

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
as
{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
or
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
, where

\pi=\prod_{i}x_{i}
and

I\subset Q(A)
is the invertible ideal
(x)
, where
x=\prod_{i}x^{n_{i}}_{i}
and

\underline{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
. We shall write
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
as
A{x^{-1}}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
is a sheaf of abelian groups. Moreover, we have an exact sequence

(3.2)

0\to{\rm fil}_{D}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\xrightarrow{V^{m-1}}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\xrightarrow{R}{\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\to 0.

Proof.

We may assume
X={\rm Spec\,}A
and, following the same notation as above, may let
D
be
given by the ideal
(x)
, where
x=\prod_{i}x^{n_{i}}_{i}
.
We only need to show that the map
R
is well-defined and surjective and

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
is a sheaf of abelian groups. The remaining assertions follow from
(
3.1
). We let
\underline{a}\in{{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})}

and let
I^{1/p}=(\prod_{i}x^{\lfloor{{n_{i}}/{p}}\rfloor}_{i})
.
Then
R(\underline{a})=\underline{b}=(b_{m-1},\ldots,b_{0})
, where
b_{i}=a_{i+1}
. We can
write (because
A
is a UFD)
a_{i+1}=a^{\prime}_{i+1}\prod_{j}x^{t_{j}}_{j}
, where
t_{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
for
every
j
and
{a^{\prime}_{i+1}}\in A
is not divisible by any
x_{j}
.

By our hypothesis,
(a_{i+1})^{p^{i+1}}x\in A
. This implies that

p^{i+1}t_{j}+n_{j}\geq 0
, which is equivalent to

p^{i}t_{j}+\lfloor n_{j}/p\rfloor\geq 0
. So we get
(b_{i})^{p^{i}}x^{\prime}\in A
for

0\leq i\leq m-1
, where
x^{\prime}=\prod_{j}x^{\lfloor{{n_{j}}/{p}}\rfloor}_{j}
. This implies that

\underline{b}\in{\rm fil}_{I^{1/p}}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
. Hence,
R
is well-defined. Also, given any

(b_{m-2},{\ldots},b_{0})\in{\rm fil}_{I^{1/p}}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
, we can
reverse the above argument to get

(b_{m-2},{\ldots},b_{0},0)\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
. This shows that
R
is surjective.

Now we show that
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
is a Zariski sheaf of abelian groups by
induction on
m
.
If
m=1
, then
{\rm fil}_{D}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
is clearly a sheaf of abelian groups
(actually of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules). If
m>1
, we let

\underline{a}=(a_{m-1},{\ldots},a_{0})\mbox{and}\underline{a}^{\prime}=(a^{\prime}_{m-1},\ldots,a^{\prime}_{0})\mbox{be \ in}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
. Enough to show

\underline{a}-\underline{a}^{\prime}\in{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
. Since
R
is additive, by induction and the
above argument, we have
R(\underline{a}-\underline{a}^{\prime})\in{\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
. So, we can
write
R(\underline{a}-\underline{a}^{\prime})=(c_{m-2},{\ldots},c_{0})
satisfying the relation
(
3.1
). Then the previous argument shows

(c_{m-2},\ldots,c_{0},0)\in{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
. Now note that
\underline{a}-\underline{a}^{\prime}=(c_{m-2},\ldots,c_{0},a_{0}-a^{\prime}_{0})
which is clearly in
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
(because,

a_{0}-a^{\prime}_{0}\in{\rm fil}_{D}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
).
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

For two Weil divisors
D
and
D^{\prime}
with complements
U
and
U^{\prime}
, respectively, we
have
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}+{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}}\subset{\rm fil}_{D+D^{\prime}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U\cap U^{\prime}}

and
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\cdot{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}}\subset{\rm fil}_{D+D^{\prime}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U\cap U^{\prime}}
under the addition and multiplication operations of

W_{m}(K)
. In particular,
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
is a subsheaf of
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules inside

W_{m}(K)
.

Proof.

Since we can check this locally, we assume that
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
, where
A
is
a local ring. We let
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}\}
and
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\}
be the
generators of the ideals of the irreducible components of
D
and
D^{\prime}
,
respectively.
We let
x=\prod_{i}x^{m_{i}}_{i}
and
y={\prod}_{j}y^{n_{j}}_{j}
, where

D=\sum_{i}m_{i}D_{i}
and
D^{\prime}=\sum_{j}n_{j}D^{\prime}_{j}
. Write
I=(x)
and
I^{\prime}=(y)
.
Suppose
\underline{a}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{x})
and
\underline{b}\in{\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m}(A_{y})
.
We can write
\underline{a}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m-1}}{{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}}V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})
and

\underline{b}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m-1}}{{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}}V^{i}([b_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})
.
This yields
{\underline{a}}\cdot{\underline{b}}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m-1}}{{\underset{i,j=0}{\sum}}}V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})V^{j}([b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j})
.

Using the identity
xV(y)=V(Fx.y)
, we have for
0\leq i,j\leq m-1
,

(3.3)

V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})V^{j}([b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\mbox{if $i+j\geq m$}\\
V^{i+j}([a_{m-1-i}^{p^{j}}b_{m-1-j}^{p^{i}}]_{m-i-j})&\mbox{if $i+j<m$}.\end{array}\right.

By (
3.1
), the condition
\underline{a}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{x})
and

\underline{b}\in{\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m}(A_{y})
means that

xa^{p^{i}}_{i}\in A
and
yb^{p^{i}}_{i}\in A
for each
i
.
From this, we get for
i+j<m
that

(a_{m-1-i}^{p^{j}}b_{m-1-j}^{p^{i}})^{p^{m-1-i-j}}xy=a^{p^{m-1-i}}_{m-1-i}b^{p^{m-1-j}}_{m-1-j}xy\in A
.
This implies by (
3.3
) that

V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})V^{j}([b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j})\in{\rm fil}_{II^{\prime}}W_{m}(A_{xy})
. By
Lemma
3.2
, we conclude that

{\underline{a}}\cdot{\underline{b}}\in{\rm fil}_{II^{\prime}}W_{m}(A_{xy})
. Also, the claim about the addition
immediately follows from Lemma
3.2
by observing that

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
and
{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}})
are contained in

{\rm fil}_{D+D^{\prime}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U\cap U^{\prime}}
.
This concludes the proof of the first part of the lemma. The second part follows from
the first by taking
D^{\prime}=0
and noting that
{\rm fil}_{0}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}=W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.
∎

For
D
as in Definition
3.1
, let
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
be the
sheaf on
X_{\rm zar}
which is locally defined as

W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)=[x^{-1}]_{m}\cdot W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\subset j_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
,
where
[-]_{m}\colon j_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\to j_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
is the Teichmüller map,
and
D
is locally defined by the invertible ideal
(x)\subset K
. One knows that

W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
is a sheaf of invertible
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules. For a sheaf of

W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules
{\mathcal{M}}
, we let
{\mathcal{M}}(D)={\mathcal{M}}\otimes_{W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
. If
A

is a local ring and
D
is defined by
I=(x)
, we also write
{\mathcal{M}}(D)
as
M(x^{-1})

if
{\mathcal{M}}
is defined by a
W_{m}(A)
-module
M
.

Lemma 3.4
.

One has the identity
{\rm fil}_{p^{m-1}D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}=W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
.

Proof.

Since we can check this locally, we assume that
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
is as in the proof of
Lemma
3.3
. We let
I=(x^{p^{m-1}})
.
Following our notations, we see that

\underline{a}=(a_{m-1},\ldots,a_{0})\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{x})
if and only if

a_{i}^{p^{i}}x^{p^{m-1}}\in A
for all
0\leq i\leq m-1
. Since
A
is a UFD, this is
equivalent to
a_{i}x^{p^{m-1-i}}\in A
for all
0\leq i\leq m-1
, or equivalently,

\underline{a}\cdot[x]_{m}={(a_{m-1}x,\dots,a_{i}x^{p^{m-1-i}},\dots,a_{0}x^{p^{m-1}})\in W_{m}(A)}
.
This finishes the proof.
∎

3.2.
Filtration of
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}

We now assume that
X
is a connected regular
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme and let

E\subset X
be a simple normal crossing divisor
with irreducible components
E_{1},\ldots,E_{r}
. We let
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
be the
inclusion of the complement of
E
. We fix
m\geq 1
.

Definition 3.5
.

For
D=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}E_{i}\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)

and an integer
q\geq 0
, we let

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}={\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)+d({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U})\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E),

considered as a subsheaf of
j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
. We shall write

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{1}\Omega^{q}_{U}
as
{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.

For
D=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}E_{i}
effective, we let

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X|D}={\rm Ker}(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\twoheadrightarrow W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{D})

(cf.
[
GK-Duality-1
, § 3]
).
If
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
are generators of the ideals of the
irreducible components of
E
, then we shall write
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
as

{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}
, where
\pi=\prod_{i}x_{i}
and
I=(\prod_{i}x^{-n_{i}}_{i})
.
We let
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(A,I)}={\rm Ker}(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\twoheadrightarrow W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A/I})
if

I\subset A
is an ideal.

Lemma 3.6
.

For
q\geq 0
, we have the following.

(1)

{\rm fil}_{p^{m-1}D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(D)
.

(2)

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
is a sheaf of finitely generated
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules.

(3)

{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}=\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(D)
. In particular,
{\rm fil}_{-E}\Omega^{d}_{U}=\Omega^{d}_{X}
, where
d
is the rank of
\Omega^{1}_{X}
.

(4)

\varinjlim_{n}{\rm fil}_{nE}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{\cong}j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.

(5)

{\rm fil}_{0}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}=W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
and

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X|E}
if
n_{i}<0
for all

1\leq i\leq r
.

Proof.

Since all claims of the lemma can be checked locally,
we shall assume (whenever necessary in the proof)
that
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
and
D\subset X
are as in Definition
3.5
.
To prove (1), we write
I^{\prime}=I^{p^{m-1}}
and note that

{\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}={\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m}(A_{\pi})\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)+d({\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m}(A_{\pi}))\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)=W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)(x^{-1})+d([x^{-1}]_{m}W_{m}(A))\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)

by Lemma
3.4
. On the other hand,

\begin{array}[]{lll}d([x^{-1}]_{m}W_{m}(A))\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)&\subset&[x^{-1}]_{m}d(W_{m}(A))\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&&+[x^{-1}]_{m}W_{m}(A){\rm dlog}([x]_{m})\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&\subset&W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)(x^{-1}).\end{array}

The desired isomorphism now follows.

It is clear that
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
is a sheaf on
X_{\rm zar}
.
To prove (2), we thus have to show that
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
is a sheaf of finite
type
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules. To this end, we choose an effective Weil divisor

D^{\prime}\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
such that
D^{\prime\prime}:=D+D^{\prime}
is effective.
In particular,
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\subset{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\subset{\rm fil}_{p^{m-1}D^{\prime\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
. Since
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is a sheaf of Noetherian rings
by Proposition
2.2
(5), it suffices to show that

{\rm fil}_{p^{m-1}D^{\prime\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
is a sheaf of finitely generated
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules.
But this follows from item (1) because
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
is a sheaf of
finitely generated
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules, by its definition in §
2.1

and Proposition
2.2
(5), and
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D^{\prime\prime})
is a sheaf of invertible

W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules. Item (3) is a special case of (1) when one takes
m=1
.

For (4), we recall that
j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\cong j_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\otimes_{W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}=j_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}

(cf.
[
Illusie
, Chap. I, Prop. 1.11]
).
Meanwhile, one checks directly from (
3.1
) that

j_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}=\varinjlim_{n}{\rm fil}_{nE}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
. This proves the desired isomorphism.

The first part of (5) is clear and to prove its second part, it suffices to show that

{\rm fil}_{-E}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X|E}
.
To show the latter, we let
\underline{a}=(a_{m-1},\ldots,a_{0})\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})

and
w\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
, where
I=(\pi)
.
Letting
\underline{a}=\sum_{i}V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})
,
we have that
a_{i}^{p^{i}}\pi^{-1}\in A
.
This implies that
\pi\mid a_{i}^{p^{i}}
. Equivalently,
\pi\mid a_{i}
(because
A
is a
UFD). We write
a_{i}=\pi\alpha_{i}
, where
\alpha_{i}\in A
.
We can write
w
as the sum of elements of the form

V^{j_{0}}([b_{0}]_{m-j_{0}})dV^{j_{1}}([b_{1}]_{m-j_{1}})\cdots dV^{j_{t}}([b_{t}]_{m-j_{t}}){\rm dlog}([x_{i_{1}}]_{m})\cdots{\rm dlog}([x_{i_{s}}]_{m}),

where
1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{s}\leq r,\ t=q-s
and
b_{i}\in A
.
We have

V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i}){\rm dlog}([x_{i_{1}}]_{m})\cdots{\rm dlog}([x_{i_{s}}]_{m})=\hskip 113.81102pt

\hskip 113.81102ptV^{i}([\alpha_{m-i-1}]_{m-i}[\pi]_{m-i}{\rm dlog}([x_{i_{1}}]_{m-i})\cdots{\rm dlog}([x_{i_{s}}]_{m-i})).

But this last term clearly lies in
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(A,I)}
. Taking the sum over

i\in\{0,\ldots,m-1\}
,
we get that
\underline{a}{\rm dlog}([x_{i_{1}}]_{m})\cdots{\rm dlog}([x_{i_{s}}]_{m})\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(A,I)}
. This implies that
\underline{a}w\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(A,I)}
.
We have thus shown that

{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(A,I)}
.

If
\underline{a}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
and
w\in W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
, then

d(\underline{a})w=d(\underline{a}w)-\underline{a}d(w)
. We showed above that

\underline{a}w\in W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{(A,I)}
and
\underline{a}d(w)\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(A,I)}
.
It follows that
d(\underline{a})w\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(A,I)}
.
This shows
{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(A,I)}
.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

(1) We remark that the equality
{\rm fil}_{-E}W_{m}\Omega^{d}_{U}=W_{m}\Omega^{d}_{X}
in item (3) of
Lemma
3.6
when
m=1
and
d
is the rank of
\Omega^{1}_{X}

is actually true for every
m\geq 1
(cf. Theorem
6.19
(14))
but the proof of the general case requires more work.

(2) We shall show latter (cf. Corollary
)
that
{\rm fil}_{-E}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}=W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X|E}
. On the other hand,
it is easy to check that

{\rm fil}_{-D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\neq W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X|D}
if
D
is effective but non-reduced
and
m\geq 2
, even when
q=0
and
r=1
.

Before we proceed further, we make the following
general definition.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
X
be an
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme and let
Z\subset X
be a closed subset.
A
{\rm Div}_{Z}(X)
-filtered Witt-complex on
X
is a sheaf of
Witt pro-complexes (in the sense of Deligne-Illusie, see
[
Hes-Mad
]
)

(\{E^{\bullet}_{m}\}_{m\geq 1},d,F,V,R)
on
X_{\rm zar}
together with a pro-subcomplex of

W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

\{{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\{E^{\bullet}_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
for every

D\in{\rm Div}_{Z}(X)

such that the following hold for every
m\geq 1
.

(1)

{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m}\cdot{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}E^{\bullet}_{m}\subset{\rm fil}_{D+D^{\prime}}E^{\bullet}_{m}
.

(2)

d({\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m})\subset{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m}
;
R({\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m})\subset{\rm fil}_{D/p}E^{\bullet}_{m}
.

(3)

F({\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m+1})\subset{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m}
;
V({\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m})\subset{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m+1}
.

(4)

{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m}\subset{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}E^{\bullet}_{m}
if
D\leq D^{\prime}
in
{\rm Div}_{Z}(X)
.

In the sequel, we shall use the term ‘filtered Witt-complex’ for an
object such as the one defined in Definition
3.8
and
write it simply as
{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{\star}
when the operators
d,F,V,R
and the
index set
{\rm Div}_{Z}(X)
are fixed.
If
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
is affine, then
\{\Gamma(X,{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m})\}
is a
filtered Witt-complex on
A
. We shall identify this with

\{{\rm fil}_{D}E^{\bullet}_{m}\}
in this case.
The following definition will be convenient in this paper.

Definition 3.9
.

We shall say that
(\{E^{\bullet}_{m}\}_{m\geq 1},d,F,V,R)
is a
good
filtered
Witt-complex if the sequence

(3.4)

0\to V^{m}({\rm fil}_{D}E^{q}_{1})+dV^{m}({\rm fil}_{D}E^{q-1}_{1})\to{\rm fil}_{D}E^{q}_{m+1}\xrightarrow{R}{\rm fil}_{D/p}E^{q}_{m}\to 0

is exact for every
q\geq 0,\ m\geq 1
and
D\in{\rm Div}_{Z}(X)
.

Letting
Z=E
, we now have the following.

Lemma 3.10
.

The Witt-complex
\left(\{j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}_{m\geq 1},d,F,V,R\right)
together with the subcomplexes

\{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}_{m\geq 1,D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)}

is a
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
-filtered Witt-complex on
X
.

Proof.

Step 1:

To show that
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
is preserved under
d
, note that

d({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E))

\subset d({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U})\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)+{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)={\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}
.
We also have

d\left(d({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U})\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\right)\subset d({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U})\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\subset{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}.

Step 2:
We show that
F({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{U})\subset{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
for every
q\geq 0,m\geq 1
.
When
q=0
, the claim is clear using (
3.1
) and the fact that

F=\overline{F}\circ R
, where
\overline{F}(a_{m-2},\ldots,a_{0})=(a_{m-2}^{p},\ldots,a_{0}^{p})
.
We now assume
q>0
and let

a\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}(A_{\pi}),\ w\in W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
. We then have

F(aw)=F(a)F(w)\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
by the

q=0
case and Theorem
2.8
.

Suppose next that
\underline{a}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}(A_{\pi})
and

w\in W_{m+1}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
. We can then write

\underline{a}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m}}{{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}}V^{i}([a_{m-i}]_{m+1-i})
.
This yields

Fd(\underline{a})=Fd[a_{m}]_{m+1}+\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m-1}}{{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}}FdV^{i+1}[a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i}=Fd[a_{m}]_{m+1}+d\underline{a}^{\prime}
, where
\underline{a}^{\prime}=(a_{m-1},\ldots,a_{0})\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
.
In particular,
d\underline{a}^{\prime}\cdot F(w)\in d({\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi}))\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\subset{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}
.
We also have

(3.5)

\begin{array}[]{lll}{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{q^{\prime}}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)&\subset&{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})W_{m}\Omega^{q+q^{\prime}}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&&+d({\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi}))W_{m}\Omega^{q+q^{\prime}-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&=&{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{q+q^{\prime}}_{A_{\pi}}.\end{array}

Since
F(d[a_{m}]_{m+1}w)=Fd[a_{m}]_{m+1}\cdot F(w)

and
F(w)\in W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)

by Theorem
2.8
, it remains to show
using (
3.5
) that
Fd[a_{m}]_{m+1}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}
.

To that end, we write
a_{m}=a^{\prime}(\prod_{i}x^{n_{i}}_{i})
, where
n_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
for every
i
and
a^{\prime}\in A
is not divisible by any
x_{i}
. Then the condition
\underline{a}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}(A_{\pi})
implies that
[\prod_{i}x^{n_{i}}_{i}]_{m+1}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}(A_{\pi})
. Letting
\gamma=\prod_{i}x^{n_{i}}_{i}
, we get

Fd[a_{m}]_{m+1}=Fd[a^{\prime}]_{m+1}\cdot F([\gamma]_{m+1})+F([a_{m}]_{m+1}){\rm dlog}([\gamma]_{m}).

We now note that
{\rm dlog}([\gamma]_{m})=\sum\limits_{i}n_{i}.{\rm dlog}([x_{i}]_{m})\in W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
by Theorem
2.8
which implies that
F([a_{m}]_{m+1}){\rm dlog}([\gamma]_{m})\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}
. On the other hand, we have
Fd[a^{\prime}]_{m+1}\cdot F([\gamma]_{m+1})\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})\cdot W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A}\subset{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}
. This proves our claim.

Step 3:
We show that
V({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})\subset{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}
for
q\geq 0,m\geq 1
. The
q=0
case is clear using
(
3.1
). For
q>0
, we let
\underline{a}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})

and
w\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
. We write
w
