as the sum of elements of the form

V^{j_{0}}([b_{0}]_{m-j_{0}})dV^{j_{1}}([b_{1}]_{m-j_{1}})\cdots dV^{j_{t}}([b_{t}]_{m-j_{t}}){\rm dlog}[x_{i_{1}}]_{m}\cdots{\rm dlog}[x_{i_{s}}]_{m},

where
1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{s}\leq r,\ t=q-s
and
b_{i}\in A
. Letting

\underline{b}=\underline{a}V^{j_{0}}[b_{0}]_{m-j_{0}}
and
w^{\prime}=dV^{j_{1}}([b_{1}]_{m-j_{1}})\cdots dV^{j_{t}}([b_{t}]_{m-j_{t}})
, we get

V(\underline{b}w^{\prime})=V(\underline{b})dV^{j_{1}+1}([b_{1}]_{m-j_{1}})\cdots dV^{j_{t}+1}([b_{t}]_{m-j_{t}})\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}(A_{\pi})\cdot W_{m+1}\Omega^{t}_{A}
.
This yields

\begin{array}[]{lll}V(\underline{a}w)&=&V(\underline{b}w^{\prime}{\rm dlog}[x_{i_{1}}]_{m}\cdots{\rm dlog}[x_{i_{s}}]_{m})=V(\underline{b}w^{\prime})\cdot{\rm dlog}[x_{i_{1}}]_{m+1}\cdots{\rm dlog}[x_{i_{s}}]_{m+1}\\
&\in&{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}(A_{\pi})\cdot W_{m+1}\Omega^{t}_{A}\cdot W_{m+1}\Omega^{s}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&\subset&{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}\Omega^{t}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\cdot W_{m+1}\Omega^{s}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&\subset&{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}.\end{array}

If
\underline{a}\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
and
w\in W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
, then

V(d\underline{a}\cdot w)=dV(\underline{a})\cdot V(w)\in d({\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}(A_{\pi}))\cdot W_{m+1}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\subset{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}
.
This proves the claim.

Step 4:
We now show that
R({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})\subset{\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
. We shall in fact prove a stronger claim, namely,

R({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})={\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.
Now, the
q=0
case of this stronger claim follows already from
Corollary
3.2
. On the other hand, the
q>0
case easily follows from the

q=0
case and Corollary
2.9
because
R
is multiplicative and commutes
with
d
.

Step 5:
In this last step, we show that
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
satisfies
condition (1) of Definition
3.8
.
Using Lemma
3.3
and an elementary computation, it suffices to
check that
{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})\cdot d({\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m}(A_{\pi}))\subset{\rm fil}_{II^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}
, where
x=\prod_{i}x^{n_{i}}_{i}
(resp.
y=\prod_{i}x^{m_{i}}_{i}
)
and
I=(x)
(resp.
I^{\prime}=(y)
).
We let
\underline{a}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m-1}}{{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}}V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})\in{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
and

\underline{b}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m-1}}{{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}}V^{i}([b_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})\in{\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m}(A_{\pi})
. This yields
{\underline{a}}\cdot d{\underline{b}}=\sum_{i,j}V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})dV^{j}([b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j})
.
It suffices now to show that
V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})dV^{j}([b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j})\in{\rm fil}_{II^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}
for every
0\leq i,j\leq m-1
.

We note that
[b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j}\in{\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m-j}(A_{\pi})
. If we write
b_{m-1-j}=b^{\prime}\prod_{l}x^{r_{l}}_{l}
, where
{b^{\prime}}\in A,\ r_{l}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and no

x_{l}
divides
b^{\prime}
, then we get that
\prod_{l}[x^{r_{l}}_{l}]_{m-j}\in{\rm fil}_{I^{\prime}}W_{m-j}({A_{\pi}})

(see the proof of Step 2). We write
\gamma^{\prime}=\prod_{l}x^{r_{l}}_{l}
.
We first assume
i\geq j
and write

V^{i}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})dV^{j}([b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j})=V^{j}(V^{i-j}[a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i}d[b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j}).

It is enough to show using Step 3 that

V^{i-j}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})d([b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j})\in{\rm fil}_{II^{\prime}}W_{m-j}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}
.

We now compute

d[b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j}V^{i-j}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})=d[b^{\prime}]_{m-j}[\gamma^{\prime}]_{m-j}V^{i-j}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})\hskip 28.45274pt

\hskip 142.26378pt+[b^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}]_{m-j}V^{i-j}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i}){\rm dlog}([\gamma^{\prime}]_{m-j}).

The terms
[\gamma^{\prime}]_{m-j}V^{i-j}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})
and
[b^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}]_{m-j}V^{i-j}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})
lie
{\rm fil}_{II^{\prime}}W_{m-j}({A_{\pi}})
by Lemma
3.3
.
Since
d[b^{\prime}]_{m-j}\in W_{m-j}\Omega^{1}_{A}
and
{\rm dlog}([\gamma^{\prime}]_{m-j})\in W_{m-j}\Omega^{1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)

by Theorem
2.8
, we conclude that

d([b_{m-1-j}]_{m-j})V^{i-j}([a_{m-1-i}]_{m-i})\in{\rm fil}_{II^{\prime}}W_{m-j}\Omega^{1}_{A_{\pi}}
.

If
i<j
, we apply the Leibniz rule, Lemma
3.3
and Step 1 to reduce
to the case
i\geq j
. The condition (4) Definition
3.8
is
clear for
\{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}_{D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)}
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Definition 3.11
.

Let
(X,E)
be the snc-pair as above. By the filtered de Rham-Witt complex on
X
,
we shall mean the Witt-complex
j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
endowed with the
filtration
\{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\}_{D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)}
. We shall usually
write it simply as
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
(X,E)
be the snc-pair as above.
A Zariski (or étale) sheaf on
X
of the form
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
will
be called a filtered Hodge-Witt sheaf or a Hodge-Witt sheaf with modulus (
D
).
The cohomology group
H^{i}_{\rm zar}(X,{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})

(for any
m\geq 1,i,q\geq 0
and
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
) will be called a
Hodge-Witt cohomology with modulus.

4.
Filtered de Rham-Witt complex of power series algebras

In the previous section, we showed that the filtered de Rham-Witt complex is an
example of a filtered Witt-complex on a regular
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme with a simple
normal crossing divisor. Our goal now is to show that it is a good filtered
Witt-complex (cf. Definition
3.9
).
This part is very challenging and before we get to its final step,
we shall need to prove several
other properties of the filtered de Rham-Witt complex which are classical for the
ordinary de Rham-Witt complex.

In this section, we shall prove the goodness of the filtered de Rham-Witt complex
in a special case when
X
is the spectrum of a power series ring in one variable.
We shall begin by providing a concrete description of the filtered de Rham-Witt
complex in this case.

4.1.
Description of
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}
using a theorem of
Geisser-Hesselholt

We let
S
be an
F
-finite Noetherian regular local
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra. Let

A=S[[\pi]]
and
K=A_{\pi}
. For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
the invertible ideal
I=(\pi^{n})\subset K
,
we shall denote
{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
by
{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and for any

W_{m}(A)
-module
M
, write
M\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}[\pi^{n}]_{m}W_{m}(A)
as
M(n)
.
We let
{\rm gr}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}=\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}
.

We shall use the following theorem of
Geisser-Hesselholt (cf.
[
Geisser-Hesselholt-Top
, Thm. B]
) in order to describe

{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. Let
I_{p}
denote the set of positive integers prime to
p
.
We fix integers
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
.

Theorem 4.1
.

(
Geisser-Hesselholt
)

Every element
\omega\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}
can be uniquely written as an infinite
series

w={\underset{i\geq 0}{\sum}}a^{(m)}_{0,i}[\pi]^{i}_{m}+{\underset{i\geq 0}{\sum}}b^{(m)}_{0,i}[\pi]^{i}_{m}d[\pi]_{m}+{\underset{s\geq 1}{\sum}}{\underset{j\in I_{p}}{\sum}}\left(V^{s}(a^{{(m-s)}}_{s,j}[\pi]^{j}_{m-s})+dV^{s}(b^{{(m-s)}}_{s,j}[\pi]^{j}_{m-s})\right),

where the components
a^{(n)}_{s,j}\in W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{S}
and

b^{(n)}_{s,j}\in W_{n}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}
.

For
s\geq 0
and
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we let

(4.1)

A^{m,q}_{i}(S)=W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m}+W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m});

B^{m,q}_{i,s}(S)=V^{s}\left(W_{{m-s}}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m-s}\right)+dV^{s}\left(W_{m-s}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m-s}\right).

Note that
A^{m,q}_{i}(S),B^{m,q}_{i,s}(S)\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Definition 4.2
.

For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we let

F^{m,q}_{n}(S)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A^{m,q}_{{n}/{p^{m-1}}}(S)&\mbox{if $p^{m-1}\mid n$}\\
B^{m,q}_{{n}/{p^{m-1-s}},s}(S)&\mbox{if $s={\rm min}\{j>0|p^{m-1-j}\mid n\}$}.\end{array}\right.

Corollary 4.3
.

There is a canonical decomposition

(4.2)

\theta^{m,q}_{A}\colon{\underset{n\geq 0}{\prod}}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi).

Proof.

Theorem
4.1
says that the canonical maps from
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S},\ F^{m,q}_{n}(S)

(for
n\geq 1
) to
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}
induce an isomorphism

(4.3)

\overline{\theta}_{A}^{m,q}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}\bigoplus{\underset{n\geq 1}{\prod}}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}.

We can extend
\overline{\theta}_{A}^{m,q}
uniquely to a map

\theta_{A}^{m,q}\colon{\underset{n\geq 0}{\prod}}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)=W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m}\bigoplus W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}\bigoplus{\underset{n\geq 1}{\prod}}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)

\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{id}}\ \bigoplus\ \overline{\theta}_{A}^{m,q}}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m}\bigoplus W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi),

where the last isomorphism follows from Corollary
2.10
.
∎

In order to extend this decomposition to
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 4.4
.

For all
n,l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
we have

[\pi]_{m}^{l}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)=F^{m,q}_{n+p^{m-1}l}(S).

Proof.

We first consider the
case
n=p^{m-1}i
with
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. In this case, one checks that

F^{m,q}_{n}(S)=[\pi]^{i}_{m}F^{m,q}_{0}(S)
. This yields

[\pi]^{l}_{m}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)=[\pi]^{i+l}_{m}F^{m,q}_{0}(S)=F^{m,q}_{p^{m-1}(i+l)}(S)=F^{m,q}_{n+p^{m-1}l}
.

Suppose now that
n=p^{m-1-s}i
with
0<s\leq m-1
and
|i|\in I_{p}
.
Let
t=n+p^{m-1}l=p^{m-1-s}i+p^{m-1}l=p^{m-1-s}(i+p^{s}l)=p^{m-1-s}i^{\prime}
,
where we let
i^{\prime}=i+p^{s}l
. Note that
|i^{\prime}|\in I_{p}
. Letting
r=m-s
, we then have

\begin{array}[]{lllr}[\pi]^{l}_{m}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)&=&[\pi]^{l}_{m}\left(V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})+dV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})\right)\\
&=&V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{r})+[\pi]^{l}_{m}dV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})\\
&{\subset}^{\dagger}&V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{r})+dV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{r})\\
&&+V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r}F^{s}d[\pi]^{l}_{m})\\
&\subset&F^{m,q}_{t}(S)+V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r}F^{s}d[\pi]^{l}_{r}),\end{array}

where
{\subset}^{\dagger}
is obtained by applying the Leibniz rule to

[\pi]^{l}_{m}V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})
.
We also used the relation
aV(b)=V(F(a).b)
.

Meanwhile, using the relation
F(d[t]_{r})=[t]_{r-1}^{p-1}d[t]_{r-1}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r}F^{s}d[\pi]^{l}_{r})&\subset&V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}d([\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{r}))\\
&\subset&V^{s}d(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{r})+V^{s}(d(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S})[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{r})\\
&\subset&dV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{r})+V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{r})\\
&=&F^{m,q}_{t}(S).\end{array}

This shows that
[\pi]^{l}_{m}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\subset F^{m,q}_{n+p^{m-1}l}(S)
for all

l,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
But this also implies
[\pi]^{-l}_{m}F^{m,q}_{n+p^{m-1}l}(S)\subset F^{m,q}_{n}(S)
,
and we are done.
∎

Combining Corollary
4.3
and Lemma
4.4
, we get

Corollary 4.5
.

There is a canonical decomposition

\theta^{m,q}_{K}\colon\left(\bigoplus_{n<0}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\right)\bigoplus\left(\prod_{n\geq 0}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\right)\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

Proof.

We first note that the canonical map

(4.4)

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(K)\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

is an isomorphism of
W_{m}(K)
-modules. This is easily deduced from

[
Illusie
, Chap. 0, (1.5.3) and Chap. I, Prop. 1.11]
.
Using this isomorphism, we get

\begin{array}[]{lll}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}&\cong&W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)[\pi^{-1}]_{m}\ \ \cong\ \ {\varinjlim}_{l\geq 0}[\pi]^{-l}_{m}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&{\cong}^{1}&{\varinjlim}_{l\geq 0}[\pi]^{-l}_{m}\prod_{n\geq 0}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\ \ {\cong}\ \ {\varinjlim}_{l\geq 0}\prod_{n\geq 0}[\pi]^{-l}_{m}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\\
&{\cong}^{2}&{\varinjlim}_{l\geq 0}\prod_{n\geq 0}F^{m,q}_{n-p^{m-1}l}(S)\ \ \cong\ \ {\varinjlim}_{l\geq 0}\prod_{n\geq-p^{m-1}l}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\\
&\cong&\left[{\varinjlim}_{l\geq 0}\left({\bigoplus}_{-p^{m-1}l\leq n\leq-1}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\right)\right]\bigoplus\left[{\varinjlim}_{l\geq 0}\left(\prod_{n\geq 0}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\right)\right]\\
&\cong&\left[{\varinjlim}_{l\geq 0}\left({\bigoplus}_{-l\leq n\leq-1}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\right)\right]\bigoplus\left(\prod_{n\geq 0}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\right)\\
&\cong&\left(\bigoplus_{n<0}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\right)\bigoplus\left(\prod_{n\geq 0}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\right),\end{array}

where the isomorphisms
{\cong}^{1}
and
{\cong}^{2}
hold by Corollary
4.3

and Lemma
4.4
, respectively.
∎

Our task now is to describe the pre-image of
{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
under

\theta^{m,q}_{K}
. We shall complete it in several steps.
We begin by checking some further properties of the groups
F^{m,q}_{n}(S)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

For all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have the following.

(1)

d(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))\subset F^{m,q+1}_{n}(S)
.

(2)

V(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))\subset F^{m+1,q}_{n}(S)
.

(3)

F(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))\subset F^{m-1,q}_{n}(S)
.

(4)

R(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}F^{m-1,q}_{n/p}(S)&\mbox{if $p|n$}\\
0&\mbox{if $p\nmid n$}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two cases: (1)
p^{m-1}|n
and (2)
p^{m-1}\nmid n
.

Case~1:

n=p^{m-1}i
.

In this case,

F^{m,q}_{n}(S)=(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}+W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m}))[\pi]^{i}_{m}
.
Since

d(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m})\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m}+W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})=F^{m,q+1}_{n}(S)
,
and
d(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m}))\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})\subset F^{m,q+1}_{n}(S)
, item (1) follows.

For (2), note that if
i\in I_{p}
, we have

V(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m})\in F^{m+1,q}_{n}(S)
by latter’s definition.
Next, we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}V(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})[\pi]^{i}_{m})&=&V(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}d([\pi]^{i}_{m}))\\
&\subset&Vd(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m})+V(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m})\\
&\subset&F^{m+1,q}_{n}(S).\end{array}

If
i=pi^{\prime}
, then
n=p^{m}i^{\prime}
and

V(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m})\subset W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{m+1}\subset F^{m+1,q}_{n}(S)
.
Also, we have
V(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m}[\pi]^{i}_{m})\subset W_{m+1}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m+1}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{m+1}\subset F^{m+1,q}_{n}(S)
.

For (3), write
n=p^{m-2}i^{\prime}
, where
i^{\prime}=pi
. We then get

F(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m})\subset W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{m-1}

which lies in
F^{m-1,q}_{n}(S)
. Similarly,

F(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})[\pi]^{i}_{m})\subset W_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m-1})[\pi]^{i^{\prime}}_{m-1}\subset F^{m-1,q}_{n}(S).

For (4), note that
R(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m})=W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m-1}
and

R(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m}[\pi]^{i}_{m})=W_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m-1}[\pi]^{i}_{m-1}
. But this implies that

R(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))=F^{m-1,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)
, where
n^{\prime}=n/p=p^{m-2}i
.

Case~2:

n=p^{m-1-s}i
with
s\geq 1
and
|i|\in I_{p}
.

In this case, we have

F^{m,q}_{n}(S)=V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})+dV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})
,
where
r=m-s

=(m+1)-(s+1)
. But this implies that

d(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))=dV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})\subset F^{m,q+1}_{n}(S)
.
This proves (1). For (2), note that

V(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))=V^{s^{\prime}}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})+VdV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})\subset

V^{s^{\prime}}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})+dV^{s^{\prime}}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})=F^{m+1,q}_{n}(S)
if we let
s^{\prime}=s+1
.

For (3), suppose first that
s=1
so that
n=p^{m-2}i
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{lll}F(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))&=&FV(W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m-1})+FdV(W_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m-1})\\
&\subset&W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m-1}+d(W_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{m-1})\\
&\subset&\left(W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{S}+W_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m-1})\right)[\pi]^{i}_{m-1}=F^{m-1,q}_{n}(S).\end{array}

If
s\geq 2
, we let
s^{\prime}=s-1\geq 1
so that
n=p^{m-2-s^{\prime}}i
and

r=(m-1)-s^{\prime}
.
This yields

\begin{array}[]{lll}F(F^{m,q}_{n}(S))&=&FV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})+FdV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})\\
&\subset&V^{s^{\prime}}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})+dV^{s^{\prime}}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})=F^{m-1,q}_{n}(S).\end{array}

Finally, for (4), note that

RV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})+RdV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})=\hskip 85.35826pt

\hskip 113.81102ptV^{s}(W_{r-1}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{{n^{\prime}}/{p^{m-2-s}}}_{r-1})+dV^{s}(W_{r-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{{n^{\prime}}/{p^{m-2-s}}}_{r-1}),

where
n^{\prime}=n/p
. The latter group is in

F^{m-1,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)

if
s<m-1
and is zero if
s=m-1
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

For all
n,n^{\prime}\geq 0
, we have
F^{m,0}_{n}(S)\cdot F^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)\subset F^{m,q}_{n+n^{\prime}}(S)
.

Proof.

First note that
W_{m}(S)\cdot F^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)\subset F^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)
. Indeed, if

p^{m-1}\mid n^{\prime}
, then this is obvious. If
p^{m-1}\nmid n^{\prime}
, then only need to show

W_{m}(S)\cdot dV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})\subset F^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)
, where

n^{\prime}=p^{m-1-s}i
,
|i|\in I_{p}
,
r=m-s
.
But this follows easily by applying the Leibniz rule.
We now assume
1\leq s\leq m-1
and write
r=m-s
. If
n=p^{m-1}i
,
then
F^{m,0}_{n}(S)=W_{m}(S)[\pi]^{i}_{m}
and we get
F^{m,0}_{n}(S)\cdot F^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)=W_{m}(S)[\pi]^{i}_{m}\cdot F^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)\subset F^{m,q}_{n+n^{\prime}}(S)

by the above observation and Lemma
4.4
.

If
|i|\in I_{p}
and
n=p^{m-1-s}i
, then
F^{m,0}_{n}(S)=V^{s}(W_{r}(S)[\pi]^{i}_{r})

and we get

\begin{array}[]{lll}F^{m,0}_{n}(S)\cdot F^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)&=&V^{s}(W_{r}(S)[\pi]^{i}_{r})\cdot F^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S)\\
&\subset^{1}&V^{s}([\pi]^{i}_{r}.F^{m-s,q}_{n^{\prime}}(S))\subset^{2}V^{s}(F^{m-s,q}_{n^{\prime}+n}(S))\\
&\subset^{3}&F^{m-s,q}_{n^{\prime}+n}(S),\end{array}

where
\subset^{1}
uses Lemma
4.6
(3),

\subset^{2}
uses Lemma
4.4
, while

\subset^{3}
uses Lemma
4.6
(2).
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

For all
n\geq 0
, the map
\theta^{m,q}_{A}
induces an inclusion

F^{m,q}_{n}(S)\hookrightarrow{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Proof.

If
n=p^{m-1}i
, we get

\begin{array}[]{lll}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)&=&A^{m,q}_{i}(S)=\left(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}+W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})\right)[\pi]^{i}_{m}\\
&\hookrightarrow&(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}+W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m}))[\pi]^{i}_{m}\\
&=&{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K},\end{array}

where the middle inclusion is clear (cf. Theorem
4.1
) and
last equality uses Lemma
3.6
(1).

If
n=p^{m-1-s}i
with
1\leq s\leq m-1
and
|i|\in I_{p}
, then

\begin{array}[]{lll}F^{m,q}_{n}(S)&=&B^{m,q}_{i,s}=V^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})+dV^{s}(W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{S}[\pi]^{i}_{r})\\
&{\hookrightarrow}&V^{s}({\rm fil}_{-p^{r-1}i}W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{s}({\rm fil}_{-p^{r-1}i}W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\\
&=&V^{s}({\rm fil}_{-n}W_{r}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{s}({\rm fil}_{-n}W_{r}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\\
&{\subset}&{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K},\end{array}

where
r=m-s
and the last inclusion holds by Lemma
3.10
.
∎

4.2.
Decomposition of
{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
into
\{F^{m,q}_{n}\}

In order to describe
{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
in terms of
\{F^{m,q}_{n}\}
via

\theta^{m,q}_{K}
, we need to dispose of the case
q=0
first. To do this, we
observe that
F^{m,0}_{n}(S)\subset{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)
for
n\geq 0
, as one easily
deduces from Lemma
4.8
for
q=0
.

Lemma 4.9
.

For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, the composite map
\lambda^{m,0}_{n}\colon F^{m,0}_{n}(S)\hookrightarrow{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)\twoheadrightarrow{\rm gr}_{-n}W_{m}(K)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The assertion of the lemma is obvious if
m=1
because
F^{1,0}_{n}(S)=S\pi^{n}
and

{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{1}(K)=\pi^{n}A
.
For
m>1
,
Lemma
3.2
implies that the sequence

(4.5)

0\to{\rm fil}_{n}W_{1}(K)\xrightarrow{V^{m-1}}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)\xrightarrow{R}{\rm fil}_{\lfloor{n}/p\rfloor}W_{m-1}(K)\to 0

is exact for every
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Comparing this sequence for
-n
and
-n-1
and
noting that
{\rm fil}_{-n-1}W_{m}(K)\subset{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)
, we get an exact sequence

(4.6)

0\to{\rm gr}_{-n}W_{1}(K)\xrightarrow{V^{m-1}}{\rm gr}_{-n}W_{m}(K)\xrightarrow{R}\frac{{\rm fil}_{-\lceil{n/p}\rceil}W_{m-1}(K)}{{\rm fil}_{-\lceil{{n+1}/p}\rceil}W_{m-1}(K)}\to 0.

If we write
n+1=pt+q
with
0\leq q<p
, we see that
V^{m-1}
is an isomorphism
if
q\neq 1
. Since
s=m-1
(cf. Definition
4.2
) in this case, we find also
that the image of
V^{m-1}
is
F^{m,0}_{n}(S)
.
If
q=1
, we can rewrite (
4.6
) as

0\to{\rm gr}_{-n}W_{1}(K)\xrightarrow{V^{m-1}}{\rm gr}_{-n}W_{m}(K)\xrightarrow{R}{\rm gr}_{-t}W_{m-1}(K)\to 0.

We now look at the diagram

(4.7)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{1,0}_{n}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{m-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{1,0}_{n}}
\textstyle{F^{m,0}_{n}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{m,0}_{n}}
\textstyle{F^{m-1,0}_{t}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{m-1,0}_{t}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm gr}_{-n}W_{1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{m-1}}
\textstyle{{\rm gr}_{-n}W_{m}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{{\rm gr}_{-t}W_{m-1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

It is easy to check that this diagram is commutative and the top row is exact.
Now, the left and the right vertical arrows are bijective by induction.
We conclude that the same is true for the middle vertical arrow too.
∎

Before we proceed further, we need to compare the topologies of
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)

induced by various filtrations. We let
\tau_{1}
be the topology on

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
induced by the filtration
\{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)(n)\}_{n\geq 0}
(cf. the beginning of §
4.1
).
We let
\tau_{2}
be the topology on

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
induced by the filtration

\{W_{m}((\pi^{n}))W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\}_{n\geq 0}
. We let
\tau_{3}
be the
topology on
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
induced by the filtration

\{I^{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\}_{n\geq 0}
, where
I=W_{m}((\pi))
. Finally, we let

\tau_{4}
be the topology on
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
induced by the filtration

\{{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\}_{n\geq 0}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

The topologies defined above all agree and
W_{m}(A)
is complete with respect
to these topologies.

Proof.

The topologies
\tau_{1},\ \tau_{2}
and
\tau_{3}
will
agree on
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
if
they do so on
W_{m}(A)
. The agreement between
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
in this special
case is an elementary exercise and the the same between
\tau_{2}
and
\tau_{3}

follows from
[
Geisser-Hesselholt-Top
, Cor. 2.3]
. The agreement between

\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{4}
on
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
follows from
Lemma
3.6
(1).
To prove the second part, it is enough to check that
W_{m}(A)
is

\tau_{2}
-complete.
But this follows from Lemma
5.4
.
∎

Corollary 4.11
.

For
n\geq 0
, the map
\theta^{m,q}_{A}
of (
4.3
)
induces an isomorphism

\theta^{m,0}_{n}\colon{\underset{n^{\prime}\geq n}{\prod}}F^{m,0}_{n^{\prime}}(S)\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K).

Proof.

The ring
W_{m}(A)
is Noetherian and
I
-adically (where
I=W_{m}((\pi))
)
complete by Proposition
2.2
(5) and Lemma
4.10
. Since

{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)
is an
ideal of
W_{m}(A)
by Lemma
3.6
(1), it follows that
{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)

is
I
-adically complete and hence
\tau_{4}
- complete by Lemma
4.10
.
That is, the canonical map

{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)\to{\varprojlim}_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\frac{{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)}{{\rm fil}_{-n-n^{\prime}}W_{m}(K)}

is an isomorphism of
W_{m}(A)
-modules. Meanwhile, one deduces from
Lemma
4.9
that the canonical map

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n^{\prime}-1}}{{\underset{i=0}{\oplus}}}F^{m,0}_{n+i}(S)\to\frac{{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)}{{\rm fil}_{-n-n^{\prime}}W_{m}(K)}
is an isomorphism.
Passing to the limit as
n^{\prime}\to\infty
, we get an isomorphism

{\underset{n^{\prime}\geq n}{\prod}}F^{m,0}_{n^{\prime}}(S)\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K),

which is induced by the canonical maps

\theta^{m,0}_{A}\colon F^{m,0}_{n^{\prime}}(S)\to W_{m}(A)
.
∎

In order to generalize Corollary
4.11
to the case when
q>0
, we let

{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}=\theta^{m,q}_{A}({\prod}_{j\geq n}F^{m,q}_{j}(S))
for
n\geq 0
. By Corollary
4.3
, this is a
descending filtration of
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)={\rm fil}^{\prime}_{0}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Lemma 4.12
.

For
n\geq 0
,
{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is a
W_{m}(A)
-submodule of

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
.

Proof.

We only need to show that
{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is closed under the action of

W_{m}(A)
. We let
\omega=\sum_{j\geq n}\alpha_{j}\in{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
,
where
\alpha_{j}\in\theta^{m,q}_{A}(F^{m,q}_{j}(S))
. Write

\omega_{i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\underset{j=n}{\sum}}}\alpha_{j}
. Since

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
is finitely generated
W_{m}(A)
-module
(cf. Lemma
3.6
(2)), it is complete with respect to the

I
-adic topology.
So, we can write
\omega=\lim_{i\to\infty}\omega_{i}
, where the limit is taken
with respect to the

\tau_{3}
topology. Now let
\gamma=\sum_{j\geq 0}\beta_{j}\in W_{m}(A)
, where

\beta_{j}\in\theta^{m,0}_{A}(F^{m,0}_{j}(S))
. Similarly to above, we can write
\gamma=\lim_{i\to\infty}\gamma_{i}
, where
\gamma_{i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}}\beta_{j}
and the
limit is taken in the
\tau_{3}
topology. Then

\lim_{i\to\infty}\gamma_{i}\cdot\omega_{i}=\gamma\cdot\omega
. But
\gamma_{i}\cdot\omega_{i}\subset\theta^{m,q}_{A}(\bigoplus\limits_{j=n}^{2i}F^{m,q}_{j}(S))
by Lemma
4.7
.
Hence,
\gamma\cdot\omega\in\sum_{j\geq n}\theta^{m,q}_{A}(F^{m,q}_{j}(S))={\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
∎

Lemma 4.13
.

d({\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})\subset{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}
for every

n\geq 0
.

Proof.

We continue using the notations from the proof of Lemma
4.12
. For

\omega\in{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, we can write
\omega=\lim_{i\to\infty}\omega_{i}
, where the limit is taken with respect to

\tau_{3}
and hence with respect to
\tau_{4}
by Lemma
4.10
.
We now let
n^{\prime}\geq 0
be any integer. We can then find
i\gg 0
such that

\omega-\omega_{i}\in{\rm fil}_{-n^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for all
i\gg n
.
This implies that
d(\omega)-d(\omega_{i})=d(\omega-\omega_{i})\in{\rm fil}_{-n^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}
for all
i\gg n
by Lemma
3.10
.
But this means that
d(\omega)=\lim_{i\to\infty}d(\omega_{i})=\sum_{j\geq n}d(\alpha_{j})
, where
d(\alpha_{j})\subset d(\theta^{m,q}_{A}(F^{m,q}_{j}(S)))=^{1}\theta^{m,q+1}_{A}(d(F^{m,q}_{j}(S)))\subset^{2}\theta^{m,q+1}_{A}(F^{m,q+1}_{j}(S))
.
Here, the equality
=^{1}
holds because the map
\theta^{m,q}_{A}
is induced by the
natural map
F^{m,q}_{j}(S)\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
, which commutes with
d
.
The inclusion
\subset^{2}
holds by Lemma
4.6
(1).
∎

Lemma 4.14
.

We have
{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subseteq{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for every

n\geq 0
.

Proof.

By definition of
{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and Corollaries
4.3
and

4.11
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}&=&{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)+d({\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}(K))W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&=&{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)+d({\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K))W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&\subset&{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)+d({\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi))\\
&&+\ {\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)d(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)).\end{array}

Next, note that every element of
{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
is a
finite sum of elements of the form
\gamma\cdot\omega
, where

\gamma\in{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)
and
\omega\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)={\rm fil}^{\prime}_{0}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. Following the proof of
Lemma
4.12
, we write
\omega=\lim_{i\to\infty}\omega_{i}
, where

\omega_{i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}}\alpha_{j}
and
\gamma=\lim_{i\to\infty}\gamma_{i}
, where
\gamma_{i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\underset{j=n}{\sum}}}\beta_{j}
(note the change in indices of
\alpha_{j}
and
\beta_{j}
). These limits are
taken in the
\tau_{3}
topology. This yields

\gamma\cdot\omega=\lim_{i\to\infty}\gamma_{i}\cdot\omega_{i}\in{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
This implies
{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\subset{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Similarly,

{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)d(W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi))\subset{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\subset{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Finally,
d({\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi))\subset d(\theta^{m,q-1}_{A}({\prod}_{j\geq n}F^{m,q-1}_{j}(S)))=d({\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})

by Lemma
4.7
.
The latter group lies in
{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
by Lemma
4.13
, and
this finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.15
.

For
n\geq 0
,
{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is open (hence closed) in

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
with
respect to the topology
\tau_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq 4
.

Proof.

Combining Lemmas
4.12
and
4.14
, we get that

{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is
\tau_{4}
-open. We now apply Lemma
4.10
.
∎

Lemma 4.16
.

We have
{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for every
n\geq 0
.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
4.14
, we only need to show that

{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
The latter assertion is obvious if
n=0
because
{\rm fil}_{0}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}=W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)={\rm fil}^{\prime}_{0}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
by
Corollary
4.3
. We assume therefore that
n\geq 1
.
In particular,
{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}
.
We now let
\omega\in{\rm fil}^{\prime}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
We write
\omega=\lim_{i\to\infty}\omega_{i}
as in the proof of
Lemma
4.12
, where the limit is taken with respect to
\tau_{3}

(cf.
[
Geisser-Hesselholt-Top
, Proof of Thm. B, p. 488]
).
Lemma
4.8
then says that
\omega_{i}\in{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

for each
i\geq n
. Since
{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is
\tau_{4}
-open (and hence
closed) in
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
by Corollary
4.15
, we conclude that

\omega\in{\rm fil}_{-n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
∎

We shall now describe
{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for arbitrary

n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. For
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we let
t=n-p^{m-1}l
.

Lemma 4.17
.

We have
{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}(K)=[\pi]^{l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)
for every
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

Let
x=(a_{m-1},\ldots,a_{0})\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)
, that is,

a_{i}^{p^{i}}\pi^{n}\in A
for each
i
.
Then
[\pi]^{l}_{m}x=(a_{m-1}\pi^{l},a_{m-2}\pi^{pl},\ldots,a_{0}\pi^{p^{m-1}l})
.
Letting
b_{i}=a_{i}\pi^{p^{m-1-i}l}
, we get
(b_{i})^{p^{i}}\pi^{t}=a_{i}^{p^{i}}\pi^{p^{m-1}l+t}=a_{i}^{p^{i}}\pi^{n}\in A
. This implies that

[\pi]^{l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)\subset{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}(K)
for all
l,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then,
we also get
[\pi]^{-l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}(K)\subset{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 4.18
.

We have
{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}=[\pi]^{l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for every
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

By definition,
{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)+d({\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}(K))W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
.
By Lemma
4.17
,
{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)=[\pi]^{l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
and

\begin{array}[]{lll}d({\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}(K))W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)&=&d([\pi]^{l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K))W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&\subset&[\pi]^{l}_{m}d({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K))W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&&+\ {\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)[\pi]^{l}_{m}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi){\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})\\
&\subset&[\pi]^{l}_{m}\big(d({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K))W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\\
&&+\ {\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})\big)\\
&=&[\pi]^{l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}.\end{array}

This shows that
{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subseteq[\pi]^{l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for
all
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. The reverse inclusion also follows in the same way we did before
(cf. Lemma
4.17
).
∎

We can finally describe the pre-image of
{\rm fil}_{\bullet}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
under the
isomorphism
\theta^{m,q}_{K}
of Corollary
4.5
as follows.

Proposition 4.19
.

For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, there is a canonical decomposition

\theta^{m,q}_{K}\colon\prod_{j\geq-n}F^{m,q}_{j}(S)\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

Proof.

For
n\leq 0
, this follows from Lemma
4.16
.
Suppose now
n\geq 0
. Choose
l\gg 0
such that
t=n-p^{m-1}l<0
.
Lemma
4.18
then says that

{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}=[\pi]^{-l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
On the other hand, we have

[\pi]^{-l}_{m}{\rm fil}_{t}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ {\cong^{1}}\ [\pi]^{-l}_{m}\left(\prod_{j\geq-t}F^{m,q}_{j}(S)\right)\ \cong\ \prod_{j\geq-t}[\pi]^{-l}_{m}F^{m,q}_{j}(S)\ \cong\ \prod_{j\geq-t}F^{m,q}_{j-p^{m-1}l}(S),

where the first and the last isomorphisms are by Lemma
4.16

(induced by
(\theta^{m,q}_{A})^{-1}
) and Lemma
4.4
, respectively.
By a change of variables
j^{\prime}=j-p^{m-1}l
, we get

\prod_{j\geq-t}F^{m,q}_{j-p^{m-1}l}(S)=\prod_{j^{\prime}\geq-n}F^{m,q}_{j^{\prime}}(S).

Hence, we get an isomorphism
{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\cong\prod_{j^{\prime}\geq-n}F^{m,q}_{j^{\prime}}(S)
, whose inverse is induced by the canonical map

F^{m,q}_{j^{\prime}}(S)\to{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for
j^{\prime}\geq-n
(because
\cong^{1}
is
induced by
(\theta^{m,q}_{A})^{-1}
). We conclude that the resulting (inverse)
isomorphism is
\theta^{m,q}_{K}
.
∎

4.3.
Goodness of
{\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}

As the first application of Proposition
4.19
, we get the following
result which proves the goodness of the filtered de Rham-Witt complex in a
special case.

Proposition 4.20
.

For every
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, there is a short exact sequence

0\to V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\to{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{R}{\rm fil}_{\lfloor{n/p}\rfloor}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to 0.

Proof.

The surjectivity of
R
is already shown in the proof of Step 4 of
Lemma
3.10
. To prove the exactness in the middle, we let

M=V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
and

N={\rm Ker}(R)
. It follows then from
[
Shiho
, Prop. 2.3]
that

M\subset N=(V^{m-1}(\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}(\Omega^{q-1}_{K}))\bigcap{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

We now let
x\in N
. By Proposition
4.19
, we can uniquely write

x=\sum_{i\geq-n}a_{i}
with
a_{i}\in F^{m,q}_{i}(S)
.
By Corollary
4.5
, we can also write

x=V^{m-1}(\sum_{i\geq-l}b_{i})+dV^{m-1}(\sum_{i\geq-l}c_{i})
for some
l\gg|n|

and
b_{i}\in F^{1,q}_{i}(S),\ c_{i}\in F^{1,q-1}_{i}(S)
.
Hence, we get
\sum_{i\geq-n}a_{i}=\sum_{i\geq-l}(V^{m-1}(b_{i})+dV^{m-1}(c_{i}))
.
Letting
a^{\prime}_{i}=(V^{m-1}(b_{i})+dV^{m-1}(c_{i}))
, we get from Lemma
4.6

that
a^{\prime}_{i}\in F^{m,q}_{i}(S)
. We conclude from Corollary
4.5
that

a_{i}=a^{\prime}_{i}
for each
i\geq-l
. In particular,
a^{\prime}_{i}=0
for
i<-n
.

We thus get

x=\sum_{i\geq-n}V^{m-1}(b_{i})+\sum_{i\geq-n}dV^{m-1}(c_{i})=V^{m-1}\left(\sum_{i\geq-n}b_{i}\right)+dV^{m-1}\left(\sum_{i\geq-n}c_{i}\right).

Note here that
V
and
d
are continuous with respect to the topology
\tau_{2}

by virtue of Lemmas
3.10
and
4.10
.
Since
b_{i}\in F^{1,q}_{i}(S)
and
c_{i}\in F^{1,q-1}_{i}(S)
, Proposition
4.19

implies that
\sum_{i\geq-n}b_{i}\in{\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

\sum_{i\geq-n}c_{i}\in{\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
. Letting

b=\sum_{i\geq-n}b_{i}
and
c=\sum_{i\geq-n}c_{i}
, we get that

x=V^{m-1}(b)+dV^{m-1}(c)
, where
b\in{\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

c\in{\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
. It follows that
x\in M
.
∎

5.
Filtered de Rham-Witt complex of regular
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebras

The goal of this section is to prove the goodness of (i.e., to
extend Proposition
4.20
to) the filtered de Rham-Witt complex of
arbitrary regular local
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebras.
We begin with an explicit description of the filtered
de Rham complex of a multivariate power series ring.

5.1.
de Rham complex of multivariate power series ring

We let
S
be an
F
-finite Noetherian regular local
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra and
let
A=S[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}]]
. We let
1\leq r\leq d
and
K=A_{\pi}
, where

\pi=\prod_{1\leq i\leq r}x_{i}
. For
\underline{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
and the
invertible ideal
I=(x^{n_{1}}_{1}\cdots x^{n_{r}}_{r})\subset K
, we shall
denote
{\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
by
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and write

M(x^{n_{1}}_{1}\cdots x^{n_{r}}_{r})
as
M(\underline{n})
for any
W_{m}(A)
-module
M

(cf. notations in the beginning of §
4.1
). Also,
\underline{n}/p
will denote

(\lfloor n_{1}/p\rfloor,\ldots,\lfloor n_{r}/p\rfloor)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
.

We let
q\geq 0
and let
F^{1,q}_{0}(S)
be as in (
2.9
).
Then one notes that
F^{1,q}_{0}(S)\subset\Omega^{q}_{K}
. For

\underline{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{d})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
,
we let
F^{1,q}_{\underline{m}}(S)=(\prod_{i}x^{m_{i}}_{i})F^{1,q}_{0}(S)
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For any
\underline{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
, there is a canonical isomorphism
of
A
-modules

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{\cong}{\underset{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{d}}{\prod}}(x^{-n_{1}}_{1}\cdots x^{-n_{r}}_{r})F^{1,q}_{\underline{m}}(S).

Proof.

From Corollary
2.11
, we have that
\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\cong{\underset{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{d}}{\prod}}F^{1,q}_{\underline{m}}(S)
.
The asserted expression for
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
now follows from
its definition and item (2) of Lemma
3.6
.
∎

For
\underline{m}=((m_{1},\ldots,m_{r}),(m_{r+1},\ldots,m_{d}))\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{d-r}
,
we let
x^{\underline{m}}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq d}x^{m_{i}}_{i}
.
Taking the colimit of

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
as
\underline{n}\to\infty
, we get

Corollary 5.2
.

Every element
\omega\in\Omega^{q}_{K}
can be written uniquely as an infinite
series

\omega={\underset{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{d-r}}{\sum}}a_{\underline{m}}x^{\underline{m}}
,
where
a_{\underline{m}}\in F^{1,q}_{0}(S)
for all
\underline{m}
and
a_{\underline{m}}=0
if

m_{i}\ll 0
for some
1\leq i\leq r
. Furthermore,
\omega\in\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
if
and only if
a_{\underline{m}}=0
for every
\underline{m}
such that
m_{i}<0
for some

1\leq i\leq r
, and
\omega\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}

if and only if
a_{\underline{m}}=0
for every
\underline{m}
such that

m_{i}<-n_{i}
for some
1\leq i\leq r
.

5.2.
From complete to non-complete case

We let
A
be a regular local
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra with
maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
. We let
\pi=x_{1}\cdots x_{r}
and

K=A_{\pi}
, where
1\leq r\leq d
and
L=Q(A)
. We let
\widehat{A}
denote the

{\mathfrak{m}}
-adic completion of

A
with the maximal ideal
\widehat{{\mathfrak{m}}}
and let
\widehat{K}=\widehat{A}_{\pi}
.
For
1\leq i\leq r
, we let
A_{i}=A_{(x_{i})},\ \widehat{A}_{i}=\widehat{A_{(x_{i})}}
and

\widehat{K}_{i}=Q(\widehat{A}_{i})
. We fix integers
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
.
For
\underline{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
and the invertible ideal

I=(x^{n_{1}}_{1}\cdots x^{n_{r}}_{r})\subset K
, we let
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}_{I}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. We also let

t\underline{n}=(tn_{1},{\ldots},tn_{r})
for any
t\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and

-\underline{1}=(-1,\ldots,-1)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
. We let

X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
and
X_{f}={\rm Spec\,}(A[f^{-1}])
for any
f\in A\setminus\{0\}
.
Let
\gamma_{i}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}
denote the pull-back map
induced by the inclusion
K\hookrightarrow\widehat{K}_{i}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For
1\leq i\leq r
, the map

\gamma_{i}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}
is injective.

Proof.

We look at the commutative diagram

(5.1)

\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}{\underset{W_{m}(A)}{\otimes}}W_{m}(A)_{[\pi]_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{i}}{\underset{W_{m}(A)}{\otimes}}W_{m}(A)_{[\pi]_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}_{i}}{\underset{W_{m}(A)}{\otimes}}W_{m}(A)_{[\pi]_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}{\underset{W_{m}(A)}{\otimes}}W_{m}(A)_{[\pi]_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{1}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{i}}{\underset{W_{m}(A_{i})}{\otimes}}W_{m}(A_{i})_{[x_{i}]_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{2}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}_{i}}{\underset{W_{m}(\widehat{A}_{i})}{\otimes}}W_{m}(\widehat{A}_{i})_{[x_{i}]_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{3}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{1}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{2}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}.}

Since
A
is a regular local
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra, the composition

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{L}
of all left vertical
arrows with
\eta_{1}
is injective (cf.
[
KP-Comp
, Prop. 2.8]
). It follows
that
\alpha_{1}
is injective. Next, Lemma
5.4
implies that

W_{m}(\widehat{A}_{i})
is the
W_{m}((x_{i}))
-adic completion of
W_{m}(A_{i})
, and therefore, it
is flat over
W_{m}(A_{i})
. We can therefore use Proposition
2.2
to deduce that
the arrow
\alpha_{2}
is injective. As the vertical arrows
on the top level are the localizations, it follows that
\beta_{1}
and

\beta_{2}
are injective.
Since the vertical arrows on the middle and the bottom levels are
isomorphisms by
[
Illusie
, Chap. I, Prop. 1.11]
,
it follows that
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
are injective. This concludes the
proof.
∎

The following result is standard.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
m\geq 1
and
S
be an
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra. Let
I\subset S
be an ideal and
let
\widehat{S}
denote the
I
-adic completion of
S
. Then the canonical
map
W_{m}(\widehat{S})\to\varprojlim_{n}{W_{m}(S)}/{W_{m}(I^{n})}
is an isomorphism.
If
S
is a local ring with maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}
, then
W_{m}(S)
is a local
ring with maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{M}}_{m}=\{\underline{a}={(a_{m-1},\ldots,a_{0})}|\ a_{m-1}\in{\mathfrak{m}}\}
and
{{\mathfrak{M}}_{m}}/{W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})}
is a
nilpotent ideal in
W_{m}({S}/{{\mathfrak{m}}})
. In particular,
({\mathfrak{M}}_{m})^{n}\subseteq W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})

for all
n\gg 0
if
S
is moreover Noetherian.

Proof.

The first claim follows from
[
Geisser-Hesselholt-Top
, Lem. 2.1]
since the
canonical map
W_{m}(\widehat{S})\to\varprojlim_{n}W_{m}({S}/{I^{n}})
is clearly bijective
as one can check by induction on
m
. For the second claim, we recall that
the kernel of the surjection
R\colon W_{m}(S)\twoheadrightarrow W_{m-1}(S)
is nilpotent
because
V^{m-1}({a})V^{m-1}({a})=V(V^{m-2}({a})\cdot FV^{m-1}({a}))=V(V^{m-2}({a})\cdot V^{m-2}(p{a}))=0
for any
a\in S
. This fact and
induction on
m
together show that
W_{m}(S)
is local with maximal ideal

{\mathfrak{M}}_{m}
. Applying this fact to
{S}/{{\mathfrak{m}}}
instead of
S
and using induction on

m
, we get that
{{\mathfrak{M}}_{m}}/{W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})}
is nilpotent. The last claim follows from
Proposition
2.2
which says that
W_{m}(S)
is Noetherian if
S
is so.
∎

If
S
is a local ring with maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
M
is any
W_{m}(S)
-module,
we let
M^{\wedge}
denote the
{\mathfrak{M}}_{m}
-adic completion of
M
. From
Lemma
5.4
, it follows that the canonical map from the

W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic completion of
M
to
M^{\wedge}
is an isomorphism.

Returning back to our set-up, we conclude
from Proposition
2.2
and Lemma
5.4

that
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}
is a finitely generated
W_{m}(A)
-module and

(5.2)

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\xrightarrow{\cong}{(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A})}^{\wedge}\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}.

Lemma 5.5
.

There are canonical isomorphisms of
W_{m}(\widehat{A})
-modules

(5.3)

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\xrightarrow{\cong}{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)}^{\wedge}\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}({\operatorname{log}}\pi).

Proof.

Since
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
is a finitely generated
W_{m}(A)
-module as we observed
earlier, the first isomorphism in (
5.3
)
is clear. By the definition of
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
and
(
5.2
), we see that the composite arrow in
(
5.3
) is surjective. To show it is injective, we look at
the commutative diagram

(5.4)

\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A_{\pi}}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}.}

The top horizontal arrow in (
5.4
)
is injective because
W_{m}(\widehat{A})
is
a flat
W_{m}(A)
-module. Furthermore,

(5.5)

\begin{array}[]{lll}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})&\cong&W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(A_{\pi})\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\\
&\cong&W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}\otimes_{W_{m}(\widehat{A})}(W_{m}(\widehat{A})\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(K))\\
&\cong&W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}\otimes_{W_{m}(\widehat{A})}W_{m}(\widehat{A})_{[\pi]_{m}}\\
&\cong&W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}\otimes_{W_{m}(\widehat{A})}W_{m}(\widehat{K})\cong W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}.\end{array}

as a
W_{m}(\widehat{A})
-module.
This shows that the right vertical arrow in (
5.4
) is
bijective (as it is same as the composition of the isomorphisms in
(
5.5
)).
It follows that the left vertical arrow is injective. This finishes
the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

The maps

(1)

d\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}
;

(2)

V\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
;

(3)

F\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
;

(4)

R\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

are continuous with respect to the
W_{\star}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic
topology of their sources and targets.

Proof.

The claims (3) and (4) are easy to verify. To prove (1) and (2), we note that

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is a finitely generated
W_{m}(A)
-module by
Lemma
3.6
. We let

J_{s}\subset W_{m}(A)
be the ideal
([x_{1}]^{s}_{m},\ldots,[x_{d}]^{s}_{m})
.
Then the
W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic topology of
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

coincides with the topology given by the filtration

\{J_{s}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\}
as shown in the proof of

[
Geisser-Hesselholt-Top
, Cor. 2.3]
.
Hence, it suffices to note that
given
s\geq 1
, one has
d([x_{i}]^{p^{m}t}_{m}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})\subset J_{s}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}
and
V([x_{i}]^{pt}_{m}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})\subset J_{s}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for all
t\geq s
and all
i
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

For
m\geq 1
, we have the following.

(1)

The map
F\colon W_{m+1}(A)\to W_{m}(A)
is a finite ring homomorphism.

(2)

The
W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic topology of
W_{m}(A)
coincides with its
W_{m+1}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic
topology via
F
.

(3)

For any
A
-submodule
M
of
\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
(resp.
\Omega^{q}_{K}
),

dV^{m-1}(M)
(resp.
V^{m-1}(M)
) is a
W_{m+1}(A)
-submodule of

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, where
W_{m+1}(A)
acts on the latter via
F
.

Proof.

The item (1) is a well-known result of Langer-Zink (cf.
[
Morrow-ENS
, Thm. 2.6]
).
For (2), we can replace (for any
m
) the

W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic topology by the one given by the
filtration
\{J_{s}\}
defined in the proof of Lemma
5.6
.
Our claim now follows because
F([x_{i}]_{m+1})=[x_{i}]^{p}_{m}
for
every
i
.
To prove (3), we let
a\in W_{m+1}(A)
and
x\in M
.
We get

F(a)dV^{m-1}(x)=F(a)FdV^{m}(x)=F(adV^{m}(x))=FdV^{m}(F^{m}(a)x)-F(daV^{m}(x))

=FdV^{m}(F^{m}(a)x)-FV^{m}(F^{m}d(a)x)=FdV^{m}(F^{m}(a)x)=dV^{m-1}(F^{m}(a)x),

because
FV^{m}=0
. This finishes the proof as
F^{m}(a)x\in M
. The other case is
easier as
F(a)V^{m-1}(x)=V^{m-1}(F^{m}(a)x)
.
∎

We shall need the following well-known commutative algebra fact to prove the
next result. We omit the proof.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
S
be a Noetherian local ring with maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}
and let

M
be a finitely generated
S
-module. Assume that
N^{\prime}\subset M^{\wedge}
is
an
\widehat{S}
-submodule. Assume further that
N\subset M\cap N^{\prime}
is an
S
-submodule
which is dense in
N^{\prime}
in the
{\mathfrak{m}}
-adic topology. Then
N^{\prime}=N^{\wedge}
and

N=M\cap N^{\prime}
.

Lemma 5.9
.

The canonical map
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}

induces an isomorphism of
W_{m}(\widehat{A})
-modules

\phi\colon({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})^{\wedge}\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}.

Proof.

Since
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is a finitely generated
W_{m}(A)
-module
(cf. Lemma
3.6
), we know that the canonical
map
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\to({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})^{\wedge}

is an isomorphism. On the other hand, we have an injection

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\hookrightarrow W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\cong W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}
,
where the last isomorphism is by (
5.5
).
It follows that
\phi
is injective.
For surjectivity, we first consider the case
q=0
. If
m=1
, the isomorphism is
clear by Lemma
3.6
(3). For
m>1
, consider the commutative diagram

W_{m}(\widehat{A})
modules.

(5.6)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}K\otimes_{W}\widehat{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{m-1}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}(K)\otimes_{W}\widehat{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}/p}W_{m-1}(K)\otimes_{W}\widehat{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\widehat{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{m-1}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}(\widehat{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}/p}W_{m-1}(\widehat{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
W=W_{m}(A)
and
\widehat{W}=W_{m}(\widehat{A})
.

The top row is obtained by tensoring
(
3.2
) (with
U={\rm Spec\,}(K),D=V((\prod\limits_{1\leq i\leq r}x_{i}^{n_{i}}))
)
with
W_{m}(\widehat{A})
(over
W_{m}(A)
), and noting that
W_{m}(\widehat{A})
is the

W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic completion of
W_{m}(A)
(cf. (
5.2
)),
and hence flat over
W_{m}(A)
. Here, the module structure on
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}K
is given
via
F^{m-1}
. By Lemma
5.7
(2), we see that the
W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic
topology on
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}K
(via
F^{m-1}
) is the
same as the
{\mathfrak{m}}
-adic topology. Hence,

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}K\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})=({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}K)^{\wedge}
, the
{\mathfrak{m}}
-adic
completion of
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}K
as an
A
-module.

We also note that the
W_{m-1}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic topology of
W_{m-1}(A)
coincides with
its
W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})
-topology via the surjection
R\colon W_{m}(A)\twoheadrightarrow W_{m-1}(A)
.
It follows that for any finitely generated
W_{m-1}(A)
-module
M
, one has
isomorphisms

(5.7)

M\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\cong{M}^{\wedge}_{m}\cong{M}^{\wedge}_{m-1}\cong M\otimes_{W_{m-1}(A)}W_{m-1}(\widehat{A}),

where
M^{\wedge}_{m}
(resp.
{M}^{\wedge}_{m-1}
) is the
W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})

(resp.
W_{m-1}({\mathfrak{m}})
)-adic completion of
M
.
Thus, the left and right vertical arrows in (
5.6
) are
isomorphisms by
m=1
case and
induction, respectively. Hence, the middle vertical arrow is also an isomorphism.
In particular, we get that
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}(K)
is dense in
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}(\widehat{K})
.

We now let
q>0
and recall that

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}
is the sum of

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}(\widehat{K})W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{A}}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
and

d({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}(\widehat{K}))W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{\widehat{A}}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)
. Hence, by
Lemmas
5.5
and
5.6
and the
q=0
case, we
get that
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is dense in
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}
.
On the other hand, the lemma is clear if
\underline{n}=p^{m-1}\underline{n}^{\prime\prime}
for some

\underline{n}^{\prime\prime}
, by Lemmas
3.6
(1) and
5.5
. We
therefore choose some
\underline{n}^{\prime}
such that
p^{m-1}\underline{n}^{\prime}>\underline{n}
so that

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}\subset{\rm fil}_{p^{m-1}\underline{n^{\prime}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{S}
for

S\in\{K,\widehat{K}\}
, and apply Lemma
5.8

with
S=W_{m}(A)
(which is a local ring with maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{M}}_{m}
by
Lemma
5.4
),
M={\rm fil}_{p^{m-1}\underline{n^{\prime}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
,
N={\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

N^{\prime}={\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}
to conclude the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.10
.

Consider
F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
as a
W_{m+1}(A)
-module (via
F
). Then
the canonical map
F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})\to F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}})
induces an isomorphism of

W_{m+1}(\widehat{A})
-modules (via
F
)

\phi:\left(F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})\right)^{\wedge}\xrightarrow{\cong}F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}})
.

Proof.

Consider
{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
as a finitely generated
W_{m+1}(A)
-module via
F

(cf. Lemma
5.7
(1)) so that
F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})\subset{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is a
W_{m+1}(A)
-submodule. By
Lemmas
5.6
(3),
5.7
(2) and
5.9
, we know that

F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
is dense in
F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}})
with
respect to
W_{m+1}(\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
-adic topology. Also, by
Lemmas
5.7
(2) and
5.9
, we have

{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}\cong{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\otimes_{W_{m+1}(A)}W_{m+1}(\widehat{A}),

the
W_{m+1}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic completion (via
F
) of
{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Now, we can apply Lemma
5.8
with
S=W_{m+1}(A)
,
M={\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
,
N^{\prime}=F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}})
and

N=F({\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
to conclude the proof.
∎

We let
{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
denote the kernel of the canonical map

\gamma=(\overline{\gamma_{i}})\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\underset{1\leq i\leq r}{\bigoplus}}\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}}{{\rm fil}_{n_{i}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}}.

It is clear that
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Assume now that
A
is complete. For any
1\leq i\leq r
and
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we let

E^{1,q}_{i,n}(K)=\left\{\omega={\underset{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{N}}^{d-r}}{\sum}}a_{\underline{m}}x^{\underline{m}}|\ a_{\underline{m}}=0\ \mbox{if}\ m_{i}<-n\right\}
.
We let

{\rm fil}^{i}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\underset{1\leq j\leq i}{\bigcap}}E^{1,q}_{j,n_{j}}(K)
.
We then get a filtration

(5.8)

\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}^{0}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}(K)\supset{\rm fil}^{1}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\supset\cdots\supset{\rm fil}^{r}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

Lemma 5.11
.

If
A
is complete, then one has a short exact sequence of
W_{m}(A)
-modules

0\to V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\to{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{R}{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

Proof.

We let
{T}=V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
.
Using
[
Shiho
, Prop. 2.3]
, we only need to show that
every element
\omega\in\left(V^{m-1}(\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}(\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\right)\bigcap{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
lies in
{T}
.
We shall show more generally that for every
0\leq i\leq r
, we can write

\omega=V^{m-1}(a)+dV^{m-1}(b)

with
a\in{\rm fil}^{i}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and
b\in{\rm fil}^{i}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}

(cf. (
5.8
)).

We write
A=k[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}]]
, where
k={A}/{{\mathfrak{m}}}
.
If
d=1
, the lemma already follows from Proposition
4.20
.
We therefore assume
d\geq 2
and write

A=B[[x_{i}]]
, where
B=k[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1},x_{i+1},\ldots,x_{d}]]
.
We then have a canonical isomorphism
\widehat{A}_{i}\cong Q(B)[[x_{i}]]
under which the
inclusion
A\hookrightarrow\widehat{A}_{i}
coincides with the map

(5.9)

\theta_{i}\colon B[[x_{i}]]\to Q(B)[[x_{i}]],

which is identity on
B
and sends
x_{i}
to itself. In particular, the
inclusion
K\hookrightarrow\widehat{K}_{i}
coincides with the map induced by

\theta_{i}
on the quotient fields of its source and target.
Furthermore,
\gamma_{i}
is induced by
\theta_{i}
.

We now write
\omega=V^{m-1}(a_{0})+dV^{m-1}(b_{0})
for some

a_{0}\in\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}^{0}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

b_{0}\in\Omega^{q-1}_{K}={\rm fil}^{0}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
.
We suppose next that
i\geq 1
and that we have found

a\in{\rm fil}^{i-1}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

b\in{\rm fil}^{i-1}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
such that

\omega=V^{m-1}(a)+dV^{m-1}(b)
.
In the notations of Corollary
5.2
, we can uniquely write

a=\sum_{\underline{m}}a_{\underline{m}}x^{\underline{m}}
and
b=\sum_{\underline{m}}b_{\underline{m}}x^{\underline{m}}

such that
a_{\underline{m}}=0=b_{\underline{m}}
for any
\underline{m}
such that
m_{j}<-n_{j}
for
some
j<i
. We can uniquely write
a=a_{1}+a_{2}
and
b=b_{1}+b_{2}
such that

(1)

a_{1},a_{2}\in{\rm fil}^{i-1}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

b_{1},b_{2}\in{\rm fil}^{i-1}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
.

(2)

a_{1}\in{\rm fil}^{i}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K},\ b_{1}\in{\rm fil}^{i}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
.
In particular,

\gamma_{i}\left(V^{m-1}(a_{1})+dV^{m-1}(b_{1})\right)\in{\rm fil}_{n_{i}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}
by Lemma
3.10
(since
\gamma_{i}

commutes with all operators and
\gamma_{i}(a_{1})\in{\rm fil}^{i}_{n_{i}}\Omega^{q}_{K_{i}}

and
\gamma_{i}(b_{1})\in{\rm fil}^{i}_{n_{i}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K_{i}}
).

(3)

No term of the infinite series
a_{2}
(resp.
b_{2}
) lies in

{\rm fil}^{i}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
(resp.
{\rm fil}^{i}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
).

The above conditions imply that

\gamma_{i}\left(V^{m-1}(a_{2})+dV^{m-1}(b_{2})\right)=\gamma_{i}\left(\omega-(V^{m-1}(a_{1})+dV^{m-1}(b_{1}))\right)\in{\rm fil}_{n_{i}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}.

On the other hand, Proposition
4.19
says that

\gamma_{i}(a_{2})=\theta^{*}_{i}(a_{2})\in{\underset{j<-n_{i}}{\prod}}F^{1,q}_{j}(Q(B))

and
\gamma_{i}(b_{2})=\theta^{*}_{i}(b_{2})\in{\underset{j<-n_{i}}{\prod}}F^{1,q-1}_{j}(Q(B))
. By Lemma
4.6
,
this implies that

\gamma_{i}\left(V^{m-1}(a_{2})+dV^{m-1}(b_{2})\right)=V^{m-1}(\gamma_{i}(a_{2}))+dV^{m-1}(\gamma_{i}(b_{2}))\in{\underset{j<-n_{i}}{\prod}}F^{m,q}_{j}(Q(B)).

We thus get

\gamma_{i}\left(V^{m-1}(a_{2})+dV^{m-1}(b_{2})\right)\in\left[{\underset{j<-n_{i}}{\prod}}F^{m,q}_{j}(Q(B))\right]\bigcap{\rm fil}_{n_{i}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}
.
But the latter intersection is trivial by Corollary
4.5
and
Proposition
4.19
. It follows by
Lemma
5.3
that
V^{m-1}(a_{2})+dV^{m-1}(b_{2})=0
.
Equivalently,
\omega=V^{m-1}(a_{1})+dV^{m-1}(b_{1})
. We are now done by the
condition (2) above and induction on
i
.
∎

We now prove the result we were after. This result generalizes Proposition
4.20
.

Proposition 5.12
.

There is a short exact sequence
of
W_{m}(A)
-modules

0\to V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{R}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to 0.

Proof.

We only need to show that
{\rm Ker}(R)\subset V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
as the surjectivity of
R
was already shown in
the proof of Lemma
3.10
.
If
A
is complete, we are done by Lemma
5.11
since

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Suppose now that
A
is not complete and let
E=V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
.

One easily verifies that
E
is a
W_{m}(A)
-submodule of

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. Indeed, Lemma
3.6
implies that

E\subset{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. Moreover, one has

a(V^{m-1}(b)+dV^{m-1}(c))=V^{m-1}(F^{m-1}(a)b-F^{m-1}d(a)c)+dV^{m-1}(F^{m-1}(a)c)

for any
a\in W_{m}(A),\ b\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

c\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
. Lemma
3.6
implies once again that

a(V^{m-1}(b)+dV^{m-1}(c))\in E
.
Using the complete case, it remains therefore to show that

E=\left[V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{\widehat{K}})\right]\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
But this follows from Lemma
5.8

because
E
is dense in

V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{\widehat{K}})
by
Lemma
5.6
and Corollary
5.10
.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

The inclusions
K\hookrightarrow\widehat{K}_{i}

induce an exact sequence

0\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{\gamma}{\underset{1\leq i\leq r}{\bigoplus}}\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}}{{\rm fil}_{n_{i}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}}.

Proof.

The corollary is equivalent to the claim that the inclusion

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is an equality.
To prove this claim, we first assume that
A
is complete and prove
by induction on
m
.
The case
m=1
follows directly from Corollary
5.2
.
For
m\geq 2
, we use the commutative diagram

(5.10)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V^{m-1}({\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}.}

Proposition
5.12
says that the top row in
(
5.10
) is exact and Lemma
5.11

implies that the bottom row is exact at
{\rm fil}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
The left and the right vertical arrows are bijective by induction.
A diagram chase shows that the middle vertical arrow is also bijective.

For
A
not necessarily complete, it suffices to show that

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}\bigcap W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
But this follows because
W_{m}(\widehat{A})
is a faithfully flat
W_{m}(A)
-algebra
(cf. Proposition
2.2
, Lemma
5.4
) and there are isomorphisms

(5.11)

\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})\cong\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})}{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\otimes_{W_{m}(A)}W_{m}(\widehat{A})}\cong\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}}{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}},

where the second isomorphism follows from (
5.5
) and
Lemma
5.9
.
∎

Corollary 5.14
.

The inclusion
{\rm fil}_{-\underline{1}}W_{m}\Omega^{d}_{K}\hookrightarrow W_{m}\Omega^{d}_{A}
is a bijection if

d
is the rank of
\Omega^{1}_{A}
.

Proof.

For
m=1
, this follows from Lemma
3.6
(3).
We now use the commutative diagram

(5.12)

\textstyle{0\to V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{-\underline{1}}\Omega^{d}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{-\underline{1}}\Omega^{d-1}_{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{-\underline{1}}W_{m}\Omega^{d}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{-\underline{1}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{d}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\to V^{m-1}(\Omega^{d}_{A})+dV^{m-1}(\Omega^{d-1}_{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{d}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{W_{m-1}\Omega^{d}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(where the vertical arrows are the canonical inclusions),
Proposition
5.12

and induction on
m
to finish the proof in the general case.
∎

We shall need the following result in the next section.

Lemma 5.15
.

The canonical maps

\frac{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}\to\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}(\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \frac{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})}\to\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{V^{m-1}(\Omega^{q}_{K})}

are injective.

Proof.

We shall prove the injectivity of the first map as the other one is completely
analogous.
We only need to show that every element

\omega\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap dV^{m-1}(\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
lies in

dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
. If
A
is complete, this is proven
exactly as we proved Lemma
5.11
(with
a=0
). If
A
is not
complete, it suffices to show that

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{\widehat{K}})=dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}).

To that end, we note that
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}=({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})^{\wedge}
by Lemma
5.9
. In particular,

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
is dense in
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{\widehat{K}}
with respect
to the
\widehat{{\mathfrak{m}}}
-adic topology. It follows from Lemma
5.6
that

dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
is a dense subgroup of

dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{\widehat{K}})
with respect to the
W_{m}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic
topology. We conclude from Lemma
5.7
that

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}}
is
the
W_{m+1}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic completion of the
W_{m+1}(A)
-module
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

via
F\colon W_{m+1}(A)\to W_{m}(A)
and

dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
is a dense subgroup of

dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{\widehat{K}})
with respect to the
W_{m+1}({\mathfrak{m}})
-adic
topology. By Lemma
5.8
, it remains
to show that
dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
is a
W_{m+1}(A)
-submodule of

{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. But this follows again from Lemma
5.7
.
∎

6.
Cartier operator on filtered de Rham-Witt complex

Recall from
[
Kato-Duality
]
(see also
[
GK-Duality
, § 10]
)
that for any regular
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme
X
, there
is a unique Cartier homomorphism
C\colon Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}

such that
W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{X}\xrightarrow{R}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}
is the composition

W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\xrightarrow{C}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}

and
Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}:={\rm Image}(F)={\rm Ker}(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\xrightarrow{F^{m-1}d}\Omega^{q+1}_{X})
.
Our goal in this section is to explain the interaction of the Cartier
homomorphism with the
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
-filtration of de Rham-Witt complex, where

E\subset X
is a simple normal crossing divisor.
We shall also prove some additional properties of the filtered de Rham-Witt
complex which are known when one ignores the filtration. In the end of the section,
we shall state a global version of our results for
F
-finite
regular
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-schemes.

We let
A
be a regular local
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra with
maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
. We let
\pi=x_{1}\cdots x_{r}
and

K=A_{\pi}
, where
1\leq r\leq d
. We let
\widehat{A}
denote the

{\mathfrak{m}}
-adic completion of

A
with the maximal ideal
\widehat{{\mathfrak{m}}}
and let
\widehat{K}=\widehat{A}_{\pi}
.
We fix
q\geq 0,m\geq 1
and let
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}:={\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for
{\underline{n}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
.
Equivalently,
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm Ker}(F^{m-1}d:{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q+1}_{K})
.
We let
{\underline{n}}/{p^{m}}=(\lfloor{{n_{1}}/{p^{m}}}\rfloor,\ldots,\lfloor{{n_{r}}/{p^{m}}}\rfloor)
.
We shall say
\underline{n}\geq\underline{n^{\prime}}
if
n_{i}\geq n^{\prime}_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
.
We let
-\underline{1}=(-1,\ldots,-1)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
.
We shall continue to use the notations of §
5.2
.

6.1.
The Cartier homomorphism

We begin with the following.

Lemma 6.1
.

The Cartier map
C\colon Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
restricts to a

W_{m+1}(A)
-linear map

C\colon Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

such that
C(dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}))=0
.

Proof.

Let
\widehat{K}_{i}
denote the quotient field of
\widehat{A_{(x_{i})}}
.
By Corollary
5.13
, we only need to show that

C\circ\gamma_{i}(\omega)=\gamma_{i}\circ C(\omega)
lies in

{\rm fil}_{\lfloor{{n_{i}}/p}\rfloor}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}}
for every
\omega\in Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and
1\leq i\leq r
.
It suffices therefore to show that

Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset F({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})

when
A=S[[\pi]]
, where
S
is a regular local
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra.

To that end, we let
x\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap F(W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.
By Corollary
4.5
, Lemma
4.6
and Proposition
4.19
,
we can write
x=\sum_{i\geq-n}a_{i}=\sum_{i\geq-l}a^{\prime}_{i}=F(\sum_{i\geq-l}b_{i})

with
a_{i}\in F^{m,q}_{i}(S)

and
a^{\prime}_{i}=F(b_{i})\in F^{m,q}_{i}(S)
for some
b_{i}\in F^{m+1,q}_{i}(S)
and for some

l\gg|n|
.
By the uniqueness of the decomposition in Corollary
4.5
,
we must have
a_{i}=a^{\prime}_{i}
for every
i\geq-l
. In particular,
a^{\prime}_{i}=0
for

i<-n
. This implies that
x=\sum_{i\geq-n}a_{i}=F(\sum_{i\geq-n}b_{i})
.
Since
\sum_{i\geq-n}b_{i}\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, the desired claim follows.
∎

The following key proposition will be used in §
7.1

(cf. Corollary
7.3
) to study Kato’s ramification filtration.

Proposition 6.2
.

If
A
is a DVR and
n\geq 0
, then every element
\omega\in Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

has the property that
\omega\in Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
if and only if

(1-C)(\omega)\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Proof.

If
\omega\in Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, then
C(\omega)=CF(\omega^{\prime})=R(\omega^{\prime})
for some
\omega^{\prime}\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}

by Lemma
6.1
. It follows from Lemma
4.6
that
C(\omega)\in{\rm fil}_{\lfloor{n/p}\rfloor}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
In particular, the map

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is defined.

Suppose now that
\omega\in Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is such that

(1-C)(\omega)\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. To show that
\omega\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

(and hence lies in
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
), we can use Corollary
5.13
to
replace
A
by
\widehat{A}
. It suffices therefore to prove our assertion when
A
is
more generally of the form
S[[\pi]]
, where
S
is a regular local
F
-finite

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra. We assume this to be the case in the rest of the proof.

We can write
\omega=F(\alpha)
for
some
\alpha\in W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. This yields
(1-C)(\omega)=(1-C)F(\alpha)=F(\alpha)-R(\alpha)
. By Corollary
4.5
, we can write
\alpha
uniquely
as
\alpha=\sum_{i\geq-l}a_{i}
for some
a_{i}\in F^{m+1,q}_{i}(S)
and
l\gg 0
.
By Lemma
4.6
, we get

(6.1)

F(\alpha)-R(\alpha)=\sum_{i\geq-l}F(a_{i})-\sum_{i\geq-l}R(a_{i})\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

If
l\leq n
, then
-n\leq-l
and hence
\alpha\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}

so that
\omega\in Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
If
l>n
, then Lemma
4.6
says that

R(a_{-l})\in F^{m,q}_{-l/p}(S)
if
p|l
and
R(a_{-l})=0
if
|l|\in I_{p}
. Since

F(a_{-l})\in F^{m,q}_{-l}
and
\sum_{i\geq-l+1}F(a_{i})-\sum_{i\geq-l}R(a_{i})\in{\rm fil}_{l-1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, it follows from (
6.1
) that

F(a_{-l})=0
. An induction argument says that

F(a_{i})=0
for all
i<-n
. Thus, we get
\omega=F(\alpha)=\sum_{i\geq-n}F(a_{i})\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
∎

The next few results explain the interaction of the Cartier homomorphism with
other operators of the filtered de Rham-Witt complex.

Lemma 6.3
.

We have the following.

(1)

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}=F({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.

(2)

F\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

induces isomorphisms

\overline{F}\colon{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{\cong}\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})};\ \ \overline{C}\colon\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

Proof.

We first note that the lemma is classical for
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}

(cf.
[
Illusie
]
and
[
Kato-Duality
]
).
To prove (1) and (2), we use Proposition
5.12
and the identity
FV^{m}=0

to get a commutative diagram

(6.2)

\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}
\textstyle{\frac{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}.}

Since
dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})=FdV^{m}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\subset F({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
, we thus get maps

(6.3)
