{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{F}}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}\frac{F({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}\hookrightarrow\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}\xrightarrow{\overline{C}}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

We now look at the commutative diagram

(6.4)

\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}
\textstyle{\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{C}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}
\textstyle{\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}
\textstyle{\frac{Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}(\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{C}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}
\textstyle{\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}.}

One knows classically that the composition of any two adjacent maps in the bottom row
is identity. Since the vertical arrows are injective by Lemma
5.15
,
we conclude that the same holds in the top row too. This proves the lemma.
∎

We let
B_{0}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}=0
and
B_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}=d{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
. We let
Z_{0}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}={\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
For
i\geq 2
, we let
Z_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
(resp.
B_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
)
be the inverse image of
Z_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}

(resp.
B_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
) under the composite map

Z_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\twoheadrightarrow\frac{Z_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{B_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}}\xrightarrow{\overline{C}}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
The following is easily deduced from (
6.2
) and
(
6.4
).

Lemma 6.4
.

(1)

Z_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}=F^{i}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{i+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}),\ \ B_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}=F^{i-1}d({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{i}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
.

(2)

Z_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\supset Z_{i+1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\supset B_{j+1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\supset B_{j}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ \ \forall\ \ i,j\geq 0
.

(3)

The maps

\overline{C}\colon\frac{Z_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{B_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}}\xrightarrow{\cong}\frac{Z_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{B_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}\Omega^{q}_{K}}

and
\overline{F}\colon\frac{Z_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{B_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}\Omega^{q}_{K}}\xrightarrow{\cong}\frac{Z_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{B_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}}

are inverses of each other.

Lemma 6.5
.

We have
Z_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}=Z_{i}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}

and
B_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}=B_{i}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Proof.

If
i=1
, the first claim follows from the definition of

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
while the second
claim follows from Lemma
5.15
.
To prove the first claim for
i\geq 2
, we look at the commutative
diagram of exact sequences

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C}
\textstyle{Z_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{i}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C}
\textstyle{Z_{i-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}\Omega^{q}_{K}}

The right vertical arrows is bijective by induction, and we showed above that the
left vertical arrow is bijective. This implies the same for the middle vertical
arrow. The second claim for
i\geq 2
is proven similarly by replacing
the
Z_{i}
-groups (resp.
Z_{i-1}
-groups) by the
B_{i}
-groups (resp.
B_{i-1}
-groups)
in the above diagram.
∎

Corollary 6.6
.

(
cf. Corollary
2.6
)

For
i\geq 1
, there is an exact sequence

0\to Z_{i}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to Z_{i-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{(-1)^{q+1}dC^{i-1}}B_{1}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/{p^{i-1}}}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}\to 0.

Proof.

The surjectivity of
(-1)^{q+1}dC^{i-1}
follows from Lemma
6.4
(1).
The rest of the corollary is easily reduced by Lemma
6.5
to showing
that

0\to Z_{i}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to Z_{i-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{(-1)^{q+1}dC^{i-1}}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}

is exact, which is classical (cf.
[
Lorenzon
, Lem. 1.14]
).
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

There exists a short exact sequence

(6.5)

0\to V^{m-1}(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})\to Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{R}Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to 0.

Proof.

By comparing with the classical case and using Lemma
3.6
,
one checks that (
6.5
) is a well-defined complex.
Moreover, Proposition
5.12
and Lemma
6.3
(1)
together imply that the map
R
is surjective. We now let
\omega\in{\rm Ker}(R)
.
We then know from Proposition
5.12
that
\omega=V^{m-1}(a)+dV^{m-1}(b)
for some
a\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

b\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
. By definition of
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
,
we then get
da=F^{m-1}d(V^{m-1}(a)+dV^{m-1}(b))=0
. In other words,

\omega\in V^{m-1}(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
.
∎

Definition 6.8
.

For
{\underline{n}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
, we let

Z_{1}R^{q}_{m,\underline{n}}(K)={\rm Ker}(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\oplus{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}\xrightarrow{V^{m},\ dV^{m}}Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})\text{ and}

R^{q}_{m,\underline{n}}(K)={\rm Ker}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\oplus{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}\xrightarrow{V^{m},\ dV^{m}}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}).

One notes that
Z_{1}R^{q}_{m,\underline{n}}(K)=R^{q}_{m,\underline{n}}(K)
. Indeed, any element

(a,b)\in R^{q}_{m,\underline{n}}(K)
has the property that
a\in Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
This is easily deduced from
[
Illusie
, Chap. I, Thm. 3.8]
using
Neron-Popescu approximation.

Definition 6.9
.

For
\underline{n}\geq-\underline{1}
, we
let
C^{m,q}_{\underline{n},\bullet}(A)
denote the 2-term complex

(6.6)

\left(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\right).

We shall use the following property of
C^{m,q}_{\underline{n},\bullet}(A)
.

Lemma 6.10
.

In the derived category of abelian groups, there exists a distinguished triangle

C^{1,q}_{\underline{n},\bullet}(A)\xrightarrow{V^{m-1}}C^{m,q}_{\underline{n},\bullet}(A)\xrightarrow{R}C^{m-1,q}_{{\underline{n}}/p,\bullet}(A)\xrightarrow{+}C^{1,q}_{\underline{n},\bullet}(A)[1].

Proof.

We consider the diagram (for
\underline{n}\geq-\underline{1}
)

(6.7)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{q}_{m-1,\underline{n}}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\oplus{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{m-1},\ dV^{m-1}}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1-C)}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{q}_{m-1,\underline{n}}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\oplus{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{m-1},\ dV^{m-1}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K},}

where we let
\widetilde{C}(a,b)=C(a)
and
\phi=1-\widetilde{C}
. The arrow
\alpha
is
the canonical inclusion (cf. Definition
6.8
).
The rows are exact since
Z_{1}R^{q}_{m-1,\underline{n}}(K)=R^{q}_{m-1,\underline{n}}(K)
as we observed
before. All vertical arrows are defined since

{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
under our
assumption on
\underline{n}
. Since
CdV^{m-1}=0
by Lemma
6.1
, this
diagram is commutative.

We now define
F^{1,q}_{\underline{n},_{\bullet}}(A)
(resp.
\widetilde{F}^{1,q}_{\underline{n},_{\bullet}}(A)
)
to be the 2-term complex consisting of the middle (resp. left)
column of (
6.7
). Using Lemma
6.7
,
our claim then is reduced to showing that the composite map

(6.8)

C^{1,q}_{\underline{n},\bullet}(A)\hookrightarrow F^{1,q}_{\underline{n},_{\bullet}}(A)\twoheadrightarrow\frac{F^{1,q}_{\underline{n},_{\bullet}}(A)}{\widetilde{F}^{1,q}_{\underline{n},_{\bullet}}(A)}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

To prove this quasi-isomorphism, we note that the injective arrow
in (
6.8
) is a quasi-isomorphism
by the definition of
\phi
. It suffices therefore to show that the surjective
arrow in (
6.8
) is a quasi-isomorphism. Equivalently, we need to
show that the left vertical arrow in (
6.7
) is bijective.
To prove this latter claim, it suffices to show that
\widetilde{C}=(C,0)
is nilpotent on

R^{q}_{m-1,\underline{n}}(K)
. To see this, note that the composite inclusion

R^{q}_{m-1,\underline{n}}(K)\xrightarrow{\alpha}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\oplus{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{q}_{K}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{K}

actually factors through the
inclusion
R^{q}_{m-1,\underline{n}}(K)\hookrightarrow B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
by

[
Illusie
, Chap. I, Thm. 3.8]
. Furthermore,
C^{m}=0
on
B_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Since
\widetilde{C}=(C,0)
is zero on
\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
, we conclude that

{(\widetilde{C})}^{m}=0
on
R^{q}_{m-1,\underline{n}}(K)
. This finishes the proof.
∎

6.2.
Some more properties of
{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}

In this subsection, we shall prove some more properties of various operators on
the filtered de Rham-Witt complex that will be used in this text. The most of these
are contained in the following.

Lemma 6.11
.

We have the following.

(1)

{\rm Ker}(F^{m-1}d\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q+1}_{K})=F({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.

(2)

{\rm Ker}(F^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})=V({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.

(3)

{\rm Ker}(dV^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K})=F^{m}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.

(4)

{\rm Ker}(V\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})=dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
.

(5)

{\rm Ker}(V^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})=F^{m-1}dV({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
.

(6)

{\rm Ker}(dV^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}/V^{m-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q+1}_{K})=F^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.

(7)

{\rm Ker}(V^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}/dV^{m-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})=F^{m}dV({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
.

Proof.

We first note that all statements are classical for
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}
and
can be easily deduced from
[
Illusie
, Chap. I, § 3]
using Neron-Popescu
approximation. Now, item (1) follows directly from Lemma
6.3
(1).
Item (2) is a special case of the more general claim that

(6.9)

{\rm Ker}(F^{l}\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+l}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})=V^{m}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{l}\Omega^{q}_{K})

for all
m\geq 0
and
l\geq 1
.
Since this general claim is known for
W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}

(e.g., use
[
Illusie
, Chap. I, (3.21.1.3)]
and Neron-Popescu approximation), one
gets that
V^{m}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{l}\Omega^{q}_{K})\subseteq{\rm Ker}(F^{l}\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+l}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.
We shall prove the reverse inclusion by induction on
l
. We first consider the
case
l=1
. It suffices to show in this case
that
V^{m}(\Omega^{q}_{K})\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}=V^{m}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K})
. But this follows
from Lemma
5.15
.

We now assume
l\geq 2
and
m\geq 1
.
We let
\omega\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+l}\Omega^{q}_{K}
be such that

F^{l}(\omega)=0
. We let
\omega^{\prime}=F(\omega)
so that
\omega^{\prime}\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+l-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and
F^{l-1}(\omega^{\prime})=0
.
The induction hypothesis
implies that
F(\omega)=\omega^{\prime}=V^{m}(x)
for some
x\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{l-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. This implies that

F^{l-2}d(x)=F^{m+l-2}dV^{m}(x)=F^{m+l-2}dF(\omega)=0
in
\Omega^{q+1}_{K}

because
dF=pFd
.
We conclude from item (1) that
x=F(y)
from some

y\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{l}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
This yields

F(\omega-V^{m}(y))=F(\omega)-FV^{m}(y)=F(\omega)-V^{m}F(y)=F(\omega)-V^{m}(x)=0.

Using the
l=1
case, we get

\omega-V^{m}(y)\in V^{m}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{l}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.
In particular,
\omega\in V^{m}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{l}\Omega^{q}_{K})
.

To prove (3), it suffices to show that

F^{m}({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})=F^{m}(W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

But this follows from
Lemmas
6.4
and
6.5
because of the
classical identity
Z_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}=F^{m}(W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K})
. The proof of (4) is identical to that of the
l=1
case of (
6.9
)
using Lemma
5.15
and the classical case.

To prove (5), we can assume
m\geq 2
. In the latter case, we only need to show
that
{\rm Ker}(V^{m-1})\subset F^{m-2}d({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
. By Lemma
6.4
, we can replace

F^{m-2}d({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
by
B_{m-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
By the classical case, we are thus reduced to showing that

B_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\bigcap{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}\\
=B_{m-1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
But this follows from Lemma
6.5
.
The proofs of (6) and (7) are analogous to the proofs of (3) and (5), respectively,
using Lemma
5.15
.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

Assuming
\underline{n}\geq-\underline{1}
, we have

d({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})\subset{\rm Image}(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}).

Proof.

For
\omega\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, we have

d(\omega)=FdV(\omega)-RdV(\omega)+FdV^{2}R(\omega)-RdV^{2}R(\omega)+FdV^{3}R^{2}(\omega)-RdV^{3}R^{2}(\omega)+\cdots+FdV^{m}R^{m-1}(\omega)-RdV^{m}R^{m-1}(\omega)
, as
RdV^{m}R^{m-1}(\omega)=0
. Hence, we get

d(\omega)=(F-R)((dV+dV^{2}R+\cdots+dV^{m}R^{m-1})(\omega))=(1-C)Fd(x),

where
we let
x=(V+V^{2}R+\cdots+V^{m}R^{m-1})(\omega)
. It suffices therefore to show
that
Fd(x)\in Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}
.
To this end, we first note that
F^{m-1}d(Fd(x))=pF^{m}d^{2}(x)=0
,
and this implies that
Fd(x)\in Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}
. Secondly,
Lemma
3.6
implies that
Fd(x)\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}

because
\omega\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. This completes
the proof.
∎

Remark 6.13
.

The same proof also shows that

d({\rm fil}_{-\underline{1}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})\subset{\rm Image}({\rm fil}_{-\underline{1}}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}\xrightarrow{R-F}{\rm fil}_{-\underline{1}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}).

Lemma 6.14
.

The abelian group
\frac{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}}{d({\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})}
is
generated by the images of elements of the form

V^{i}([x]_{m-i}){\rm dlog}([x_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([x_{q}]_{m})
, where

[x]_{m-i}\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-i}(K),\ x_{i}\in K^{\times}
and
0\leq i\leq m-1
.

Proof.

The
q=0
case of the lemma is clear and so we assume
q\geq 1
.
We know from Lemma
3.6
(2) that

{{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}\Omega^{q}_{K}}=\pi^{-{\underline{n}}}\Omega^{q}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)

(
\pi^{-{\underline{n}}}=x_{1}^{-n_{1}}\cdots x_{r}^{-{n_{r}}}
),
which is clearly generated as an abelian group by
elements of the desired kind. This proves our assertion when
m=1
.
Using this case and induction on
m
, the general case follows at once by
Proposition
5.12
, combined with the
observation that
[x]_{m-i-1}\in{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m-i-1}(K)
implies

[x]_{m-i}\in{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}}W_{m-i}(K)
and

R(V^{i}([x]_{m-i})w_{1})=V^{i}([x]_{m-i-1})w_{2}
if we let

w_{1}={\rm dlog}([x_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([x_{q}]_{m}))
and

w_{2}={\rm dlog}([x_{1}]_{m-1})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([x_{q}]_{m-1})
.
∎

Recall from
[
Illusie
, Chap. I]
that the multiplication map

p\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
has a factorization

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle R}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{p}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Lemma 6.15
.

(
cf
.
[
JSZ
, Cor. 2.2.7]
)

Let
\omega\in W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Then
\omega\in{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
if and only if

\overline{p}(\omega)\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Proof.

The ‘only if’ part is clear because
R\colon{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is surjective. For the reverse implication,
we let
\widetilde{\omega}\in W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
be such that
R(\widetilde{\omega})=\omega
.
This yields

VF(\widetilde{\omega})=p\widetilde{\omega}=\overline{p}(w)\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Since
F^{m-1}VF(\widetilde{\omega})=pF^{m-1}(\widetilde{\omega})=0
,
it follows from Lemma
6.11
(2) that
VF(\widetilde{\omega})=V(y^{\prime})
for some

y^{\prime}\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. We now apply Lemma
6.11
(4)
(for
W_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}
) to conclude that
F(\widetilde{\omega})-y^{\prime}=dV^{m-2}(z^{\prime})=FdV^{m-1}(z^{\prime})
for some
z^{\prime}\in\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
.
That is,
F(\widetilde{\omega}-dV^{m-1}(z^{\prime}))=y^{\prime}\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Lemma
6.11
(1) (for
W_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}
) then implies that

F^{m-1}d(y^{\prime})=0
.

As
y^{\prime}\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
,
we apply Lemma
6.11
(1) one more time to conclude that

F(\widetilde{\omega}-dV^{m-1}(z^{\prime}))=y^{\prime}=F(y^{\prime\prime})
for some

y^{\prime\prime}\in{\rm fil}_{\underline{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
In particular,
F(\widetilde{\omega}-dV^{m-1}(z^{\prime})-y^{\prime\prime})=0
.
We now apply (
6.9
) (for
W_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}
and
l=1
)
to find
z^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{q}_{K}
such that

\widetilde{\omega}-dV^{m-1}(z^{\prime})-y^{\prime\prime}=V^{m-1}(z^{\prime\prime})
.
That is,
\widetilde{\omega}-y^{\prime\prime}=V^{m-1}(z^{\prime\prime})+dV^{m-1}(z^{\prime})
.
Equivalently,
R(\widetilde{\omega}-y^{\prime\prime})=0
so that

\omega=R(\widetilde{\omega})=R(y^{\prime\prime})\in{\rm fil}_{{\underline{n}}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Recall that
{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,(-)}
denotes the improved Milnor
K
-theory sheaf
(cf.
[
Kerz-JAG
]
) on the big Zariski site of the category of

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-schemes.
The following result will be used at a later stage in this text.

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
X={\rm Spec\,}(A),\ X_{\pi}={\rm Spec\,}(A[1/{\pi}])
and
Z\in\{X,X_{\pi}\}
.
Let
q\geq 0
and
i>0
be any integers. Then

H^{i}_{\rm zar}(Z,{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z})=0
.
In particular, there is a short exact sequence

(6.10)

0\to H^{0}_{\rm zar}(Z,{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z})\xrightarrow{p^{m}}H^{0}_{\rm zar}(Z,{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z})\to H^{0}_{\rm zar}(Z,{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z}}/{p^{m}})\to 0

for every
m\geq 1
.

Proof.

The second part of the lemma follows directly from
its first part using the exact sequence of Zariski sheaves

(6.11)

0\to{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z}\xrightarrow{p^{m}}{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z}\to{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z}}/{p^{m}}\to 0.

The first part of the lemma for
X
is well-known and follows
from
[
Kerz-JAG
, Prop. 10(8)]
.
We shall prove the same for
X_{\pi}
by induction on
r
.
Suppose first that
r=1
and let
Y={\rm Spec\,}({A}/{(\pi)})
.
For any regular scheme
W
and
n\geq 1
, we let
G_{n}(W)
denote the Gersten
complex

{\underset{w\in W^{(0)}}{\oplus}}K^{M}_{n}(k(w))\xrightarrow{\partial}{\underset{w\in W^{(1)}}{\oplus}}K^{M}_{n-1}(k(w))\xrightarrow{\partial}\cdots.

We then have an exact sequence

0\to G_{q-1}(Y)[-1]\to G_{q}(X)\to G_{q}(X_{\pi})\to 0.

Since
H^{i}(G_{q}(X))=H^{i}(G_{q-1}(Y))=0
for
i>0
by

[
Kerz-JAG
, Prop. 10(8)]
), it follows that
H^{i}_{\rm zar}(X_{\pi},{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X_{\pi}})=H^{i}(G_{q}(X_{\pi}))=0
for
i>0
.

Suppose now that
r\geq 2
and set
\pi_{1}=x_{2}\cdots x_{r}
. We let

X_{1}={\rm Spec\,}(A[{1}/\pi_{1}])
,
Y_{1}={\rm Spec\,}({A[{1}/{\pi_{1}}]}/{(x_{1})})
,
and
U=X_{1}\setminus Y_{1}=X_{\pi}
. Then we have an exact sequence

0\to G_{q-1}(Y_{1})[-1]\to G_{q}(X_{1})\to G_{q}(U)\to 0.

We see by induction on
r
that
H^{i}(G_{q-1}(Y_{1}))=0=H^{i}(G_{q}(X_{1}))
for

i>0
. It follows that
H^{i}_{\rm zar}(X_{\pi},{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X_{\pi}})=H^{i}(G_{q}(U))=0
for
i>0
.
∎

6.3.
Global results

Let
X
be a Noetherian regular
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme and let

E
be a simple normal crossing divisor on
X

with irreducible components
E_{1},\ldots,E_{r}
. Let
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
be the
inclusion of the complement of
E
in
X
. Let
\eta_{i}
denote the generic
point of
E_{i}
and let
\widehat{K}_{i}
be the quotient field of
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}_{X,\eta_{i}}}
. Let

j_{i}\colon{\rm Spec\,}(\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}_{X,\eta_{i}}})\to X
denote the canonical map.
We fix integers
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
. Let
D=\sum_{i}n_{i}E_{i}\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)

and
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}:={\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\bigcap j_{*}(Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})
.
Lemma
6.1
immediately implies the following.

Corollary 6.17
.

The Cartier map
C\colon Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
restricts to a
W_{m+1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-linear map

C\colon Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}

such that
C(dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}))=0
.

Definition 6.18
.

If
D+E\geq 0
, we let

W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}
denote the 2-term complex of Zariski sheaves

(6.12)

\left(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\right).

The local results of Sections
5.2
,

6.1
and
6.2
immediately imply the following
(the last item of the theorem follows from
[
Shiho
, Prop. 2.10]
).

Theorem 6.19
.

Given any
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
, we have the following.

(1)

There exists an exact sequence of Zariski sheaves of
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

0\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}\frac{(j_{i})_{*}(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}})}{(j_{i})_{*}({\rm fil}_{n_{i}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{K}_{i}})}.

(2)

There is a short exact sequence of Zariski sheaves of
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

0\to V^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U})+dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{R}{\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to 0.

(3)

{\rm Ker}(F^{m-1}d\colon{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U})=Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}=F({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{U})
.

(4)

There exist isomorphisms of Zariski sheaves of
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

\overline{F}\colon{\rm fil}_{{D}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{\cong}\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})};\ \ \overline{C}\colon\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})}\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm fil}_{{D}/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}.

(5)

{\rm Ker}(F^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U})=V({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{U})
.

(6)

{\rm Ker}(dV^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{U})=F^{m}({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{U})=Z_{m}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.

(7)

{\rm Ker}(V\colon{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{U})=dV^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})
.

(8)

{\rm Ker}(V^{m}\colon{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{U})=F^{m}dV({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})=B_{m}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.

(9)

If
D+E\geq 0
, there exists a distinguished triangle

W_{1}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}\xrightarrow{V^{m-1}}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}\xrightarrow{R}W_{m-1}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D/p}}\xrightarrow{+}W_{1}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}[1]

in the derived category of sheaves of abelian groups on

X_{\rm zar}
.

(10)

\varinjlim\limits_{D\geq-E}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\left(j_{*}Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{1-C}j_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\right)={\bf R}j_{*}(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}}).

(11)

{\rm Ker}(dV^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}/V^{m-1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U})=F^{m-1}({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})
.

(12)

{\rm Ker}(V^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}/dV^{m-1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})=F^{m}dV({\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})
.

(13)

j^{*}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D})
is quasi-isomorphic to

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}}
.

(14)

The inclusion
{\rm fil}_{-E}W_{m}\Omega^{d}_{U}\hookrightarrow W_{m}\Omega^{d}_{X}
is a bijection if

d
is the rank of
\Omega^{1}_{X}
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
:

The first three items follow directly from Lemma
3.10

and Theorem
6.19
(2). If
f\colon(X^{\prime},E^{\prime})\to(X,E)
is a morphism
between two snc-pairs (cf. §
2.1
), then it is clear from the
construction of
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
that the pull-back map

f^{*}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X\setminus E}\to f_{*}(W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X^{\prime}\setminus E^{\prime}})
restricts
to a map
f^{*}\colon{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X\setminus E}\to f_{*}({\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X^{\prime}\setminus E^{\prime}})
whenever
f^{*}(D)\leq D^{\prime}
.
∎

7.
Kato’s filtration via filtered de Rham-Witt

The goal of this section is to present Kato’s ramification filtration

[
Kato-89
]
of the
p
-adic étale cohomologies of henselian discrete valuation
fields in terms of the hypercohomology of the complexes

W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}
. This will extend
[
Kato-89
, Thm. 3.2]
to

H^{q}(K)\{p\}
for all
q\geq 1
.
We let
k={\mathbb{F}}_{p}
and let
{{\rm{\bf{Et}}}}_{k}
denote the big étale
site of the category of all
k
-schemes. For a local
k
-algebra
R
, we let

K^{M}_{*}(R)
denote the Milnor
K
-theory of
R
. We let
{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{\star,(-)}
denote the
Milnor
K
-theory sheaf on
{{\rm{\bf{Et}}}}_{k}
. In this paper, all Milnor
K
-groups and
Milnor
K
-theory sheaves will be the one introduced in
[
Kerz-JAG
]
(cf.
§
6.2
).

7.1.
Cohomology of
W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}
and Kato filtration

We begin by setting up our notations.
Given any positive integer
n
prime to
p
,
and any integer
q
, we let
{{\mathbb{Z}}}/n(q)
denote the classical étale sheaf
of
n
-th roots of unity
\mu_{n}
and its (positive and negative) powers on
{{\rm{\bf{Et}}}}_{k}

(cf.
[
Milne-EC
, p. 163]
).
For
X\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{k}
and
q,m\geq 1
, we let
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}
be the image of the
map of étale sheaves
{\rm dlog}\colon({\mathcal{O}}^{\times}_{X})^{q}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}
, given by

{\rm dlog}(\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{q}\})={\rm dlog}[x_{1}]_{m}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}[x_{q}]_{m}
(cf.
[
Illusie
, Chap. I, § 5.7]
).
This map uniquely factors through the composite quotient map

({\mathcal{O}}^{\times}_{X})^{q}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}\twoheadrightarrow{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}/{p^{m}}

(cf.
[
Morrow-ENS
, § 1.2]
,
[
Zhao
, Lem. 3.2.8]
).
We shall denote the resulting map
{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}/{p^{m}}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}
also by

{\rm dlog}
. This map is bijective if
X
is regular (cf.
[
Morrow-ENS
, Thm. 5.1]
).
We let
W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{{\operatorname{log}}}={{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}
and
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{{\operatorname{log}}}=0
if

m\leq 0
.
For
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
n=p^{m}r\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
(p,r)=1
, we let

{{\mathbb{Z}}}/n(q):={{\mathbb{Z}}}/r(q)\oplus W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(-,{\operatorname{log}})}[-q]

and consider it as a complex of sheaves on
{{\rm{\bf{Et}}}}_{k}
.
We let
H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}/n(j)):={\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}/n(j))
.

If
r^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
r\mid r^{\prime}
and
p\nmid r^{\prime}
, we have a natural map

{\mathbb{Z}}/r(q)\to{\mathbb{Z}}/r^{\prime}(q)
, induced by the maps
{{\mathbb{Z}}}/r\cong\frac{1}{r}{\mathbb{Z}}/{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}{\mathbb{Z}}/{\mathbb{Z}}\cong{\mathbb{Z}}/r^{\prime}
and
\mu_{r}\hookrightarrow\mu_{r^{\prime}}
. On the other hand, the
map
\overline{p}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(-,{\operatorname{log}})}\to W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{(-,{\operatorname{log}})}

(cf. Lemma
6.15
) induces a
canonical map
{\mathbb{Z}}/p^{m}(q)\to{\mathbb{Z}}/p^{m+1}(q)
. As a result, we have canonical maps

{\mathbb{Z}}/n(q)\to{\mathbb{Z}}/n^{\prime}(q)
, whenever
n\mid n^{\prime}
. In particular,

\{{\mathbb{Z}}/n(q)\}_{n\geq 1}
form an ind-sheaf on
{{\rm{\bf{Et}}}}_{k}
for any given
q\geq 0
.
Given
X\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}_{k}
, we let
H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}(j))=\varinjlim_{n}H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}/n(j))
and
H^{q}(X)=H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}(q-1))
so that

H^{q}(X)\{p\}=H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(q-1))
. We let

H^{q}_{n}(X)=H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{n}(q-1))
so that

H^{q}_{p^{m}}(X)=H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})
.
We write
H^{q}_{n}(X)
(resp.
H^{q}(X)
)
as
H^{q}_{n}(A)
(resp.
H^{q}(A)
) if
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
.
We shall use the following result a few times in this text.

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume that
X={\rm Spec\,}(R)
with
R
regular local.
Let
{\mathfrak{m}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
be the maximal ideal of
R
and let
\pi=x_{1}\cdots x_{r}
for some
1\leq r\leq d
. We let
X_{\pi}={\rm Spec\,}(A[1/{\pi}])
.
Then the canonical map

H^{q}_{p^{m}}(Z)\to H^{q}(Z)
is injective and
identifies
H^{q}_{p^{m}}(Z)
as
H^{q}(Z)[p^{m}]
for
Z\in\{X,X_{\pi}\}
.
If
X
is any regular
k
-scheme, then
H^{1}_{p^{m}}(X)=H^{1}(X)[p^{m}]
.
If
X
is any regular
k
-scheme with

{\rm Pic}(X)[p]=0
, then
H^{2}_{p^{m}}(X)=H^{2}(X)[p^{m}]
.

Proof.

Recall that we have an exact sequence
of étale sheaves (cf.
[
Geisser-Levine
, Thm. 8.1]
)

(7.1)

0\to{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}/{p^{m}}\to{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}/{p^{m+n}}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{n}}{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}/{p^{n}}\to 0

for every
n\geq 1
and
q\geq 0
, where
\alpha_{n}
is such that the composition

{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}/{p^{m+n}}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{n}}{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}/{p^{n}}\cong\frac{{p^{m}}{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}}{p^{m+n}{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}\hookrightarrow{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,X}}/{p^{m+n}}
is multiplication by
p^{m}
.
It suffices therefore to show that the canonical map

H^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(Z,{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z}}/{p^{m+n}})\to H^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(Z,{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{q,Z}}/{p^{n}})
is surjective for
q\geq 0
if
X
is local and

Z\in\{X,X_{\pi}\}
. But this follows from Lemma
6.16
.

Since the map
H^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{0,X}}/{p^{m+n}})\to H^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathcal{K}}^{M}_{0,X}}/{p^{n}})
is always surjective, we get

H^{1}_{p^{m}}(X)=H^{1}_{m+n}(X)[p^{m}]
for any regular
k
-scheme
X
.
For
q=2
, the claim follows from our assumption using the exact sequence

0\to{{\rm Pic}(X)}/{p^{m}}\to H^{2}_{p^{m}}(X)\to{\operatorname{Br}}(X)[p^{m}]\to 0.

∎

If
R
is a regular local ring, the exact sequence

(7.2)

0\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{{(-)},{\operatorname{log}}}\to Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(-)}\xrightarrow{1-C}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(-)}\to 0

of sheaves on
{\rm Spec\,}(R)_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}
yields an exact sequence

(7.3)

0\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{R,{\operatorname{log}}}\to Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{R}\xrightarrow{1-C}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{R}\xrightarrow{\delta^{q}_{m}}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(R)\to 0.

We also have a commutative diagram (cf.
[
Kato-89
, § 1.3]
)

(7.4)

\textstyle{{K^{M}_{q}(R)}/{p^{m}}\otimes W_{m}\Omega^{q^{\prime}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes\delta^{q^{\prime}}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{q+q^{\prime}}_{p^{m}}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{q+q^{\prime}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{q+q^{\prime}}_{m}}
\textstyle{{K^{M}_{q}(R)}/{p^{m}}\otimes H^{q^{\prime}+1}_{p^{m}}(R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{H^{q+q^{\prime}+1}_{p^{m}}(R),}

where the bottom row is induced by the Bloch-Kato map

\beta^{q}_{R}\colon K^{M}_{q}(R)\to H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(R,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}(q))
,
followed by the cup product on the étale cohomology of

W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(-),{\operatorname{log}}}

on
{\rm Spec\,}(R)
. The top row is induced by the dlog map, followed by the wedge
product. We shall denote the composite map

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{R}\xrightarrow{\delta^{q}_{m}}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(R)\hookrightarrow H^{q+1}(R)

also by
\delta^{q}_{m}
.

The set-up of §
7
:

From now on until the end of §
7
, we fix an
F
-finite henselian
discrete valuation ring
A
containing
k
and
let
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\pi)
denote the maximal ideal of
A
. Let
{\mathfrak{f}}={A}/{{\mathfrak{m}}}
and

K=Q(A)
. We let
X={\rm Spec\,}(A),\ D_{n}=V((\pi^{n}))
and

U={\rm Spec\,}(K)
.
We fix integers
n,q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, and let
T^{m,q}_{n}(K)
denote the
cokernel of the map
(1-C)\colon Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.

Lemma 7.2
.

We have

(1)

H^{i}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(X,Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D_{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})=H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\rm fil}_{D_{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U})=0
for
i\geq 1
.

(2)

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D_{n}}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}K^{M}_{q}(K)/p^{m}&\mbox{if $i=0$}\\
T^{m,q}_{n}(K)&\mbox{if $i=1$}\\
0&\mbox{if $i>1$.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

The item (1) is clear since the underlying sheaves are quasi-coherent
sheaves of
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules.
To prove (2), we look at the hypercohomology exact sequence

(7.5)

0\to{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D_{n}}})\to Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{{n}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D_{n}}})\xrightarrow{}0,

where
{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}({\mathcal{E}})
(resp.
H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}({\mathcal{E}})
) is the shorthand notation for

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{E}})
(resp.
H^{i}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{E}})
) and the last term of
(
7.5
) is

0
by (1). Since
W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\subset{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{cl}{\rm dlog}\colon K^{M}_{q}(A)/p^{m}&\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm Ker}(1-C:Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{A})\\
&\subset{\rm Ker}(1-C:Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})={\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q}_{X|{D_{n}}})\\
&\subset{\rm Ker}(1-C:Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})={K^{M}_{q}(K)}/p^{m}.\end{array}

On the other hand, as
A
is a DVR,
{\rm dlog}(K^{M}_{q}(K)/p^{m})\subset Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is generated as a group by elements of the form

(1)

{\rm dlog}([a_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([a_{q}]_{m})
and

(2)

{\rm dlog}([a_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([a_{q-1}]_{m})\wedge{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})
,

where
a_{i}\in A^{\star}
. However, the elements of the form (1) and (2) also lie
within
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{0}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
for
n\geq 0
.
Since such elements clearly lie in
{\rm Ker}(1-C\colon Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})
, we conclude that
K^{M}_{q}(K)/p^{m}\cong{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q}_{X|{D_{n}}})
and

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q}_{X|{D_{n}}})={\rm Coker}(1-C:Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})=T^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.
The remaining claim follows from item (1).

∎

By Lemma
7.2
, we get an exact sequence

(7.6)

0\to{K^{M}_{q}(K)}/p^{m}\to Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{\delta^{m,q}_{n}}T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to 0.

This sequence maps canonically to the exact sequence (
7.3
) with

R=K
and hence induces a map
\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}\colon T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K)
.
We shall denote the composite map
T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K)\hookrightarrow H^{q+1}(K)
also
by
\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}
.

By Lemma
3.10
(see Step 5 for
D=0
,
D^{\prime}=D_{n}
), we see that the product map
on the top row of (
7.4
) (with
R=K
) restricts to
a map

(7.7)

K^{M}_{q}(K)/p^{m}\otimes{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q^{\prime}}_{K}\xrightarrow{\cup}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q+q^{\prime}}_{K}.

It follows from Lemmas
6.12
and
6.14

that the composite map

\partial_{n}^{m,q}:K^{M}_{q}(K)\otimes{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)\xrightarrow{\cup}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{\delta^{m,q}_{n}}T^{m,q}_{n}(K)

is surjective. We therefore get the following.

Corollary 7.3
.

The map

\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}\colon T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to{\rm Image}({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)\otimes K^{M}_{q}(K)\xrightarrow{\lambda^{q}_{m}}H^{q+1}(K))

is a bijection.

Proof.

We only need to show that the map

T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K)
is injective. But this follows from
Proposition
6.2
.
∎

We now recall Kato’s filtration on
H^{q}(K)
from
[
Kato-89
, § 2]
. For any

A
-algebra
R
, let
R^{h}
denote the henselization of
R
with respect to the
ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}R
. We let

{\rm Spec\,}({R}/{{\mathfrak{m}}R})\xrightarrow{\iota}{\rm Spec\,}(R)\xleftarrow{j}{\rm Spec\,}(R[\pi^{-1}])

be the inclusions and let

(7.8)

V^{q}_{n}(R)={\mathbb{H}}^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}({R}/{{\mathfrak{m}}R},\iota^{*}{\bf R}j_{*}({{\mathbb{Z}}}/{n}(q-1)))\xrightarrow{\cong}V^{q}_{n}(R^{h})\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(R^{h}[\pi^{-1}],{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{n}(q-1))

\mbox{and}\ V^{q}(R)={\varinjlim}_{n}V^{q}_{n}(R)\cong V^{q}(R^{h})\cong H^{q}(R^{h}[\pi^{-1}]).

The following definition is due to Kato
[
Kato-89
, Defn. 2.1]
.

Definition 7.4
.

For
n,q\geq 0
, we let
{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)
be the set of all elements

\chi\in H^{q+1}(K)
such that
\{\chi,1+\pi^{n+1}T\}:=\sigma_{n,q}(\chi\otimes(1+\pi^{n+1}T))=0
in
V^{q+2}(A[T])

under the composite map
\sigma_{n,q}
:

H^{q+1}(K)\otimes((A[T])^{h}[\pi^{-1}])^{\times}\to H^{q+1}(K)\otimes H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}((A[T])^{h}[\pi^{-1}],{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1))\xrightarrow{\cup}V^{q+2}(A[T]).

We let
{\rm gr}^{{\rm bk}}_{0}H^{q+1}(K)={\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{0}H^{q+1}(K)
and

{\rm gr}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)={{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)}/{{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n-1}H^{q+1}(K)}
if
n\geq 1
.

The following result of Kato (cf.
[
Kato-89
, Thm. 3.2]
)
provides a description of
{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)
in terms of Milnor
K
-theory and
Witt-vectors.

Theorem 7.5
.

One has the following.

(1)

{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{1}(K)\{p\}={\underset{m\geq 1}{\bigcup}}\delta^{0}_{m}({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K))
.

(2)

{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)\{p\}
coincides with the image of

K^{M}_{q}(K)\otimes{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{1}(K)\{p\}\xrightarrow{\cup}H^{q+1}(K).

In
[
Kerz-Saito-Eratum
]
, Kerz-Saito proved the following refined version
of Theorem
7.5
(1).

Theorem 7.6
.

One has
{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{1}(K)[p^{m}]=\delta^{0}_{m}({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K))
.

The theorems of Kato and Kerz-Saito provide a simple description of
Kato’s ramification filtration on
H^{1}(K)[p^{m}]
in terms of Witt-vectors.
The objective of this section is to extend this description to all
q\geq 0
.

7.2.
The
V
-operator on
T^{\star,q}_{n}(K)

We fix
n,q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
as
before. When
n>0
, we shall write
n=p^{r}l
, where
r\geq 0
and
p\nmid l
.
We let
S^{m,q}_{n}(K)={\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)[p^{m}]
. Since
T^{m,q}_{n}(K)

is a
p^{m}
-torsion group, we already deduce from
Corollary
7.3
, Theorem
7.5
and (
7.4
) that the map

\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}
has a factorization

\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}\colon T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\hookrightarrow S^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.
In the next few lemmas, we shall study the map
V\colon T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to T^{m+1,q}_{n}(K)

and estimate its cokernel in terms of the differential forms on
{\mathfrak{f}}
.
We begin with the following.

Lemma 7.7
.

There is a commutative diagram

(7.9)

\textstyle{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1-C}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1-C}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}.}

Proof.

By Lemmas
3.10
and
6.1
,
we only need to show that
VC=CV
on
Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}

and
V(Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})\subset Z_{1}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
For showing the first claim, it is enough to
show that
VCF=CVF
. But this is clear because

VCF=VR=RV=CFV=CVF
.
For the second claim, note that
\omega\in Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
implies

F^{m-1}d(\omega)=0
. This implies in turn that
F^{m}dV(\omega)=F^{m-1}d(\omega)=0
. That is,
V(\omega)\in Z_{1}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
∎

Using Lemma
7.7
, we get a commutative diagram
(cf.
[
Kato-89
, § 1.3]
)

(7.10)

\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{m,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{S^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{\rm can}}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{m+1,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{T^{m+1,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\delta}^{m+1,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{S^{m+1,q}_{n}(K),}

where the middle
V
is the unique map induced by the
commutative diagram (
7.9
), and the right vertical arrow
is the canonical inclusion
{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)[p^{m}]\hookrightarrow{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)[p^{m+1}]
. Since the canonical inclusion

H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K)\hookrightarrow H^{q+1}_{p^{m+1}}(K)
(cf. Lemma
7.1
) is induced by

\overline{p}=V\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{(-,{\operatorname{log}})}\to W_{m+1}\Omega^{q}_{(-,{\operatorname{log}})}
, we see that the
right square of (
7.10
) is commutative.

It follows from the commutative diagram (
7.16
)
that the map
T^{m,q}_{n-1}(K)\to T^{m,q}_{n}(K)
is injective
for every
n\geq 1
.
We let
\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)=\frac{T^{m,q}_{n}(K)}{T^{m,q}_{n-1}(K)}
.
Since the commutative diagram (
7.9
) is compatible with
the change in values of
n\geq 0
, it induces maps

V\colon\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to\widetilde{T}^{m+1,q}_{n}(K)
as
n
varies over all
positive integers. We let
\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)=\frac{\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)}{V(\widetilde{T}^{m-1,q}_{n}(K))}
.

In the rest of the discussion, we shall assume
n\geq 1
unless stated otherwise.

Lemma 7.8
.

The map
V\colon\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to\widetilde{T}^{m+1,q}_{n}(K)
is surjective for every

m\geq r+1
.

Proof.

Since
V(a{\rm dlog}([x_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([x_{q}]_{m})=V(a){\rm dlog}([x_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([x_{q}]_{m})
,
(
7.9
) implies that the diagram

(7.11)

\textstyle{K^{M}_{q}(K)\otimes{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{m,q}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes V}
\textstyle{T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{K^{M}_{q}(K)\otimes{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{m+1,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{T^{m+1,q}_{n}(K)}

is commutative. Furthermore, the horizontal arrows are surjective by
Corollary
7.3
. It suffices therefore to show that

\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}(K)}{{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m+1}(K)+V({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K))}=0
for

m\geq r+1
.

To that end, we look at the commutative diagram of exact sequences (cf.
Lemma
3.2
)

(7.12)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m+1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R^{m}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\lfloor{{(n-1)}/{p^{m}}}\rfloor}W_{1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R^{m}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{\lfloor{{n}/{p^{m}}}\rfloor}W_{1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since
m\geq r+1
, we see that
\lfloor{{(n-1)}/{p^{m}}}\rfloor=\lfloor{{n}/{p^{m}}-{1}/{p^{m}}}\rfloor=\lfloor{{n}/{p^{m}}}\rfloor
.
This implies that the right vertical arrow in (
7.12
) is
bijective and proves what we had asserted.
∎

For the next three lemmas, we assume that
1\leq m\leq r
and consider the map

\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\colon A\otimes{\underset{(q\ \mbox{times})}{A^{\times}\otimes\cdots\otimes A^{\times}}}\to\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K);

\widetilde{\theta}_{1}(x\otimes y_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes y_{q})=\delta^{m,q}_{n}([x\pi^{-np^{1-m}}]_{m}{\rm dlog}([y_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([y_{q}]_{m}))\text{ mod }T^{m,q}_{n-1}(K).

This is defined because
np^{1-m}=p^{r+1-m}l\in{\mathbb{N}}
and

[x\pi^{-np^{1-m}}]_{m}\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)
.

Lemma 7.9
.

\widetilde{\theta}_{1}
descends to a group homomorphism

\theta^{q}_{1}\colon\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\to\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.

Proof.

For
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{q}\}\subset A^{\times}
, we write

{\rm dlog}([y_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([y_{q}]_{m})={\rm dlog}(\underline{y})
.
If
x=a\pi
for some
a\in A
, then

\delta^{m,q}_{n}([x]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y}))=\delta^{m,q}_{n}([a\pi^{-(n-p^{m-1})p^{1-m}}]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y}))\in T^{m,q}_{n-1}(K)
,
as
[a\pi^{-(n-p^{m-1})p^{1-m}}]_{m}\in{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}(K)
.
Suppose next that
y_{i}=1+a\pi
for some
a\in A
and
1\leq i\leq q
.
By
[
Kato-89
, Lem. 3.5]
, we see that
as an element of
{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{K}
,
[x]_{m}{\rm dlog}([1+a\pi]_{m})
actually lies
in
{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{K}
. It follows that

[x]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})\in{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and hence

\delta^{m,q}_{n}([x]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y}))
dies in
\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.
We have thus shown that
\widetilde{\theta}_{1}
descends a group homomorphism

(7.13)

\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\colon{\mathfrak{f}}\otimes{\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}\to\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K).

We now let
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{l}\}
and
\{x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{l^{\prime}}\}
be two sets of
elements of

A^{\times}
such that
\sum_{i}x_{i}-\sum_{j}x^{\prime}_{j}=a\pi
for some
a\in A
.
Let
y_{1},\ldots,y_{q-1}\in A^{\times}
. To show that
\widetilde{\theta}_{1}
factors through

\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
, it suffices to show, using
[
Bloch-Kato
, Lem. 4.2]
, that

\widetilde{\theta}_{1}
kills elements of the form

(\sum_{i}x_{i}\otimes x_{i}-\sum_{j}x^{\prime}_{i}\otimes x^{\prime}_{i})\otimes y_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes y_{q-1}.

To prove this claim, we let
w_{0}=[\pi^{-np^{1-m}}]_{m}{\rm dlog}[y_{1}]_{m}\cdots{\rm dlog}[y_{q-1}]_{m}
. In
{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
, we then get the
following:

w_{0}\left(\sum_{i}[x_{i}]_{m}{\rm dlog}[x_{i}]_{m}-\sum_{j}[x^{\prime}_{i}]_{m}{\rm dlog}[x^{\prime}_{j}]_{m}\right)=w_{0}d\left(\sum_{i}[x_{i}]_{m}-\sum_{j}[x^{\prime}_{j}]_{m}\right)

=w_{0}d\left(\left[\sum_{i}x_{i}-\sum_{j}x^{\prime}_{i}\right]_{m}+V(\underline{b})\right)=w_{0}d[a\pi]_{m}+{w_{0}}dV(\underline{b})

for some
\underline{b}=(a_{m-2},\ldots,a_{1},a_{0})\in W_{m-1}(A)
.

Since
w_{0}\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
, we get that
[a\pi]_{m}w_{0}\in{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}
, and hence
w_{0}d[a\pi]_{m}=[a\pi]_{m}w_{0}{\rm dlog}[a\pi]_{m}\in{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
. So, it dies in
\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
. On the other hand,

w_{0}dV(\underline{b})=dV(F(w_{0})\underline{b})-V(Fd(w_{0})\underline{b})\in(1-C)({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K})+V({\rm fil}_{n}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K})

by Lemmas
3.10
and
6.12
and hence also dies in

\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
. This proves the claim and concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

An identical proof shows that
\widetilde{\phi}\colon A\otimes A^{\times}\otimes\cdots\otimes A^{\times}\to\widetilde{T}^{r+1,q}_{n}(K)
, given by

\widetilde{\phi}(x\otimes y_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes y_{q})=\delta^{m,q}_{n}([x\pi^{-l}]_{m}{\rm dlog}([y_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([y_{q}]_{m}))

mod
T^{r+1,q}_{n-1}(K)
, induces a homomorphism

(7.14)

\phi^{q}\colon\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\to\widetilde{M}^{r+1,q}_{n}(K).

Lemma 7.10
.

\theta^{q}_{1}(Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}})=0
.

Proof.

By
[
Kato-89
, § 3, p. 111]
,
Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
is generated by the elements of
the types

(1)

x{\rm dlog}(x)\wedge{\rm dlog}(y_{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}(y_{q-1})
and

(2)

x^{p}{\rm dlog}(y_{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}(y_{q})
,

where
x,y_{j}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{\star},1\leq j\leq q
.
We now let
x,y_{1},\ldots y_{q}\in A^{\times},\ w=x^{p}\pi^{-np^{1-m}}
and

{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})_{l}={\rm dlog}([y_{1}]_{l})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([y_{q}]_{l})
. Since
m-1<r
and
p^{r}|n
, we can write

w=(x\pi^{-(n/p)p^{1-m}})^{p}
.

Letting
w^{\prime}=x\pi^{-(n/p)p^{1-m}}
,
we get by Lemma
6.3
(1) that

[w^{\prime}]_{m+1}\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m+1}(K)\text{ and }[w]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})_{m}=F([w^{\prime}]_{m+1}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})_{m+1})\in Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}.

In particular,

\delta^{m,0}_{n}\circ(1-C)([w]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})_{m})=0
in
T^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.
From this, we get

\delta^{m,q}_{n}([w]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})_{m})=\delta^{m,q}_{n}\circ C([w]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})_{m})=\delta^{m,q}_{n}([w^{\prime}]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})_{m}).

Since the last term is in
{\rm fil}_{n/p}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\subset{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
,
it follows that
\delta^{m,q}_{n}([w]_{m}{\rm dlog}(\underline{y})_{m})
dies in
\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.
This shows that the type (2) elements go to zero under
\theta_{1}^{q}
.

Next, we let
a=\pi^{-np^{1-m}}
and
w_{l}^{\prime\prime}={\rm dlog}([y_{1}]_{l})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([y_{q-1}]_{l})
so that

[xa]_{m}{\rm dlog}([x]_{m})w_{m}^{\prime\prime}=[a]_{m}d([x]_{m})w_{m}^{\prime\prime}=d([xa]_{m}w_{m}^{\prime\prime})-[x]_{m}d([a]_{m})w_{m}^{\prime\prime}.

Lemma
6.12
implies
\delta^{m,q}_{n}([xa]_{m}{\rm dlog}([x]_{m})w^{\prime\prime}_{m})=-\delta^{m,q}_{n}([x]_{m}d([a]_{m})w^{\prime\prime}_{m})
. On the other hand,

\begin{array}[]{lll}[x]_{m}d([a]_{m})w^{\prime\prime}_{m}&=&[x]_{m}w^{\prime\prime}_{m}(-{n}/{p^{m-1}})[a]_{m}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m}\\
&=&p(-{n}/{p^{m}})[xa]_{m}w^{\prime\prime}_{m}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m}\\
&=&(-{n}/{p^{m}})VF([xa]_{m}w^{\prime\prime}_{m}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m}).\end{array}

Since

F([xa]_{m}w^{\prime\prime}{\rm dlog}[\pi]_{m})\in{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
,
we conclude that
\delta^{m,q}_{n}([x]_{m}d([a]_{m})w^{\prime\prime})
dies in

\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
. Hence, so does
\delta^{m,q}_{n}([xa]_{m}{\rm dlog}([x]_{m})w^{\prime\prime})

(image of the elements of type (1)).
This concludes the proof.
∎

We next consider the map

\widetilde{\theta}_{2}\colon A\otimes{\underset{(q-1\ \mbox{times})}{A^{\times}\otimes\cdots\otimes A^{\times}}}\to\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K);

\widetilde{\theta}_{2}(x\otimes y_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes y_{q-1})=\delta^{m,q}_{n}([x\pi^{-np^{1-m}}]_{m}{\rm dlog}([y_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([y_{q-1}]_{m})\wedge{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m}))

\hskip 284.52756pt\text{ mod }T^{m,q}_{n-1}(K).

Lemma 7.11
.

\widetilde{\theta}_{2}
descends to a group homomorphism

\theta^{q}_{2}\colon\frac{\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}{Z_{1}\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}\to\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.10
, we see that multiplication by
{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})

preserves
{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}
. Since
F^{m-1}d(a{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})=F^{m-1}d(a){\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})
and
C(a{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m}))=C(a){\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})
for

a\in Z_{1}W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{K}
,
it follows that the map
W_{m}\Omega^{q-1}_{K}\xrightarrow{{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}
induces maps

T^{m,q-1}_{n}(K)\xrightarrow{{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})}T^{m,q}_{n}(K),\ \widetilde{T}^{m,q-1}_{n}(K)\xrightarrow{{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})}\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K).

Since
V(a{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m}))=V(a){\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})
, we get that these maps
also induce

\widetilde{M}^{m,q-1}_{n}(K)\xrightarrow{{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})}\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K).

Since
{\theta}^{q}_{2}={\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})\circ{\theta}^{q-1}_{1}={\theta}^{q}_{1}\circ{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})
, we are done by Lemma
7.10
.
∎

Combining the previous three lemmas, we get a homomorphism

(7.15)

(\theta^{q}_{1},\theta^{q}_{2})\colon\frac{\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}{Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}\bigoplus\frac{\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}{Z_{1}\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}\to\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ \ {\rm for}\ \ 1\leq m\leq r.

Lemma 7.12
.

We have the following.

(1)

The map
\phi^{q}\colon\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\to\widetilde{M}^{r+1,q}_{n}(K)
is surjective.

(2)

For
1\leq m\leq r
, the map
(\theta^{q}_{1},\theta^{q}_{2})

in (
7.15
) is surjective.

Proof.

The map
\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)}{{\rm fil}_{n-1}W_{m}(K)}\otimes K^{M}_{q}(K)\to\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
is surjective by Corollary
7.3
, and

{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)=[\pi^{-\alpha}]_{m}W_{m}(A)
by Lemma
3.6
(1),
where
\alpha=np^{1-m}
if
1\leq m\leq r
, and
\alpha=l
if
m=r+1
.
Using Lemmas
6.12
and
6.14
, it only remains to show for
proving (1) that we can replace
K^{M}_{q}(K)
by
K^{M}_{q}(A)
on the left hand side of
this surjection.
To show the latter statement, it is enough to consider the case
q=1
and to show that

[x\pi^{-l}]_{r+1}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{r+1})
lies in the image of
A\otimes A^{\times}

under
\phi^{1}
if
x\in A^{\times}
. But we know that

[x\pi^{-l}]_{r+1}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{r+1})=-l^{-1}[x]_{r+1}d([\pi^{-l}]_{r+1})
. On the other hand,

[x]_{r+1}d([\pi^{-l}]_{r+1})=d([x\pi^{-l}]_{r+1})-[x\pi^{-l}]_{r+1}{\rm dlog}([x]_{r+1})
.
It follows from this that
\delta^{r+1,1}_{n}([x\pi^{-l}]_{r+1}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{r+1}))

=\phi^{1}(l^{-1}x\otimes x)
.

To prove (2), we can repeat the above steps which reduces finally to showing that

\delta^{m,1}_{n}([x\pi^{-\alpha}]_{m}{\rm dlog}([y]_{m}))
lies in the image of

(\theta^{1}_{1},\theta^{1}_{2})
, where
y\in K^{\times}
. To show this, we write

y=u\pi^{\beta}
for some
u\in A^{\times}
and
\beta\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. This yields

[x\pi^{-\alpha}]_{m}{\rm dlog}([y]_{m})=[x\pi^{-\alpha}]_{m}{\rm dlog}([u]_{m})+\beta[x\pi^{-\alpha}]_{m}{\rm dlog}([\pi]_{m})=\theta^{1}_{1}(x\otimes u)+\theta^{1}_{2}(\beta x)
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

7.3.
The main theorem

We shall now prove the main result of this section which extends

[
Kato-89
, Thm. 3.2]
to
H^{q+1}(K)\{p\}
for all
q\geq 1
.
We shall assume until Theorem
7.15
that
n\geq 1
.
We look at the commutative diagram

(7.16)

\textstyle{T^{m,q}_{n-1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n-1}}
\textstyle{T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{S^{m,q}_{n-1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{m,q}_{n}(K).}

We let
\widetilde{S}^{m,q}_{n}(K)=\frac{S^{m,q}_{n}(K)}{S^{m,q}_{n-1}(K)}
and let

\widetilde{N}^{m,q}_{n}(K)=\frac{\widetilde{S}^{m,q}_{n}(K)}{\widetilde{S}^{m-1,q}_{n}(K)}
. We let

\psi^{m,q}_{n}\colon\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to\widetilde{S}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
be the map induced
by
\{\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
. It is clear that
\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}

descends to a homomorphism
\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}\colon\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to\widetilde{N}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.

Lemma 7.13
.

For
m\geq 1
, the map

\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}\colon\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to\widetilde{N}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
is bijective.

Proof.

When
m\geq r+2
, we are done by Lemma
7.8
and

[
Kato-89
, Lem. 3.6.1]
as both groups are zero. Otherwise, we
look at the maps

\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\xrightarrow{\phi^{q}}\widetilde{M}^{r+1,q}_{n}(K)\xrightarrow{\overline{\delta}^{r+1,q}_{n}}\widetilde{N}^{r+1,q}_{n}(K)

and (when
1\leq m\leq r
)

\frac{\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}{Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}\bigoplus\frac{\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}{Z_{1}\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}\xrightarrow{\theta^{q}_{1},\theta^{q}_{2}}\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\xrightarrow{\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}}\widetilde{N}^{m,q}_{n}(K).

Kato shows in
[
Kato-89
, § 3]
(see the last step in proof of Theorem 3.2
on p. 114) that the above two composite maps are bijective.
We now apply Lemma
7.12
to finish the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.14
.

For
m\geq 1
, the square (
7.16
) is Cartesian.

Proof.

Since
T^{m,q}_{n-1}(K)\to T^{m,q}_{n}(K)
is injective
(cf. diagram (
7.16
)), it is sufficient to show that the map

\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}\colon\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to\widetilde{S}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
is injective.
We shall prove this by induction on
m
.
When
m=1
, the map
\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
is bijective, and
we are done by Lemma
7.13
.
We now assume
m\geq 2
and
look at the commutative diagram

(7.17)

\textstyle{\widetilde{T}^{m-1,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\delta}^{m-1,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{T}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{M}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{S}^{m-1,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{S}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{N}^{m,q}_{n}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

It is clear from the definition of the groups
{S}^{m,q}_{n}(K)
that

S^{m-1,q}_{n}(K)\bigcap S^{m,q}_{n-1}(K)=S^{m-1,q}_{n-1}(K)
, and this implies that
the bottom row of (
7.17
) is exact.
The left vertical arrow in this diagram is injective by induction, and the
right vertical arrow is bijective by Lemma
7.13
.
It follows that the middle vertical arrow is injective.
∎

We can now finally prove the following main result of §
7
.
Recall that
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
and
D_{n}=V((\pi^{n}))
.

Theorem 7.15
.

For
m\geq 1
and
n,q\geq 0
, the map

\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}\colon T^{m,q}_{n}(K)\to{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)[p^{m}]

is an isomorphism, where
T^{m,q}_{n}(K)={\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D_{n}}})
.

Proof.

By Corollary
7.3
, we only need to show that
\overline{\delta}^{m,q}_{n}
is surjective.
For this, we let
w\in{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)[p^{m}]=S^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.
As
H^{q+1}(K)[p^{m}]=H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K,{\operatorname{log}}})

by Lemma
7.1
, we have that
w\in H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K,{\operatorname{log}}})
.
Since
\delta^{m,q}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\twoheadrightarrow H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K,{\operatorname{log}}})
, it
follows by Lemma
3.6
(4)
that
{\varinjlim}_{n}T^{m,q}_{n}(K)

=H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(K,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K,{\operatorname{log}}})
.
In particular,
w\in T^{m,q}_{n^{\prime}}(K)\bigcap S^{m,q}_{n}(K)
for all
n^{\prime}\gg 0
.
We conclude from Lemma
7.14
that
w\in T^{m,q}_{n}(K)
.
∎

The following easy consequence of Theorem
7.15
will be used in the
next section.

Corollary 7.16
.

For
m\geq 1
and
n,q\geq 0
, the canonical map

\frac{H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K)}{{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)[p^{m}]}\to\frac{H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(\widehat{K})}{{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(\widehat{K})[p^{m}]}

is bijective.

Proof.

By Theorem
7.15
, the term on the left hand side is the étale
cohomology with support
{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D_{n}}})
at the closed
point of
X
. Similarly, the term on the right hand side is

{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}(\widehat{X},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{{\widehat{X}}|{D_{n}}})
, where
\widehat{X}={\rm Spec\,}(\widehat{A})
.
By comparing the long exact cohomology sequences associated to the complexes

W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D_{n}}}
and
W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{{\widehat{X}}|{D_{n}}}
, we see meanwhile that
the map
{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D_{n}}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}(\widehat{X},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{{\widehat{X}}|{D_{n}}})
is bijective.
∎

8.
Global Kato filtration via filtered de Rham-Witt

The goal of this section is to present Kato’s ramification filtration
of regular
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-schemes in terms of the hypercohomology of the complexes

W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}
. This will extend a result of Kerz-Saito
(cf.
[
Kerz-Saito-ANT
, § 3]
)
to
H^{q+1}(U)\{p\}
for all
q\geq 0
. The main result of this section has several
applications. Some of these will be presented in the latter sections of
this text.

We let
k={\mathbb{F}}_{p}
and let
X
be a Noetherian regular
F
-finite
k
-scheme.
Let
E
be a simple normal crossing divisor on
X

with irreducible components
E_{1},\ldots,E_{r}
. Let
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
be the
inclusion of the complement of
E
in
X
. Let
\eta_{i}
denote the generic
point of
E_{i}
and let
K_{i}
(resp.
\widehat{K}_{i}
) be the quotient field of

{\mathcal{O}}^{h}_{X,\eta_{i}}
(resp.
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}_{X,\eta_{i}}}
). Let

h_{i}\colon{\rm Spec\,}({{\mathcal{O}}^{h}_{X,\eta_{i}}})\to X
denote the canonical map.
We fix integers
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
. Let
D=\sum_{i}n_{i}E_{i}\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)

with each
n_{i}\geq 0
.
In view of Lemma
7.1
, we shall make no distinction between

H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(L,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{L,{\operatorname{log}}})
and
H^{q+1}(L)[p^{m}]
in our exposition if

L
is any
F
-finite henselian discrete valuation field containing
k
.
Recall the complex of sheaves

W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}=\left[Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\right]

on the big étale site of
X
. Recall also that

j^{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}\cong W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}}
.

8.1.
Global Kato filtration

For
1\leq i\leq r
, let
j_{i}\colon H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}})=H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)\to H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K_{i})
be the canonical restriction map.
We recall the global Kato filtration of
H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)
below.

Definition 8.1
.

(cf.
[
Kato-89
, § 7, 8]
,
[
Kerz-Saito-ANT
, Defn. 2.7]
)

We let
n\geq 1
be an integer and write
n=p^{m}r
where
m\geq 0
and

p\nmid r
. We let

{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U):={\rm Ker}\left(H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)\xrightarrow{\bigoplus j_{i}}\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}\frac{H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K_{i})}{{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n_{i}}H^{q+1}(K_{i})[p^{m}]}\right);

{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{n}(U):={\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)\bigoplus H^{q+1}_{r}(U).

{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}(U):=\varinjlim_{n}{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{n}(U).

By Corollary
7.16
, we can replace each
K_{i}
by
\widehat{K}_{i}
in the
definition of
{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)
.
Note also that each quotient on the right hand side of the definition of

{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)
makes sense by Lemma
7.1
.
It is clear that
{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}(U)={\rm Ker}\left(H^{q+1}(U)\{p\}\xrightarrow{\bigoplus j_{i}}\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}\frac{H^{q+1}(K_{i})}{{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n_{i}}H^{q+1}(K_{i})}\right)

\bigoplus H^{q+1}(U)\{p^{\prime}\}
. It is also easily checked that the canonical map

\varinjlim_{D}{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)\to H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)
is bijective.

Example 8.2
.

(1) When
q=0
, we have

(8.1)

{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{1}_{p^{m}}(U)\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm Hom}_{\rm cont}(\pi_{1}^{\rm ab}(X,D)/p^{m},{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}}),

where
\pi_{1}^{\rm ab}(X,D)
is the abelianized étale fundamental group with modulus
(
[
Kerz-Saito-ANT
, Defn. 2.7]
,
[
GK-Nis
, Defn. 2.9]
) which is the
abelianization of the automorphism group of the fiber
functor of the Galois category of finite étale covers of
U
whose
ramifications are bounded at each generic point of
E
by means of Kato’s Swan
conductor.

(2) When
q=1
, there is a canonical exact sequence

(8.2)

0\to{{\rm Pic}(U)}/{p^{m}}\to{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{2}_{p^{m}}(U)\to{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}{\operatorname{Br}}(U)[p^{m}]\to 0,

where
{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}{\operatorname{Br}}(U)
is the Brauer group with modulus (cf.
[
KRS
, Defn. 8.7]
,
where it was denoted by
{\operatorname{Br}}^{{\rm div}}(X|D)
).
It is defined as the subgroup of
{\operatorname{Br}}(U)
consisting of elements
\chi

such that for every
1\leq i\leq r
, the image of

\chi
under the canonical map
{\operatorname{Br}}(U)\to{\operatorname{Br}}(K_{i})=H^{2}(K_{i})

lies in
{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n_{i}}H^{2}(K_{i})
.
The above exact sequence is a direct consequence of the sheaf exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}_{U}\xrightarrow{p^{m}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}_{U}\xrightarrow{{\rm dlog}}W_{m}\Omega^{1}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}}\to 0.

By
[
Kerz-Saito-ANT
, Lem. 3.6]
, there is a canonical isomorphism

{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)\cong{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D})\text{ if }q=0.

Our goal in this section is to extend this isomorphism to arbitrary
q\geq 0
.
Applications of such an isomorphism will be given in the following sections.
More applications will appear in
[
KM-1
]
and
[
KM-2
]
.

8.2.
Cartier operator on local cohomology

The proof of our main theorem will be based on a local result that we shall prove
in this subsection. We begin with the following.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
{\mathfrak{f}}
be an
F
-finite field of characteristic
p
.
Let
R={\mathfrak{f}}[[Y_{1},Y_{2}]],\ K=R[(Y_{1}Y_{2})^{-1}]
and
n,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let’s write

F^{1,q}_{0}=F^{1,q}_{0}(R)=\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}{\rm dlog}Y_{1}\oplus\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}{\rm dlog}Y_{2}\oplus\Omega^{q-2}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}{\rm dlog}Y_{1}{\rm dlog}Y_{2}

and

Z_{1}F^{1,q}_{0}=Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\oplus(Z_{1}\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}){\rm dlog}Y_{1}\oplus(Z_{1}\Omega^{q-1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}){\rm dlog}Y_{2}\oplus(Z_{1}\Omega^{q-2}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}){\rm dlog}Y_{1}{\rm dlog}Y_{2}.

Then
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
has the following unique presentation.

(8.3)

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}ip\geq-n\\
jp\geq-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}\left(Z_{1}F^{1,q}_{0}\right)\ +\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-m\\
p\nmid i\text{ or }p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}F^{1,{q-1}}_{0}).

Moreover, one has

(1)

({\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})[(Y_{1}Y_{2})^{-1}]=\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})[(Y_{1}Y_{2})^{-1}]=Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, where we consider

Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
as
R
-modules via the
Frobenius action.

(2)

Let
\omega\in\Omega^{q}_{K}
. Then

(a)

\omega\in({\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})[(Y_{1})^{-1}]
iff it can be written
uniquely as

\omega=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-N_{0}\\
j\geq-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}a_{i,j},\text{ for some $N_{0}>0$ and }a_{i,j}\in F^{1,q}_{0}.

(b)

\omega\in({\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})[(Y_{2})^{-1}]
iff it can be written
uniquely as

\omega=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-M_{0}\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}b_{i,j},\text{ for some $M_{0}>0$ and }b_{i,j}\in F^{1,q}_{0}.

(c)

\omega\in(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})[(Y_{1})^{-1}]
iff it can be written
uniquely as

\omega=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-N^{\prime}_{0}\\
jp\geq-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}e_{i,j}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-N^{\prime}_{0}\\
j\geq-m\\
p\nmid i\text{ or }p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}f_{i,j}),

for some
N^{\prime}_{0}>0
and

e_{i,j}\in Z_{1}F^{1,q}_{0},\ f_{i,j}\in F^{1,{q-1}}_{0}.

(d)

\omega\in(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})[(Y_{2})^{-1}]
iff it can be written
uniquely as

\omega=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}ip\geq-n\\
j\geq-M^{\prime}_{0}\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}e^{\prime}_{i,j}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-M^{\prime}_{0}\\
p\nmid i\text{ or }p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}f^{\prime}_{i,j}),

for some
M^{\prime}_{0}>0
and
e^{\prime}_{i,j}\in Z_{1}F^{1,q}_{0},\ f^{\prime}_{i,j}\in F^{1,{q-1}}_{0}.

Proof.

It is clear from Corollary
5.2
and Lemma
5.6
that
the right hand side of (
8.3
) is contained in

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
To prove the reverse inclusion, we let
\omega\in Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Lemma
6.3
(2) for
m=1
and Corollary
5.2
then say that
there exists an element
\omega_{1}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}ip\geq-n\\
jp\geq-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}e_{i,j}
with

e_{i,j}\in Z_{1}F^{1,q}_{0}
such that
\omega-\omega_{1}\in d({\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q-1}_{K})
. By Corollary
5.2
and
Lemma
5.6
, the latter group is
of the form
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-m\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}F^{1,{q-1}}_{0})
.
We can however write this term as

\begin{array}[]{lll}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-m\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}F^{1,{q-1}}_{0})&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-m\\
p\nmid i\text{ or }p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}F^{1,{q-1}}_{0})\ +\ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-m\\
p\mid i,j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}F^{1,{q-1}}_{0})\\
&\subset&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-m\\
p\nmid i\text{ or }p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}F^{1,{q-1}}_{0})\ +\ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}ip\geq-n\\
jp\geq-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}\left(Z_{1}F^{1,q}_{0}\right).\end{array}

It follows that
\omega=\omega^{\prime}_{1}+\omega_{2}
, where
\omega^{\prime}_{1}
lies in the
first and
\omega_{2}
lies in the second sum on the right hand side of
(
8.3
). The uniqueness of this presentation is clear from
Corollary
5.2
.

We now prove the other claims. Item (1) is clear because
{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
and

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
are
R
-modules. Items (2.a) and (2.b) follow directly
from Corollary
5.2
using again that
{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is
an
R
-module. Items (2.c) and (2.d) follow
from (
8.3
) once we note
that the
R
-module structure of
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
is via the
canonical map
R\to R^{p}
(given by
a\mapsto a^{p}
).
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
R
be as above and
{\mathfrak{m}}=(Y_{1},Y_{2})
. Assume
m,n\geq 0
. Then the map

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
induces a
monomorphism

(1-C)^{*}\colon H^{2}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}(R,Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})\hookrightarrow H^{2}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}(R,{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}),

where
H^{*}_{\mathfrak{m}}(R,-)
is the Zariski
(equivalently, étale) cohomology with support at the closed point of

{\rm Spec\,}(R)
.

Proof.

For any
R
-module
M
and
non-zero element
a\in R
, we write
M[a^{-1}]
as
M_{a}
. We let
\pi=Y_{1}Y_{2}
.
By
[
Brodman-Sharp
, Thm. 5.1.20]
, there is then an exact sequence

(8.4)

M_{Y_{1}}\oplus M_{Y_{2}}\xrightarrow{\rho}M_{\pi}\xrightarrow{\phi}H^{2}_{\mathfrak{m}}(M)\to 0,

where
\rho((a,b))=a-b
.
Now consider the following commutative diagram.

(8.5)

\textstyle{(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{Y_{1}}\oplus(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f\oplus g}
\textstyle{(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{\pi}=Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{({\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{Y_{1}}\oplus({\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{2}}
\textstyle{({\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{\pi}=\Omega^{q}_{K},}

where each
\theta_{i}
is the difference map
\rho
of (
8.4
),
and
f,g,h
are the maps induced by
1-C
. To show
(1-C)^{*}
is injective, we
need to show for any
\alpha\in(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{\pi}
that

h(\alpha)\in{\rm Image}(\theta_{2})
implies

\alpha\in{\rm Image}(\theta_{1})
.

We now let
\alpha\in Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{K}

such that
h(\alpha)\in{\rm Image}(\theta_{2})
. Since
Z_{1}\Omega^{q}_{K}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0,m\geq 0}Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
, there exists
N,M\gg 0
such that
\alpha\in Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(N,M)}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
By Lemma
8.3
(see (
8.3
)), we can write

(8.6)

\alpha=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}ip\geq-N\\
jp\geq-M\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}c_{i,j}\ +\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-N\\
j\geq-M\\
p\nmid i\ or\ p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}d_{i,j});

where
c_{i,j}\in Z_{1}F^{1,q}_{0}
and
d_{i,j}\in F^{1,{q-1}}_{0}
.

We may assume that
N\geq n
and
M\geq m
. Then we can write
\alpha=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}
, where

\alpha_{1}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}ip\geq-n\\
jp\geq-M\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}c_{i,j}\ +\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-n\\
j\geq-M\\
p\nmid i\ or\ p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}d_{i,j}),\alpha_{2}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}ip\geq-N\\
jp\geq-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}c_{i,j}\ +\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq-N\\
j\geq-m\\
p\nmid i\ or\ p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}d_{i,j}),

(8.7)

\begin{array}[]{lll}\alpha_{3}&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}-N\leq ip\leq-n\\
-M\leq jp\leq-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}c_{i,j}\ +\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}-N\leq i\leq-n\\
-N\leq j\leq-m\\
p\nmid i\ or\ p\nmid j\end{subarray}}d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}d_{i,j})\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}-N\leq ip\leq-n\\
-M\leq jp\leq-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{ip}Y_{2}^{jp}c_{i,j}\ +\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}-N\leq i\leq-n\\
-N\leq j\leq-m\\
p\nmid i\ or\ p\nmid j\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}d^{\prime}_{i,j},\end{array}

where we set
d^{\prime}_{i,j}:=i\cdot{\rm dlog}Y_{1}\cdot d_{i,j}+j\cdot{\rm dlog}Y_{2}\cdot d_{i,j}+d(d_{i,j})
so that
d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}d_{i,j})=Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}d^{\prime}_{i,j}
.

By Lemma
8.3
(2.c),(2.d), we conclude that
\alpha_{1}

(resp.
\alpha_{2}
) lies in

(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{Y_{2}}
(resp.
(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{(n,m)}\Omega^{q}_{K})_{Y_{1}}
). It follows that
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\in{\rm Image}(\theta_{1})
.

We claim that
\alpha_{3}=0
. This will finish the proof.

Since
h(\alpha)\in{\rm Image}(\theta_{2})
, it follows from the commutativity of (
8.5
) that
h(\alpha_{3})=h(\alpha)-h(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2})\in{\rm Image}(\theta_{2})
. We shall show that this implies
\alpha_{3}=0
.
Indeed, suppose that
-N_{0}
is the least exponent of
Y_{1}
appearing in
\alpha_{3}
in the last equality of (
8.7
). Then we may write
\alpha_{3}
as

\alpha_{3}=\sum\limits_{-M<j<-m}Y_{1}^{-N_{0}}Y_{2}^{j}e_{-N_{0},j}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}-N_{0}<i<-n\\
