-M<j<-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}e_{i,j},

where
Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}e_{i,j}
is either of the form
d(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}d_{i,j})

(if
p\nmid i
or
p\nmid j
), or it is of the form
Y_{1}^{pi}Y_{2}^{pi}c_{i,j}

(in case
p
divides both
i,j
), where
c_{i,j}\in Z_{1}F^{1,q-1}_{0}
. Thus we have

C(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}e_{i,j})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}0,&\text{if }p\nmid i\text{ or }p\nmid j\\
Y_{1}^{i/p}Y_{2}^{j/p}C(e_{i,j}),&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

That is,
C(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}e_{i,j})
is either zero or the exponent of
Y_{1}
in

C(Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}e_{i,j})
is strictly greater than
i
(because
i<0
).
This implies that
(1-C)(c)
can be written as

(1-C)(\alpha_{3})=\sum\limits_{-M<j<-m}Y_{1}^{-N_{0}}Y_{2}^{j}e_{-N_{0},j}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i>-N_{0}\\
-M<j<-m\end{subarray}}Y_{1}^{i}Y_{2}^{j}e^{\prime}_{i,j},

where
e_{i,j},e^{\prime}_{i,j}\in F^{1,q}_{0}
.

Since
(1-C)(\alpha_{3})\in{\rm Image}(\theta_{2})
, it implies that every term of the above sum has the property that either the exponent of
Y_{1}
is at least
-n
or the exponent of
Y_{2}
is at least
-m
. By the uniqueness statement in Lemma
8.3
, we therefore conclude that
Y_{1}^{-N_{0}}Y_{2}^{j}e_{-N_{0},j}=0
for all
j<-m
. This shows that
-N_{0}
is not the least exponent of
Y_{1}
appearing in
\alpha_{3}
, which leads to a contradiction. This proves the claim and concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

8.3.
The main result

We return to our original set-up of §
8
.
We need the following results to prove the main theorem.
For any
x\in X
, we let
R_{x}={\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}^{h},\ X_{x}={\rm Spec\,}(R_{x})
. We let
E_{x}
denote
the pull-back of
E
to
X_{x}
and
U_{x}=X_{x}\setminus E_{x}
.
We let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
denote the maximal ideal of
R_{x}
, and let

\widehat{R}_{x}
be the
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
-adic completion of
R_{x}
. We let
\widehat{X}_{x}={\rm Spec\,}(\widehat{R}_{x})
.
We let
\widehat{E}_{x}
denote the pull-back of
E
to
\widehat{X}_{x}
and
\widehat{U}_{x}=\widehat{X}_{x}\setminus\widehat{E}_{x}
. We write
{\mathcal{F}}_{m}=W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}
as
abbreviation.

Lemma 8.5
.

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})=0
.

Proof.

We first claim that

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})=0
for every
x\in X^{(1)}\bigcap E
.
To prove the claim, we use the exact sequence

\cdots\to{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\xrightarrow{f}{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\xrightarrow{g}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m}).

By Lemma
7.2
(2), we see that
f
is an isomorphism.
Also
g
is injective by Proposition
6.2
because

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})={\rm Coker}(1-C\colon Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D_{x}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K_{x}}\to{\rm fil}_{D_{x}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K_{x}})
by Lemma
7.2
(2). Hence, we get

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})=0
. This proves the claim.

We now recall that for any bounded complex of abelian sheaves
{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}
on

X_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}
,
one has the coniveau spectral sequence

(8.8)

E_{1}^{p,q}({\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet})=\bigoplus\limits_{x\in X^{(p)}\cap E}{\mathbb{H}}^{p+q}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet})\implies{\mathbb{H}}^{p+q}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}).

The above claim shows that
E^{1,0}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})=0
. Since

E^{0,1}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})
is clearly zero, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{m}
be as in Lemma
8.5
. Then the restriction map

{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\to\bigoplus\limits_{x\in X^{(1)}\cap E}{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})
is
injective.

Proof.

We begin by claiming that
{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})=0
for
x\in X^{(2)}\bigcap E
.
To prove this claim, we first reduce to proving it when
m=1
(and
D\geq 0

arbitrary) using Theorem
6.19
(9).
We next look at the hypercohomology long exact
sequence

(8.9)

H^{1}_{x}(X_{x},{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{1})\to H^{2}_{x}(X_{x},Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}})\xrightarrow{(1-C)^{*}}H^{2}_{x}(X_{x},{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}}).

Since
x\in X^{(2)}
and
{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}}
is a free
R_{x}
-module, one knows that

H^{1}_{x}(X_{x},{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}})=0
(cf.
[
Bruns-Herzog
, Prop. 3.5.4.b]
).
To prove the claim, it suffices therefore to show that
(1-C)^{*}
in
(
8.9
) is injective.

To prove this injectivity, we first deduce from Lemma
5.9

and a combination of Corollary
5.10
and Lemma
6.3

that
{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{U}_{x}}
(resp.
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{U}_{x}}
) is the
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
-adic
completion of the finitely generated (cf. Lemma
3.6
(2))
R_{x}
-module

{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}}
(resp.
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}}
).
We now apply
[
Bruns-Herzog
, Prop 3.5.4.(d)]
to conclude that
the vertical arrows in the commutative diagram

(8.10)

\textstyle{H^{2}_{x}(X_{x},Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ (1-C)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{x}(X_{x},{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{x}(\widehat{X}_{x},Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{U}_{x}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ (1-C)^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{x}(\widehat{X}_{x},{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{\widehat{U}_{x}})}

are bijective. We are therefore reduced to showing that the bottom
horizontal arrow in this diagram is injective. But this follows from
Lemma
8.4
because we can write
\widehat{R}_{x}=k(x)[[Y_{1},Y_{2}]]

and
D=V((Y^{n}_{1}Y^{m}_{2}))
(where
m,n\geq 0
). This proves the claim.

The claim implies that
E^{2,0}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})=0
in (
8.8
).
Since
E^{0,2}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})
is clearly
0
, we get

{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})=E^{1,1}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})
. One also checks that

E^{1,1}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})=E^{1,1}_{3}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})\hookrightarrow E^{1,1}_{2}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})\hookrightarrow E^{1,1}_{1}({\mathcal{F}}_{m})=\bigoplus\limits_{x\in X^{(1)}\cap E}{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

We now prove the main theorem of §
8
. This will prove
Theorem
1.3
.

Theorem 8.7
.

For
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, there is a canonical isomorphism

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U).

Proof.

We shall use the exact sequence

(8.11)

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\to H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m}),

in which
{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})
has been identified with
H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}})

using Theorem
6.19
(13).
The first term in this exact sequence is zero by Lemma
8.5
.
We now let
x\in E^{(0)}
and consider the commutative diagram of exact sequences

(8.12)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1)}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U_{x},{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(2)}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})}

where the vertical arrows are the restriction maps.

The arrow (2) in the bottom row is surjective because the next term

{\mathbb{H}}^{2}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})
is zero by Lemma
7.2
. On the other hand,
the arrow (1) in the bottom row of
(
8.12
) is identified with the inclusion

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\cong{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n_{x}}H^{q+1}(K_{x})[p^{m}]\hookrightarrow H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K_{x})
by Theorem
7.15
if we let
n_{x}
denote the multiplicity of

D
at
x
. It follows that the composition of the maps

H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X_{x},{\mathcal{F}}_{m})
coincides
with the map
H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)\to\frac{H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K_{x})}{{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n_{x}}H^{q+1}(K_{x})[p^{m}]}
, induced by the pull-back via the canonical map

X_{x}\to X
.

Hence, we get

\begin{array}[]{cl}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})&\cong{\rm Ker}\left(H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{E}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\right)\\
&\cong{\rm Ker}\left(H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(U,W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U,{\operatorname{log}}})\to\bigoplus\limits_{x\in X^{(1)}\cap E}{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}}_{m})\right)\\
&={\rm Ker}\left(H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)\to\bigoplus\limits_{x\in X^{(1)}\cap E}\frac{H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(K_{x})}{{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n_{x}}H^{q+1}(K_{x})[p^{m}]}\right)\\
&={\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U),\end{array}

where the second isomorphism follows from Lemma
8.6
.
This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

An immediate, yet important, consequence of Theorem
8.7
is the following
functoriality property of the Kato filtration.
This property is not obvious from Definition
8.1
.

Corollary 8.8
.

Let
f\colon(X^{\prime},E^{\prime})\to(X,E)
be a morphism between two snc-pairs (cf.
§
2.1
) and let

D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
be an effective divisor. Then the pull-back

f^{*}\colon H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(X\setminus E)\to H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(X^{\prime}\setminus E^{\prime})
restricts to a
map
f^{*}\colon{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(X\setminus E)\to{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{D^{\prime}}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(X^{\prime}\setminus E^{\prime})
for every
q\geq 0,m\geq 1

whenever
D^{\prime}\geq f^{*}(D)
in
{\rm Div}_{E^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
.

Proof.

It is clear from Theorem
1.1
and the definitions of

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
and the Cartier operator that
there is a morphism of complexes
f^{*}\colon W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}\to f_{*}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{{X^{\prime}}|{D^{\prime}}})
, and this induces the map

f^{*}\colon{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X^{\prime},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{{X^{\prime}}|{D^{\prime}}})
. We now apply Theorem
8.7
to
conclude the proof.
∎

Using Theorem
8.7
in Example
8.2
(2), we get the
following cohomological description of the Brauer group with modulus.

Corollary 8.9
.

For
m\geq 1
, there is a canonical exact sequence

(8.13)

0\to{\rm Pic}(U)/p^{m}\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{1,\bullet}_{X|D})\to{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{D}{\operatorname{Br}}(U)[p^{m}]\to 0.

If
n\geq 1
is any integer and we write
n=p^{m}r
where
m\geq 1
and

p\nmid r
. We let

{{\mathbb{Z}}}/n(q)_{X|D}:={{\mathbb{Z}}}/r(q)\bigoplus W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q-1,\bullet}_{X|D}[q-1]

for any
q\geq 1
. We then get

Corollary 8.10
.

For any
n,q\geq 1
, there is a canonical isomorphism

{\mathbb{H}}^{q}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/n(q-1)_{X|D})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q}_{n}(U).

9.
Refined Swan conductor on schemes

In this section, we shall give our first application of Theorem
8.7
.
The context of this application is the following. If
A
is an
F
-finite
henselian discrete
valuation ring with maximal ideal
(\pi)
, quotient field
K
and residue field
{\mathfrak{f}}

of characteristic
p>0
, then Kato constructed a refined Swan
conductor map

(9.1)

{{\rm rsw}}^{q+1}_{K,n}\colon{\rm gr}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)\to\pi^{-n}\Omega^{q+1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\otimes_{A}{\mathfrak{f}}

for
n\geq 1,q\geq 0
and showed that it is injective. This is one of the
main results of
[
Kato-89
]
. If
X
is, more generally,
a regular finite type
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme with
simple normal crossing divisor
E
and if
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
is effective, then
Kato-Leal-Saito
[
KLS
, § 4]
showed that Kato’s Swan conductor at the
generic points of
E
glue together to give rise to a refined Swan conductor map

{{\rm Rsw}}\colon\frac{{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{D}H^{1}(X)}{{\rm fil}^{{\operatorname{log}}}_{D/p}H^{1}(X)}\to H^{0}_{\rm zar}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)}{{\mathcal{O}}_{X(D/p)}})
which is
injective.

We shall construct a general version of this map using Theorem
8.7
.
The generalized refined Swan conductor map that we construct has many applications.
For instance, Kato’s integrality theorem
(cf.
[
Kato-89
, Thm. 7.1]
) follows immediately from this construction.
The generalized refined Swan conductor map also plays the central role
in the proofs of the main results of
[
KM-2
]
. We shall use the following definition
from
[
Kato-89
]
.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
X
be a Noetherian regular
F
-finite
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme and
U\subset X
a
dense open subscheme. We let
E\subset X
be an irreducible Weil divisor
disjoint from
U
. Given
q\geq 0
and
\chi\in H^{q+1}(U)\{p\}
, we let
{\rm sw}_{E}(\chi)

denote Kato’s Swan conductor (cf.
[
Kato-89
, Def. 2.3]
)
of the image of
\chi
in
H^{q+1}(K_{E})
,
where
K_{E}
is the henselization of the total quotient ring of
X
at
E
.

9.1.
The generalized refined Swan conductor

Let
k={\mathbb{F}}_{p}
. Let
X
be a connected Noetherian regular
F
-finite
k
-scheme and let

E
be a simple normal crossing divisor on
X
with irreducible components

E_{1},\ldots,E_{r}
. Let
D\geq D^{\prime}\geq D/p\geq 0
be two divisors in
{\rm Div}_{E}(X)

(note that
|D/p|\subset|D|\subseteq E
).
We let
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the complement of
E
.
For any pair of integers
q\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, we then have a short exact
sequence of complexes of étale sheaves

(9.2)

0\to W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D^{\prime}}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}\to\frac{W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}}{W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D^{\prime}}}\to 0

on
X
, where the first map is the canonical inclusion.
Letting
{\mathcal{F}}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}:=\frac{W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D}}{W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D^{\prime}}}
, we get an injection

(9.3)

\alpha_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}\colon\frac{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D})}{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D^{\prime}})}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{F}}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}).

The complex
{\mathcal{F}}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}
has the form

(9.4)

{\mathcal{F}}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}=\left(\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\xrightarrow{1-\overline{C}}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\right).

From our assumption and the definition of
C
(cf. (
6.12
)),
we see that
\overline{C}=0
and we can write

(9.5)

{\mathcal{F}}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}=\left(\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{\rm can}}}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\right),

where ‘
{\operatorname{\rm can}}
’ is the canonical map induced by the inclusion

Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\hookrightarrow{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.
From the definition of
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
, we see that ‘
{\operatorname{\rm can}}
’ is injective.
In particular,

(9.6)

{\mathcal{F}}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}+Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\right)[-1].

We therefore get an injection

(9.7)

\alpha^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}\colon\frac{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D})}{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D^{\prime}})}\hookrightarrow H^{0}_{\rm zar}\left(X,\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}+Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\right).

By Theorem
6.19
(3),
F^{m-1}d
induces a map

(9.8)

F^{m-1}d\colon\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}+Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\to\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}.

Lemma 9.2
.

The above map is injective.

Proof.

We assume that
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
, and let
\alpha
be a global section of

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
such that
\alpha^{\prime}:=F^{m-1}d(\alpha)\in{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}
. By Theorem
6.19
(8), we get that

\alpha^{\prime}\in{\rm Ker}(V^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{U})\bigcap{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}
. Equivalently,

\alpha^{\prime}\in{\rm Ker}(V^{m-1}\colon{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}\to{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{U})
.
By Theorem
6.19
(8) again, we get that

F^{m-1}d(\alpha)=F^{m-1}d(\beta)
for some
\beta\in{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\subseteq{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
. But this implies that

\alpha=\beta+\gamma
for some
\gamma\in Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.
Since the assertion of the lemma is local, this finishes the proof.
∎

Letting
\widetilde{\alpha}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}=(-1)^{q+1}(F^{m-1}d)\circ\alpha^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}
,
we get the following.

Corollary 9.3
.

There exists a canonical injection

\widetilde{\alpha}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}\colon\frac{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D})}{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|D^{\prime}})}\hookrightarrow H^{0}_{\rm zar}\left(X,\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}\right).

Since the operator
V
commutes with each of
F,C
and
d
, it follows that

V\circ{\alpha}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}={\alpha}^{m+1,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}\circ V
and

\widetilde{\alpha}^{m,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}=\widetilde{\alpha}^{m+1,q}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}\circ V
.
Letting
F=D-D^{\prime}
and using Lemma
3.6
and Theorem
8.7
, the
above corollary implies the following.

The main result of this section is the following.

Theorem 9.4
.

(1)
There exists a (strict) map of ind-abelian groups

(9.9)

Remark 9.5
.

As mentioned earlier, if
X
is of finite type over
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
and
q=0
, part (2) of
Theorem
9.4
was shown by Kato-Leal-Saito in
[
KLS
, Thms. 4.2.2, 4.3.1]
.

If
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
and
E
is irreducible, then we shall write
{\rm Rsw}^{m,q+1}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}
as
{\rm Rsw}^{m,q+1}_{A|(n,n^{\prime})}
if
D=nE
and
D^{\prime}=n^{\prime}E
.

Suppose now that
D
and
D^{\prime}
are as in Theorem
9.4
, and let
\chi\in{\rm fil}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)
. Let
1\leq i\leq r
and let
n_{i}
(resp.
n_{i}^{\prime}
) be the multiplicity of
D
(resp.
D^{\prime}
). Then we have the following straightforward corollary
of Theorem
9.4
(3) (cf.
[
Matsuda
, Rem. 5.2.1]
).

Corollary 9.6
.

The element
{\rm Rsw}^{m,q+1}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}(\chi)\in H^{0}_{\rm zar}\left(F,\Omega^{q+1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(D)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F}\right)

has the property that its stalk at
\eta_{i}
is
{\rm Rsw}^{m,q+1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X,\eta_{i}}|(n,n^{\prime})}(\chi)
, where
\eta_{i}
is the generic point of
E_{i}
.

9.2.
A special case

We now consider a special case of Theorem
9.4
. We fix an integer

i\in\{1,\ldots,r\}
, let
D=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}}n_{j}E_{j}

with
n_{i}\geq 1
and let
D^{\prime}=D-E_{i}
.
For
j\neq i
, we let
\overline{E}_{j}
denote the
scheme-theoretic intersection
E_{i}\bigcap E_{j}
. We let
E_{o}=E-E_{i}
and let

F_{i}
denote the scheme-theoretic intersection
E_{o}\cap E_{i}
. We let

D_{i}=\iota^{*}_{i}(D)
and
{D}^{\prime}_{i}=\iota^{*}_{i}(D^{\prime})
, where

\iota_{i}\colon E_{i}\hookrightarrow X
is the inclusion.
Using Lemma
3.6
, Theorem
9.4
and Corollary
8.8
, we
get the following.

One can present
\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}
in a simplified
form in the special case of this subsection which is helpful in computing the
refined Swan conductor.
We need the next lemma for this purpose.
We let
(X,E)
be the snc-pair as above and let
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
. Let

u\colon X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of a regular connected divisor. We assume that

E^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\times_{X}E
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
X^{\prime}
.

Lemma 9.8
.

The following sequences are exact for every
q\geq 0
.

(1)

0\to\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\to\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}(E+X^{\prime}))\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{res}}}u_{*}\Omega^{q-1}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})\to 0
.

(2)

0\to\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}(E+X^{\prime}))(-X^{\prime})\to\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\to u_{*}\Omega^{q}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})\to 0
.

Proof.

We assume
q\geq 1
as the lemma is otherwise obvious.
By Proposition
2.2
, we first note that
\Omega^{1}_{X}
(resp.
\Omega^{1}_{X^{\prime}}
)
is a locally free sheaf on
X
(resp.
X^{\prime}
) and that
{\rm rank}(\Omega^{1}_{X})={\rm rank}(\Omega^{1}_{X^{\prime}})+1<\infty
.
It is now easy to define the residue map

\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}(E+X^{\prime}))\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{res}}}u_{*}\Omega^{q-1}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})
locally,
and check that this glues to a global definition (cf.
[
SP
, Tag 0FMU]
).
The exactness of (1) is an easy consequence of
[
Kato-89
, Lem. 7.2]
.
To prove (2), we use the fundamental exact sequence

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-X^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\overline{d}}u^{*}\Omega^{1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\xrightarrow{u^{*}}\Omega^{1}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})\to 0

of log differentials (cf.
[
Ogus
, Prop. IV.2.3.2]
).
All terms of this sequence are locally free sheaves
on
X^{\prime}
such that the rank of the middle term is the sum of the ranks of the other
two terms. This implies that
\overline{d}
must be injective.
Taking the higher exterior powers, we thus get a short exact sequence

(9.13)

0\to\Omega^{q-1}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})(-X^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\overline{d}}u^{*}\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\to\Omega^{q}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})\to 0.

We now look at the commutative diagram

(9.14)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(-X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(-X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}(E+X^{\prime}))(-X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{res}}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{q}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{u_{*}\Omega^{q}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{u_{*}\Omega^{q}_{X^{\prime}}({\operatorname{log}}E^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}

The left column is exact by (1) and the bottom row is exact by
(
9.13
). It follows that the middle row is exact which proves
(2).
∎

The following is a further simplification of the target of the refined
Swan conductor in the special case of §
9.2
.

Lemma 9.9
.

Under the set-up of Corollary
9.7
, there exists an exact sequence

0\to\Omega^{q+1}_{E_{i}}({\operatorname{log}}F_{i})({D}_{i})\xrightarrow{\nu^{\prime}_{i}}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}\xrightarrow{\nu_{i}}\Omega^{q}_{E_{i}}({\operatorname{log}}F_{i})({D}_{i})\to 0.

Proof.

We look at the commutative diagram

(9.15)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{q+1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(D^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\Omega^{q+1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{res}}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{q}_{E_{i}}({\operatorname{log}}F_{i})({D}_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}

where the middle row is the residue exact sequence and
\alpha
is the canonical
inclusion whose image lies in
\Omega^{q+1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E_{o})(D)
, as one easily checks.
We let

denote the resulting factorization. The left column is exact
by Lemma

(2). It follows that the bottom row is also exact, and
this finishes the proof as
{\rm Coker}(\alpha)=\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}

(3).
∎

The following variant of Lemma
9.9
will be useful
in
[
KM-2
]
, and its proof is immediate if we replace

D^{\prime}
by
D-2E_{i}
in (
9.15
).

Corollary 9.10
.

Assume that
n_{i}\geq 2
and let
D^{\prime\prime}=D-2E_{i}
. Then
we have an exact sequence

0\to\frac{\Omega^{q+1}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E_{o})(D)}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}\xrightarrow{\psi^{\prime}_{i}}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}\xrightarrow{\psi_{i}}\Omega^{q}_{E_{i}}({\operatorname{log}}F_{i})(D_{i})\to 0,

where
\psi_{i}
is the composite map
\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}\twoheadrightarrow\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}{{\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}}\xrightarrow{\nu_{i}}\Omega^{q}_{E_{i}}({\operatorname{log}}F_{i})(D_{i})
.

Remark 9.11
.

Using Corollary
9.10
, it is straight-forward to
define a non-log version of the filtration
{\rm fil}^{\operatorname{log}}_{D}H^{q+1}_{p^{m}}(U)

(cf.
[
KM-2
]
) which generalizes Matsuda’s filtration for
q=0

(cf.
[
Matsuda
]
) and derive its functoriality.
One would like to construct an analogue of the generalized refined Swan conductor
of Corollary
9.7
for the non-log version. To our
knowledge, this problem is yet unsolved and we shall pursue it elsewhere.

9.3.
Agreement with Kato’s Swan conductor

Suppose now in Corollary
9.7
that
X={\rm Spec\,}(A)
, where
A
is a henselian
discrete valuation ring with maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\pi)
, the quotient field
K
and the
residue field
{\mathfrak{f}}
. We write
D=D_{n}=V((\pi^{n}))
so that
{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}={\rm fil}_{n}\Omega^{q+1}_{K}
. Corollary
9.7
then yields the map

{{\rm Rsw}}^{q+1}_{K,n}:={{\rm Rsw}}^{q+1}_{X|(D,D^{\prime})}\colon{\rm gr}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)\hookrightarrow\pi^{-n}\Omega^{q+1}_{A}({\operatorname{log}}\pi)\otimes_{A}{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\Omega^{q+1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\oplus\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
.
Recall Kato’s refined Swan conductor
{{\rm rsw}}^{q}_{K,n}
from (
9.1
).
We refer to §
7
for various notations that we shall use in this
subsection.

Lemma 9.12
.

We have
{{\rm Rsw}}^{q+1}_{K,n}={{\rm rsw}}^{q+1}_{K,n}
.

Proof.

We let
\chi\in{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{q+1}(K)
. We then have

\chi\in{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{q,\bullet}_{X|{D_{n}}})
for some
m\geq 1
.
By definition (cf.
[
Kato-89
, § 3.9]
),

{\rm rsw}^{q+1}_{K,n}(\chi)=(\alpha,\beta)
so that

\lambda^{q+2}_{\pi}(T\alpha,T\beta)=\{\chi,1+\pi^{n}T\}\in H^{q+2}(K)
, where

\lambda^{q+2}_{\pi}\colon\Omega^{q+1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}[T]}\oplus\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}[T]}\to H^{q+2}((A[T])^{h}[\pi^{-1}])

is as in
[
Kato-89
, § 1.9, p. 107]
.
Using the surjectivity of the composite map

K^{M}_{q}(K)\otimes{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}(K)\to{\rm fil}_{n}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{K}\xrightarrow{\delta^{m,q}_{n}}T^{m,q}_{n}(K)
(cf. Corollary
7.3
, Theorem
7.15
), it suffices
to prove
{{\rm Rsw}}^{m,q+1}_{K,n}(\chi)={{\rm rsw}}^{m,q+1}_{K,n}(\chi)
in the
following two cases.

Case 1:

\chi=\delta^{m,q}_{n}\left(V^{i}([x]_{m-i}){\rm dlog}([u_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([u_{q}]_{m})\right)
(cf. (
7.6
)), where

x\in K^{\times}
such that
x^{p^{m-i-1}}\in{\rm fil}_{n}K
for some
i
and

u_{j}\in A^{\times}
for each
j
.

In this case, we let
\chi^{\prime}=\delta^{m,0}_{n}(V^{i}([x]_{m-i}))
and
\alpha={\rm dlog}([u_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([u_{q}]_{m})

so that
\chi^{\prime}\in{\rm fil}^{{\rm bk}}_{n}H^{1}_{m}(K)
and
\chi=\chi^{\prime}\cup\alpha
by the
commutative diagram (
7.4
). In particular,

(9.16)

\{\chi,1+\pi^{n}T\}=\{\chi^{\prime}\cup\alpha,1+\pi^{n}T\}=(-1)^{q}\{\chi^{\prime},1+\pi^{n}T\}\cup\alpha.

Meanwhile, one knows from Kato’s definition of his refined Swan conductor
(cf.
[
Matsuda
, Rem. 3.2.12]
,
[
KLS
, (3.2.4)]
) that

(9.17)

-(F^{m-1}d(V^{i}([x]_{m-i})))={\rm rsw}^{1}_{K,n}(\chi^{\prime})=(\alpha_{1},\beta_{1})\in\Omega^{1}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\oplus{\mathfrak{f}}.

In particular,
\lambda^{2}_{\pi}(T\alpha_{1},T\beta_{1})=\{\chi^{\prime},1+\pi^{n}T\}\in H^{2}((A[T])^{h}[\pi^{-1}])
. We thus get

\{\chi,1+\pi^{n}T\}=(-1)^{q}\lambda^{2}_{\pi}(T\alpha_{1},T\beta_{1})\cup\alpha=\lambda^{q+2}_{\pi}\left((-1)^{q}T\alpha_{1}\wedge\overline{\alpha},T\beta_{1}\wedge\overline{\alpha}\right),

where
\overline{\alpha}
is the image of
\alpha
under the quotient map

\Omega^{q}_{A}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega^{q}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
.
By definition of
{{\rm Rsw}}^{m,q+1}_{K,n}(\chi)
, it remains therefore to show that

(-1)^{q+1}F^{m-1}d(V^{i}([x]_{m-i})\alpha)=(-1)^{q}\left(\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1}{\rm dlog}(\pi)\right)\wedge\overline{\alpha}=(-1)^{q}\alpha_{1}\wedge\overline{\alpha}+(\beta_{1}\overline{\alpha})\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)
.

To that end, we note that

\begin{array}[]{lll}(-1)^{q+1}F^{m-1}d(V^{i}([x]_{m-i})\alpha)&=&(-1)^{q+1}F^{m-1}(dV^{i}([x]_{m-i})\wedge\alpha)\\
&=&(-1)^{q}\left(-F^{m-1}dV^{i}([x]_{m-i})\right)\wedge\alpha\\
&{=}^{1}&(-1)^{q}(\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1}{\rm dlog}(\pi))\wedge\overline{\alpha}\\
&=&((-1)^{q}\alpha_{1}\wedge\overline{\alpha})+(\beta_{1}\overline{\alpha}\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)),\\
\end{array}

where
{=}^{1}
holds by (
9.17
). This proves the desired
identity.

Case 2:

\chi=\delta^{m,q}_{n}\left(V^{i}([x]_{m-i}){\rm dlog}([u_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([u_{q-1}]_{m})\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)\right)
, where

x\in K^{\times}
such that
x^{p^{m-i-1}}\in{\rm fil}_{n}K
for some
i
and

u_{j}\in A^{\times}
for each
j
.

In this case, we let
\alpha={\rm dlog}([u_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([u_{q-1}]_{m})\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)
and

\alpha^{\prime}={\rm dlog}([u_{1}]_{m})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm dlog}([u_{q-1}]_{m})
.
Repeating the argument of the previous case, we then get

\begin{array}[]{lll}\{\chi,1+\pi^{n}T\}&=&(-1)^{q}\{\chi^{\prime},1+\pi^{n}T\}\cup\alpha\\
&=&(-1)^{q}\lambda^{2}_{\pi}(T\alpha_{1},T\beta_{1})\cup\alpha\\
&=&(-1)^{q}\lambda^{2}_{\pi}(T\alpha_{1},0)\wedge\alpha^{\prime}\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)+\lambda^{2}_{\pi}(0,T\beta_{1})\wedge\alpha\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)\\
&=&(-1)^{q}\lambda^{2}_{\pi}(T\alpha_{1},0)\wedge\alpha^{\prime}\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)\\
&=&(-1)^{q}\lambda^{q+2}_{\pi}\left(0,T\alpha_{1}\wedge\overline{\alpha^{\prime}}\right)\end{array}

It remains therefore to show that

(-1)^{q+1}F^{m-1}d(V^{i}([x]_{m-i})\alpha)=(-1)^{q}\alpha_{1}(\alpha^{\prime}\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi))
.
To check this, we note that

\begin{array}[]{lll}(-1)^{q+1}F^{m-1}d(V^{i}([x]_{m-i})\alpha)&=&(-1)^{q+1}F^{m-1}d(V^{i}([x]_{m-i})\wedge\alpha^{\prime}\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)\\
&{=}^{2}&(-1)^{q}(\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1}{\rm dlog}(\pi))\wedge\alpha^{\prime}\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)\\
&=&(-1)^{q}\alpha_{1}(\alpha^{\prime}\wedge{\rm dlog}(\pi)),\end{array}

{=}^{2}
holds by (
9.17
).
This shows the desired identity and concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 9.13
.

Using Lemma
9.12
, it is easy to check that Kato’s integrality and
specialization theorems (cf.
[
Kato-89
, Thms. 7.1, 9.1]
)
in positive characteristics are immediate consequences of Corollary
9.7
.

10.
Duality for Hodge-Witt cohomology with modulus

In this section, we shall prove a duality theorem for the filtered Hodge-Witt cohomology
when
k
is a perfect field. This can be interpreted as
an extension of Ekedahl’s duality theorem
[
Ekedahl
, Cor. II.2.2.23]
to the
Hodge-Witt cohomology with modulus.
We begin by setting up notations and proving some intermediate results
which are corollaries of Theorem
6.19
.

10.1.
Canonical filtration of
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}

We let
k
be an
F
-finite field of characteristic
p
and let

X
be a Noetherian regular and connected
F
-finite
k
-scheme.
Let
E
be a simple normal crossing divisor on
X

with irreducible components
E_{1},\ldots,E_{r}
. Let
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
be the
inclusion of the complement of
E
in
X
.
We fix integers
q\geq 0,\ m\geq 1
.

Let
W_{m}X
denote the scheme
(|X|,W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
, where
|X|
is the underlining topological space of
X
(we caution that this is different from the group
W_{n}(X)
defined in
§
3
).
Then the transition map
R\colon W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\twoheadrightarrow W_{m-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
and the Frobenius
homomorphism
F\colon W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to W_{m-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
define a closed immersion
\rho_{X}\colon W_{m-1}X\hookrightarrow W_{m}X

and a finite morphism
\phi\colon W_{m-1}X\to W_{m}X
, respectively, of

W_{m}X
-schemes.

The standard properties of
(R,F,V,d)
yield the following
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module
homomorphisms.

(10.1)

W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\xrightarrow{R}\rho_{*}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{X},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\xrightarrow{F}\phi_{*}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{X}

(10.2)

\phi_{*}W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{X}\xrightarrow{V}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \overline{\phi}^{m}_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\xrightarrow{d}\overline{\phi}^{m}_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q+1}_{X},

where
\overline{\phi}\colon W_{m}X\to W_{m}X
is the finite morphism induced by the
absolute Frobenius
\overline{F}\colon W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}

(recall that
\overline{F}=\psi
in our notation when
m=1
).

Since
dF=pFd
and
p\Omega^{q+1}_{X}=0
, we have the morphism of

W_{m+1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

(10.3)

F^{m-1}d\colon\phi_{*}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\to\phi^{m}_{*}\Omega^{q+1}_{X}.

In this section, we shall often use the notation
R_{m}
for the map

R\colon W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{X}\to W_{m-1}\Omega^{q}_{X}
. This will help us
keep track of the changing domains of
R
.

We let
S={\rm Spec\,}(k)
and
let
f_{m}\colon W_{m}X\to W_{m}S
denote the projection
map induced by the structure map
f\colon X\to S
. We have a
commutative diagram

(10.4)

\textstyle{W_{m-n}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{n}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{f_{m-n}}
\textstyle{W_{m}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{m}}
\textstyle{W_{m-n}S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{n}_{S}}
\textstyle{W_{m}S.}

The horizontal arrows are closed immersions. The vertical arrows are
are proper (resp. projective) if
f
is proper (resp. projective).
We let
\iota=\rho^{m-1}_{S}
and
\nu=\rho^{m-1}_{X}
.

We let
D\in{\rm Div}_{E}(X)
and
define the ‘canonical filtration’ of
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}
as follows.

Definition 10.1
.

For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we let

{\rm Fil}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm Ker}(R^{m-n}\colon{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm fil}_{{D}/{p^{m-n}}}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U})&\mbox{if $n<m$}\\
0&\mbox{if $n\geq m$.}\end{array}\right.

The surjectivity of
R^{m-n}
in the above definition follows from the goodness of
the filtered de Rham-Witt complex (cf. Theorem
6.19
) and an
elementary observation that
\lfloor{{\lfloor{n/{p^{s}}}\rfloor}/p}\rfloor=\lfloor{n/{p^{s+1}}}\rfloor
for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
s\geq 0
.
We have a decreasing filtration

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}={\rm Fil}^{0}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\supset{\rm Fil}^{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\supset\cdots\supset{\rm Fil}^{m-1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}\supset{\rm Fil}^{m}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}=0.

We let

(10.5)

{\rm gr}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}=\frac{{\rm Fil}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{{\rm Fil}^{n+1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}}.

It is easy to check that for
0\leq n\leq m-1
,
there is a canonical isomorphism
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

(10.6)

\begin{array}[]{lll}{\rm gr}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}\Omega^{q}_{U}&\xrightarrow{\cong}&\rho^{m-n-1}_{*}{\rm Fil}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D/{p^{m-n-1}}}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}\\
&=&{\rm Ker}(\rho^{m-n-1}_{*}{\rm fil}_{D/{p^{m-n-1}}}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{R}\rho^{m-n}_{*}{\rm fil}_{D/{p^{m-n}}}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U}).\end{array}

Lemma 10.2
.

(
cf.
[
Illusie
, Cor. I.3.9]
)

Let
n\geq 0
be any integer. Then we have the following.

(1)

There is a canonical exact sequence of
W_{n+1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

0\to\phi^{n}_{*}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{B_{n}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\xrightarrow{V^{n}}{\rm Fil}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{\beta_{n}}\phi^{n}_{*}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}}{Z_{n}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}}\to 0.

(2)

There is a canonical exact sequence of
W_{n+2}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

0\to\phi^{n+1}_{*}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}}{Z_{n+1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}}\xrightarrow{dV^{n}}\phi_{*}{\rm Fil}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{n}}\phi^{n+1}_{*}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{B_{n+1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\to 0.

Proof.

We begin by proving (1).
By items (2) and (8) of Theorem
6.19
,
we have an exact sequence

(10.7)

0\to\phi^{n}_{*}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{B_{n}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}}\xrightarrow{V^{n}}{\rm Fil}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{V^{n}({\rm fil}_{D^{\prime}}\Omega^{q}_{U})}

and
{\rm Coker}(V^{n})
is generated by the image of
dV^{n}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})
.
Combining this with the items (6) and (11) of Theorem
6.19
, we get
the exact sequence (1) in which the map
\beta_{n}
is defined by the
property that
\beta_{n}(V^{n}(a)+dV^{n}(b))
is the image of
b
under the quotient map

{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}\twoheadrightarrow\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}}{Z_{n}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}}
.

To prove (2), we use item (2) and (6) of
Theorem
6.19
to get an exact sequence

0\to\phi^{n+1}_{*}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}}{Z_{n+1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U}}\xrightarrow{dV^{n}}\phi_{*}{\rm Fil}^{n}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{dV^{n}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q-1}_{U})},

where
dV^{n}
is
W_{n+2}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-linear by Lemma
5.7
(3)
and the image of the last map is generated by
V^{n}({\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U})
.
Combining this with items (8) and (12) of Theorem
6.19
, we get
the exact sequence (2) in which
\alpha_{n}
is defined by the property that

\alpha_{n}(V^{n}(a)+dV^{n}(b))
is the image of
a
under the quotient map

{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}\twoheadrightarrow\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{B_{n+1}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}}
.
∎

By the definition of the
W_{n+1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-linear map
\overline{p}
in §
6.2
,
and items (3) and (8) of Theorem
6.19
, there is a unique

W_{n+1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-linear
map
\theta_{n}\colon\phi^{n}_{*}B_{n}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}\to\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{\overline{p}({\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U})}
such that

V=\theta_{n}\circ F^{n-1}d\colon{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{\overline{p}({\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U})}
.

Lemma 10.3
.

For
n\geq 0
, there is a canonical exact sequence of
W_{n+1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

0\to\phi^{n}_{*}B_{n}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}\xrightarrow{\theta_{n}}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{\overline{p}({\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U})}\xrightarrow{F^{n}}\phi^{n}_{*}Z_{n}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q}_{U}\to 0.

Proof.

We begin by noting that
F^{n}
is surjective by item (6) of Theorem
6.19
.
To show the exactness at the middle term, we let
x\in{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}

be any element whose
image
\overline{x}
in
\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{\overline{p}({\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U})}

lies in the kernel of
F^{n}
. By item (5) of Theorem
6.19
,
we can write
x=V(y)
, where
y\in{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U}
.
This implies that
\overline{x}=\theta_{n}(F^{n}d(y))
, and hence we are done
by item (8) of Theorem
6.19
. It remains to prove that
\theta_{n}

is injective.

To that end, we look at the commutative diagram

(10.8)

\textstyle{\phi^{n}_{*}B_{n}{\rm fil}_{D}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{n}}
\textstyle{\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{\overline{p}({\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}\phi^{n}_{*}B_{n}\Omega^{q+1}_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{n}}
\textstyle{j_{*}\frac{W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}_{U}}{\overline{p}(W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U})},}

where the vertical arrows are the canonical maps induced by the inclusion

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}\hookrightarrow j_{*}W_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}
.
The bottom horizontal row is injective by
[
Ekedahl
, Lem. 0.6]
and the left
vertical arrow is injective by Lemma
6.5
. It follows that

\theta_{n}
on the top row is injective.
∎
